# Architecture — structure, data, hooks, helpers

*Split out of `CLAUDE.md`. The gates read this file too — see `DOC_FILES` in `scripts/doc-check.cjs`.*
## Repository Structure

```
/
├── index.html            # Vite entry point (CSP meta tag, loads src/main.jsx)
├── package.json          # Dependencies and npm scripts
├── vite.config.js        # Vite configuration
├── tsconfig.json         # TypeScript (strict mode enabled)
├── eslint.config.js      # ESLint flat config (TS + react-hooks)
├── CLAUDE.md             # This file
│
├── src/
│   ├── main.jsx          # React root (ReactDOM.createRoot). Seeds `cave-lang` from the browser's preferred languages via detectUiLang BEFORE React mounts (English fallback); it also stamps `cave-lang-auto` when the auto-detection fired (drives the one-time language toast). `main.jsx` also sets `<html lang>` ONCE here, pre-mount. **It is NOT kept in sync by a React effect, and must not be** — the sync effect was REMOVED because mutating `document.documentElement` in the installed iOS PWA recomputed the fixed-position layer and floated the bottom dock mid-page (Safari was fine; it appeared the day the effect shipped). This line claimed the opposite until it was corrected, i.e. it invited exactly the regression App.tsx's own comment warns against.
│   ├── App.tsx           # Root component: global state, routing, hooks composition; unconditionally renders <CuratorApp />
│   ├── CuratorApp.tsx    # Curator top-level shell (default UI since v2.8). Renders the curator views, dock, modals, overlays. **La colonne de contenu est un `<main>`, et c'était le SEUL repère de région manquant de toute l'app.** Balayé : `BottomDock` porte un `<nav>` et il n'y avait rien d'autre nulle part — donc un lecteur d'écran n'offrait aucun saut « aller au contenu » et atteindre la page demandait de traverser la barre du haut à chaque navigation, sur chaque vue. La colonne ENTIÈRE plutôt que le seul bloc des vues : les modales qu'elle contient sont toutes `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal`, ce qui fait ignorer le reste du document tant qu'elles sont ouvertes. `<main>` n'a aucun style propre qui diffère d'un `<div>`, donc la mise en page ne bouge pas d'un pixel — ce qui compte sur une colonne dont les invariants de positionnement iOS ont été gagnés à la main. Verrouillé par `landmarks.test.ts` (exactement UN `<main>`, ouvert et fermé ; le `<nav>` du dock toujours là), au niveau de la SOURCE parce que ce qui pourrit ici est la BALISE et que monter `CuratorApp` exige le contexte complet ; commentaires blanchis d'abord, celui qui explique le choix contenant le mot `<main>`. **Un `<header>` par vue n'est délibérément PAS exigé** : vingt-neuf `TopBar` sont candidates, la valeur d'un deuxième repère est bien moindre que celle du premier, et l'imposer serait la garde trop stricte qui fait réécrire vingt-neuf vues.
│   ├── AppContext.tsx    # React context (AppCtx / useAppCtx) — single ctx consumed by every Curator view
│   ├── constants.ts      # APP_VERSION, APP_BUILD, SK, enumerations, BT/BL/BP/BA/BJ/BW templates
│   ├── types.ts          # TypeScript interfaces (Tobacco, Lot, Pipe…)
│   ├── i18n.ts           # Registry — **ENGLISH is the only STATIC import; every other language loads ON DEMAND.** `LANG` starts as `{en}` and `ensureLang(code)` adds a dictionary to it (glob loaders, one in-flight promise per code, resolves `false` — NEVER rejects — when the code is unknown or the chunk can't be fetched). **"can't be fetched" now includes "STALLS" — `settleWithin` gives every call a 6 s window and resolves `false` past it.** `.catch(() => false)` covers a REJECTED dynamic import and nothing at all for a hung one (a dead cellular socket, a service worker that never answers, a fetch cut mid-deploy): such a promise settles neither way, so `.then`/`.catch`/`.finally` all stay unrun — **and `main.jsx` gates `ReactDOM.createRoot().render()` on this promise, so the whole app sat on its pre-mount "Chargement…" shell for ever, with no error and no React.** Reported from the installed iOS PWA. The reasoning in `main.jsx` stopped exactly one case short and said so in prose ("resolves false when the chunk cannot be fetched … so the failure path needs no special handling here") — a stalled fetch IS that case, and it was the one form the contract claimed to handle and did not; same shape as two earlier defects one subsystem over. Fixed in `ensureLang` rather than at the call site because that is where the promise is created, so it covers `saveLang` too (whose spinner could likewise never stop). **BOTH return paths are raced** — guarding only the fresh load leaves the shared in-flight promise able to hang, and `bootNeverHangs.test.ts` asserts there are exactly two. The underlying load is deliberately NOT cancelled: a dictionary arriving late is still installed, since losing a chunk that did arrive would be a second bug on the first. 6 s because the costs are asymmetric — too short means one launch in English and a reload fixes it; too long means the app never opens. `isLangLoaded(code)` reports what's in memory. Also exports `translate(lang, key)` — the pure key→string lookup used by App.tsx's t(); falls back to **English** when the language is unknown or not loaded yet (was French, which only made sense while all five were guaranteed present), and is nullish-guarded so an intentional empty-string value ("") returns "" and NEVER the raw key (a `dict[k] || k` fallback rendered "tasting_upcoming_pre" on screen). Why English is the static one: something must be compiled in or a failed import leaves the app with no text at all — and that makes "offline with no German chunk cached" land on English, exactly like "German doesn't exist". `main.jsx` awaits `ensureLang(active)` before mounting (t() is synchronous, so a late dictionary would paint one English frame at a French user); `saveLang` awaits too and KEEPS the current language when the load fails, surfacing `lang_offline_err`.
│   ├── i18n/
│   │   ├── languages.ts  # LANGUAGES registry (code + label) — single source of truth for the UI languages offered in the Settings switcher. Carries the "ADD A LANGUAGE" checklist. `fr` is canonical. Fr/en/es/de/it. **`pt` (European Portuguese) — the sixth, and the first added turned the six silent per-language fallbacks into `LANG_ASSETS` + doc:check gates 13/14. Adding it needed a dictionary, an enum column, a `LANG_ASSETS` row, the three doc pages, and ONE site the gates did not cover — see the geo.ts country table below. **That lesson was generalised into doc:check gate 15**: gates 13/14 each guard ONE named map, so each new axis needed its own gate; gate 15 guards the SHAPE — any flat per-language object literal in production source must cover every registry code, with `// lang-axis-ok: <reason>` for a deliberate subset. It immediately caught a site the Portuguese addition had MISSED (`main.jsx`'s pre-mount boot strings, five languages) and the hardcoded default in the layout checker itself.**
│   │   ├── fr.ts         # French dictionary (export var FR) — one key per line
│   │   ├── en.ts         # English dictionary (export var EN) — one key per line
│   │   ├── es.ts         # Spanish dictionary (export var ES)
│   │   ├── de.ts         # German dictionary (export var DE)
│   │   ├── it.ts         # Italian dictionary (export var IT)
│   │   └── pt.ts         # European Portuguese dictionary (export var PT). pt-PT, NOT Brazilian: ecrã / ficheiro / gerir / guardar / aceder / utilizador, AO90 spelling. Machine-translated from `fr` with the catalogue glossary, then checked mechanically (placeholders, edge spaces, symbols, casing, a Brazilian-marker list) before being written out.
│   ├── theme-curator.ts  # Curator palette (C.* incl. steel/steelHi/amberHi) + fonts (F.*) + CURATOR_CHART_COLORS, CARD_ACCENTS (shared list-card accent rotation), DOCK_ACCENT, catColor() (the category palette itself lives in `constants.ts` — `CURATOR_CAT_COLORS` was deleted). The type scale — `T` (named font-size tokens, uplift: body 15, cardTitle 20, sectionHead 24, …), `fs(px)`→`calc(px * var(--cave-font-scale,1))` and `fsInput(px)`→`max(16px, calc(...))` (iOS zoom floor). EVERY font size in the app flows through `fs()`/`fsInput()` so the S/M/L "Taille du texte" setting (`cave-font-scale`) rescales the whole UI live via the one CSS variable. Page titles keep baseline 44 but still scale.
│   ├── utils.ts          # Pure helpers (parseAgingMax, lotAgingStatus, daysSince, totalWeight, migrateData…). `monotonicId()` — strictly-increasing time-based id for entity SUB-records not keyed by a data counter (lots, pipe-maintenance entries): returns Date.now(), bumped to `last+1` when the clock hasn't advanced, so two created in the same millisecond can't collide (which would make update/remove hit both). Used by useTobaccoStore lot creation + usePipeStore.addMaintenance; `_resetMonotonicIdForTests()` clears the module counter. `newUid()` — a stable, globally-unique entity id (crypto.randomUUID, base36-random fallback) minted ONCE at creation and carried in backups: the cross-device MERGE identity (Tier 2, see useImportConfirm), distinct from the per-device numeric `id`. Minted by every store's add* on the 4 top-level entities + sessions; migrateData BACKFILLS it on the 4 top-level entities but NEVER on sessions. **Extended to the SUB-records — every LOT (useTobaccoStore lot creation, each bulk clone its own; makeLotDuplicate strips it for a fresh mint) and every MAINTENANCE entry (usePipeStore.addMaintenance) now carry a `uid`, and migrateData backfills both (per-tobacco lots, per-pipe maintenance, gated on `!uid` → idempotent). Lots use uid-FIRST merge (see useImportConfirm) so two genuinely-different tins with identical acquisition data can't collapse; App.tsx's load-path uid-persist detects uid-less lots/maintenance too.** `nextBoxNumber(tobaccos)` — the next lot box number = largest STRICTLY-numeric box across all LIVE lots + 1, or `""` when the user doesn't number their boxes (non-numeric labels ignored, soft-deleted rows skipped). Shared by `LotFormModal`'s add-lot prefill (defaults to `"1"` in the visible field) and `useTobaccoStore.addTobacco`'s starter cellar lot, so both number in lock-step. `dedupeIds(arr, startAt?)` — re-stamps missing / zero / negative / DUPLICATE ids in an array IN PLACE (keeps the first occurrence of each valid id; a unique non-numeric id like `"T1"` is left alone), minting fresh ids from a counter seeded above the max valid id present, and returns the next free counter so a caller threading a global counter (nxT…) can write it back. Deterministic + idempotent — the generalisation of the maintenance repair. `migrateData` runs it on every tobacco's lots, every pipe's maintenance and the five top-level collections (threaded through the reconciled nx* counters). This REPAIRS pre-existing duplicate ids (the invariants only FLAGGED them, and the counter reconciliation only prevented FUTURE collisions). **THE LOT PASS SEEDS ITS COUNTER FROM THE GLOBAL MAXIMUM AND THREADS IT, WHILE THE DUPLICATE TEST STAYS PER TOBACCO — and getting that split wrong is data loss in EITHER direction, which is the entry.** `dedupeIds` seeds its counter from the array it is HANDED and keeps a LOCAL `seen`, so `for (const tob of d.tobaccos) dedupeIds(tob.lots)` made two id-less lots under two tobaccos both become **1**: a MINTED id could land on an id another tobacco already carried, and `useTrashOps.permanentlyDelete("lot", id)` + `sweepExpiredTrash` both filter **by lot id ACROSS EVERY TOBACCO**, so purging one tobacco's trashed lot hard-deletes another's LIVE lot and clears `lotId` on its sessions — no user action, no message. Threading a globally-seeded counter closes that, and it is deliberately WIDER than the `lot-id-unique-global` invariant (which skips soft-deleted lots) because the trash ops do not: a minted id landing on a TRASHED lot elsewhere is exactly how the live one disappears. **THE OTHER DIRECTION WAS WRITTEN, SHIPPED INTO THE WORKING TREE, AND REVERTED BEFORE COMMIT — and this paragraph asserted it.** Flattening every lot in the cellar into one array and deduping THAT also re-stamps ids two tobaccos ALREADY share, which orphans every session referencing them: `session.lotId` is matched by VALUE and nothing warns. **A pre-existing case in `migrateData.test.ts` («does not treat the same lot id across DIFFERENT tobaccos as a duplicate») pinned exactly that and was RIGHT; it was one edit away from being overwritten to make the wider repair pass.** A visible duplicate is recoverable; a journal that quietly lost its lot links is not. So an EXISTING cross-tobacco duplicate is left alone. **The residual that left — the trash ops reaching across tobaccos — is now CLOSED at those call sites, which is where it was said to belong**: `lotRefKey(tobaccoId, lotId)` (utils.ts) is the identity of a lot REFERENCE, the same key `checkLotInvariants` uses for the balance, so the arithmetic and the deletion finally agree on what identifies a lot. See the trash-scope entry under useTrashOps. The function itself was always correct and tested; what was missing was the SEED it was called with — the five top-level collections had threaded their counters all along, and the lots were the one place that did not. Locked by `lotIdGlobalRepair.test.ts`, probed in BOTH directions (the per-array seed reddens 2 cases; the over-reaching flatten reddens my own «LEFT ALONE» case AND the pre-existing one).
│   ├── globals.d.ts      # Type declarations for Google APIs (JSZip, google, __PENDING_GDRIVE_*)
│   │
│   ├── components/
│   │   ├── Charts.jsx    # SVG chart components (hBars, vBars, donutChart, calendarHeatmap) — uses theme-curator tokens. `donutChart(items, size, weightUnit, totalLabel)` takes its centre caption from the caller (StatsView passes `t("chart_total_active")`) — it was the hardcoded French "total actif" in BOTH render paths, rendered as-is in all five languages. The same build routed the centre text's colour through `style={{ fill }}` instead of a `fill=` presentation attribute, per the themed-token rule. This file was invisible to ESLint until the .jsx config gap was closed (see the .jsx-scope note under the custom-rules index) — which is how those two strings survived. **Why `checkJs` stays off (measured, so it stops being re-raised):** turning it on reports ~76 errors here, and all but two are `TS7006 implicitly any` on a file that is deliberately untyped; the two substantive ones are `Element.style` on the `querySelector("[data-hbar]")` hover handlers, which are inert (the node is a real `<div>` and an `if (bar)` guard precedes it). What matters is what callers already get: TypeScript infers the signatures from the JS, so `StatsView`/`HomeViewV2` DO get **arity** checking — probed by passing 5 args to `donutChart`, which fails with `Expected 0-4 arguments, but got 5`. What they do NOT get is argument TYPES. A hand-written `.d.ts` would close that but introduce a second source of truth nothing verifies, which is a worse failure mode than the gap; a wrong-shaped array shows up as a visibly broken chart on first render.
│   │   ├── EB.tsx        # Error Boundary class (extracted from App.tsx). Chunk-load-error auto-recovery (SW+caches purge → reload) + 30s anti-loop guard + manual fallback screen. Re-exported by App.tsx so main.jsx / EB.test.tsx importers are unchanged. IndexedDB preserved across recovery.
│   │   └── curator/
│   │       ├── icons.tsx          # SVG line-icon set (Ico) + the `Orn` diamond ornament. `restore` (a counter-clockwise arrow) serves the trash row's per-row restore action, which had to stop being a TEXT button — see TrashModal. (`OrnRule`, a rule–diamond–rule divider, and its private `Rule` were DELETED: no view ever rendered either. They read as live because their own unit test imported them — **knip counts a test file as a consumer**, the same blind spot that kept `tobaccoHasTag` and `findParityGaps` alive.)
│   │       ├── primitives.tsx     # FadeIn, useEnter, AnimNum, Stars, Lbl, IconBtn, PressCard,
│   │       │                      # GrowBarH/V, useWAAPILoop, TopBar, PageTitle, SectionHead,
│   │       │                      # StatCard, StatTile, SpecRow, Stagger, ScreenWash,
│   │       │                      # EmptyState (icon + sentence + optional ways forward)
│   │       ├── BottomDock.tsx     # Bottom nav with animated brass indicator. The outer fixed strip is transparent + `pointerEvents:none` (only the translucent pill catches taps) and the pill is a light glass tint `rgba(18,24,21,0.24)` + `blur(24px) saturate(1.3)` (progressively lightened: 201 = 0.62/blur18, 202 = 0.32/blur24, 203 = 0.24/blur24) so page content scrolls clearly under the frosted pill (iOS/RTS "content under the tab bar"). The heavy blur keeps the dock labels legible over busy content. Position/portal/width invariants unchanged (still portaled to body, width 100%). Verify translucency on the INSTALLED iOS PWA. **These three values were VERIFIED on the installed iOS PWA — do not re-tune them without repeating that test.** The pill's tint and shadow were mode-blind: the tint was a raw `rgba(18,24,21,0.24)` inline here (a DARK glass that stayed dark on parchment, gold labels at 2.27:1 — it became the `C.dockPill` token, and its `rgba()` is exactly what the lint selectors were added to catch), and the `boxShadow` was likewise a hardcoded dark drop + white inset (now `C.dockShadow`). **Both dark fallbacks are byte-identical to the pre-fix values**, which is the whole reason the installed-PWA look could be declared unchanged rather than merely hoped unchanged; light mode gets a lighter, tighter shadow and no white top edge. The light tint's alpha was then set to **0.24, matching dark** (it had shipped at 0.72, i.e. barely translucent — reported from the app as "le mode clair n'est pas aussi transparent que le mode sombre"). **Headless proves nothing here**: Chromium does not rasterise `backdrop-filter` into a screenshot, so a picture of the pill shows an unblurred ground and invites diagnosing a blur bug that does not exist; `theme:contrast` measures the ratio but not the glass. The only trustworthy check is the installed PWA over scrolled content, in both modes.
│   │       ├── Modal.tsx          # Generic modal primitive (backdrop, esc, transition;
│   │       │                      # Deferred unmount for ghost-click defence.
│   │       │                      # Backdrop carries `overscrollBehavior:contain` (touch scroll can't chain to the background page); opt-in `capHeight` prop makes the panel a bounded flex column (maxHeight:100% of the padded backdrop, display:flex/column) so a caller's inner `overflow-y:auto; overscroll-behavior:contain` region owns the scroll instead of the whole backdrop — used by the Lot/Maint edit modals for a sticky top action bar + scroll body. **`capHeight` is the RULE for any modal taller than the screen, not an option for edit forms.** The catalogue fiche was the last one still on the old `maxHeight: "85vh"` + `overflowY: auto` shape and it reproduced the very report `capHeight` was written for — reported from the installed iOS PWA, swiping moved the catalogue list underneath instead of the fiche. MEASURED at 390x844 with the full catalogue behind: the fiche's own scroll range was 842-717 = **125 px** against a **5261 px** document, and `overscroll-behavior` was left at `auto` **on the element that IS the scroll port** — so a swipe outrunning 125 px chained onward. The backdrop's `contain` cannot help: it is not a scroll port in this layout (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`), and containment applies to scroll ports, so the chain walks straight past it. `85vh` was the other half — a guess that cannot know the backdrop's own padding (8% top + 24px bottom + both safe areas). **The shape that works, and the only one to use: `Modal capHeight` + an inner `flex: 1; minHeight: 0; overflowY: auto; overscrollBehavior: contain`** — `minHeight` is load-bearing, a flex item defaults to `min-height: auto` and refuses to shrink below its content. **CHROMIUM CANNOT ARBITRATE THIS**: a synthetic CDP drag stays contained both before AND after the change (no fling momentum, different WebKit chaining), so a headless run proves nothing here; what it does establish is the mechanism. CONFIRMED ON THE INSTALLED iOS PWA by the user. When you add a modal whose content can exceed the screen, use `capHeight` — do not reach for a `vh` cap. **That build's own claim, «Verified by sweep: no `maxHeight: <n>vh` scroll region remains in any modal», WAS FALSE, and how it was false is the lesson.** The sweep was `grep 'maxHeight: "[0-9]*vh"'` — ONE spelling — while the two survivors are written `maxHeight: "min(60vh, 460px)"` (SearchModal) and `"min(78vh, 700px)"` (SettingsModal), which that pattern cannot match; **neither carried `overscrollBehavior` at all.** **MEASURED BY ME, after publishing borrowed figures once — see the note at the end of this paragraph.** The property, which is what matters and does not depend on the fixture: the modal BODY is the scroll port and the BACKDROP is not, so the backdrop's `contain` cannot stop anything. Search at 390x844 on a 40-tobacco seed: backdrop `scrollHeight === clientHeight === 844` (travel **0**, NOT a scroll port) while the results list carries **5295 px** of travel over a 1527 px page. **Settings is the stronger case, because it shows the protection was accidental**: at 390x844 the backdrop overhangs its padded box by **8 px**, so it IS a scroll port and incidentally caught the chain — but at 834x1112 (iPad, a supported target: the column cap was raised to 760 px for it) the `min(78vh, 700px)` cap makes the panel fit, the backdrop measures 1112 = 1112, and the **857 px** body scroll had nothing below it. A defect that hides on the phone and appears on the tablet. This is the repo's oldest failure shape, already recorded at the hex-alpha rule: *each survived because every sweep grepped ONE spelling.* **A grep is not a sweep.** `modalScrollContained.test.ts` is the sweep: it keys on what MAKES something a scroll port (`overflowY: auto|scroll`), never on how its height is capped, so a `vh`, `min()`, `calc()` or `flex` cap is all one case. **Its rule is per-FILE, not per-port, and that narrowing was paid for**: the first version demanded `contain` on EVERY port and flagged three more — CompareModal's 240 px picker and two Settings widgets at 380/200 px — which are all NESTED inside a body that now contains, so the page behind was never reachable, and forcing them would BREAK the handoff where a short inner list gives its remaining travel to the modal body. What must hold is only that the chain meets a `contain` before the document. **Only `overscroll-behavior: contain` was added; the two `min()` caps were left alone** — unlike a bare `85vh`, `min(60vh, 460px)` is conservative by construction, so the cap was not the defect. **One more lesson, about the AUDIT rather than the code: the first version of this paragraph quoted the auditing agent's figures as if they were measurements of mine.** They were reproducible — Settings' 857 px and the 8 px phone overhang came back identical — but the Search numbers are fixture-dependent and mine differ by 4x, and none of that was known when they were written down as fact. **A number in this file must be one the writer produced.** Re-measure before quoting, or state the property instead.)
│   │       ├── FormFields.tsx     # FormScreen, FormSection, TextField, TextAreaField,
│   │       │                      # SelectField, StarsField, SegmentedField, PhotoField
│   │       ├── Notice.tsx         # Tinted info / success / warn / error banner
│   │       ├── MaturityChip.tsx   # THE maturity badge, covering ALL 4 bands (young/optimal/peak/tooOld). **Absorbed the warn-only `AgingBadge` (deleted)** — it named the same two states in different words, so the "Trop âgé" filter chip listed cards reading "TROP VIEUX" (all 5 languages, both pairs). One key per band FOR THE BADGE (the filter chips are a separate, mixed set — see the note below, which is what this sentence stops short of): `mat_old` adopted the domain wording (Overaged / Überreif / añejo) and `aging_warn`/`aging_soon` are gone; `bucketFromAgingStatus()` is the ONE bridge from `lotAgingStatus`'s two-state vocabulary. The `lg` size is AgingBadge's banner variant (body font, sentence case), used by LotFormModal. Colour/label/⚠ table locked here; labels via t("mat_young|mat_optimal|mat_peak|mat_old"). `alwaysCount` shows the "· N" suffix even for a single lot. **The inventory FILTER CHIPS still use their own keys for the first two bands** — `f_young` / `f_optimal` (`InventoryListView`) against the badge's `mat_young` / `mat_optimal`; the last two bands share `mat_peak` / `mat_old` outright and cannot drift. **That divergence was settled later, and it was far narrower than "two vocabularies" — MEASURE BEFORE BELIEVING A BACKLOG ENTRY.** Laid side by side, `young` differed only by a legitimate PLURAL (Jeunes/Jeune, Jóvenes/Joven, Giovani/Giovane), which is right because a chip labels a SET; `peak` and `old` are one key. **Exactly ONE band was wrong**, `optimal`, where five languages used a different word entirely: en "At peak", de "Reif", it "A maturità", es "En su punto", pt "No ponto". The English pair is why it had to move rather than stay a preference: band 2's chip said "At peak" while band 3's badge says "Near peak", so the two labels INVERT the real order — filtering "At peak" hands you the band before it. That is the control lying about which slice it selects, not a wording taste. `f_optimal` now carries each language's own `mat_optimal` word, pluralised exactly as `f_young` already was (Optimal / Óptimas / Optimal / Ottimali / Ótimas); **French was left alone because its chip already equalled its badge** — changing it would have been rewriting correct work to satisfy a sweep. The help's five chip mentions moved in the same commit. Locked by `maturityChipWords.test.ts`, which compares an accent-folded 4-char STEM (so a plural passes and a different word does not) and separately forbids any chip from wearing ANOTHER band's word — the defect exactly. Probed: restoring "At peak" names the band and the language. **The same defect was later found on the third surface, the fiche's aging BANNER.** `bucketFromAgingStatus` maps `lotAgingStatus`'s "approaching" straight onto the `peak` bucket, so the banner and the chips beneath it describe the SAME lots — and Spanish's `aging_nearing_peak` read « cerca del óptimo » while `mat_optimal` is « Óptima » and `mat_peak` is « Cerca del pico ». The banner named the band BEFORE the one it was describing, on the same screen as chips naming it correctly. **A SYNONYM is fine and two languages legitimately use one** (fr « approchent la maturité », pt « perto da maturidade »); what is forbidden is wearing ANOTHER band's word, which is the only thing the new block in `maturityChipWords.test.ts` asserts — same accent-folded 4-char stem as its siblings. Probed. Per-lot in InventoryDetailView LotRow (sm) + lot detail modal (md); the InventoryListView card shows the FULL distribution — one chip per present band, each with its lot count.
│   │       ├── ModalAction.tsx    # Primary / secondary / danger modal action button
│   │       ├── FilterControls.tsx # ToggleBtn / ActiveFilterPill / FilterChipSimple
│   │       ├── TrashIndicator.tsx # Amber trash IconBtn shown when dataRaw has soft-deleted rows (renders a count badge — 1-99, "99+" beyond — so users with multiple trashed items see the volume before opening it)
│   │       ├── AromaPicker.tsx    # The tappable "aroma wheel" — grouped, multi-select chips over AROMA_WHEEL (utils/aromas.ts). Props { value: string[], onChange, accent }; emits keys in canonical wheel order. Used IDENTICALLY by SessionFormView + TastingView (running stage). a11y: real buttons + aria-pressed. Read-only aroma chips render inline in JournalView's SessionDetailModal.
│   │       ├── ProgressiveMore.tsx  # The footer of a progressively-rendered list: the IntersectionObserver sentinel AND the labelled button, which are ONE decision. The sentinel is what makes browsing continuous (nobody taps a button every sixty rows down a catalogue); the button is what stops it being a cap that hides its own existence — the failure the Home's maintenance section already shipped once — and it is the only way through for a reader with no observer, navigating by keyboard, or arriving by find-in-page. Renders NOTHING when the whole list is shown, so an ordinary collection never sees it. `list_more` (six languages) carries `{n}`.
│   │       ├── TagChipRow.tsx     # The item's OWN collections on a FICHE, folded behind the "Collections · N" label (collapsed by default). Was a byte-identical copy in all three fiches (tobacco / pipe / accessory) before being promoted here. Measured before changing anything: with five tags the block wrapped to two lines (62 px at 390 px wide), so the fold wins a line back; with two tags nothing changes, since the label row exists either way. The COUNT on the closed label is part of the contract — a disclosure that hides how much it hides forces a tap to find out, which costs more than the row it saved. Locked by TagChipRow.test.tsx.
│   │       ├── TagEditor.tsx      # Shared free-text tag / collection editor (add via Enter/+, remove via chip ×, tap a suggestion to add an existing tag). All mutations go through the caller's sanitizeTags. Used identically by the Tobacco/Pipe/Accessory forms. Extracted from TobaccoFormView. **The REUSE SUGGESTIONS fold away** (`tag_reuse` + a count, collapsed by default) — up to 12 dashed chips is two or three rows inside a form section, and they are an accelerator rather than something you need in view while typing. The item's own tags and the input stay visible: hiding either would make the editor worse, not smaller. Locked by TagEditor.test.tsx.
│   │       ├── CatalogOffer.tsx   # The "this blend is in the reference catalogue — fill the form in one tap" offer (`ai_db_hint` + `ai_db_apply` / `ai_db_ignore`). **The two host views resolve the candidate hit with `tobaccoDbLookupSync(brand, name, lang)` — they used to clamp it to `lang === "en" ? "en" : "fr"`, a two-language leftover from before v1.3, so the catalogue DESCRIPTION the offer was judged against was the French one for four of the six languages. Pass `lang`; `pickLang` owns the requested → en → fr ladder.** Rendered by TobaccoFormView + WishFormView **directly under the BRAND field**, NOT inside AICard: the card sits above the whole form, so once the iOS keyboard is up it is scrolled off the top and a recognised blend went unnoticed (same action → feedback adjacency rule as the cloud-save status in Settings). Deliberately NOT a modal — the match is detected AS the brand + name are typed, so a pop-up would interrupt typing and steal the keyboard. Local `dismissed` state folds it away for the rest of the form; `busy`/`error` make it stand down while a fill runs. Its PLACEMENT (after the brand field, before the next one) is asserted on source order by CatalogOffer.test.tsx — presence alone would pass from the old spot too. **The form placeholders name a blend the catalogue can actually resolve**, so typing them DEMONSTRATES the offer instead of missing it. Locked by CatalogOffer.test.tsx, which checks the placeholder against the real catalogue file. **The identity order is BRAND then NAME in all four entry forms** (tobacco / wishlist / pipe / accessory) — tobacco and wishlist used to ask for the name first while pipes and accessories asked for the brand, the same two fields in opposite orders depending on the screen. Consequence for this component: the offer follows the SECOND identity field (the match needs both), so the placement test asserts `brand < name < offer < next field`, and a cross-form test asserts all four agree on the order.
│   │       └── AICard.tsx         # AICard — always visible (grey-out when no API key). AICard takes an `aiProvider` prop and renders the active provider name (`· anthropic` / `· openai` / `· gemini`) next to the title so multi-provider setups can see at a glance which key is about to run. **The `dbHinted` prop is GONE** — its catalogue hint moved to `CatalogOffer.tsx` (above); do NOT re-add a catalogue hint to this card. (The sibling `URLImageField` was removed — the URL-link image feature was dropped; forms now only import a device photo.) **The `· provider` tag carries `whiteSpace: "nowrap"`** — the space after the middle dot was a legal break point, so at 390 px in FR the header rendered `AUTO-` + a lone `·` on line 1 and `COMPLÉTER` + `ANTHROPIC` on line 2, the dot divorced from the word it qualifies. **Found by LOOKING at the card** while checking the greyed no-key state: the ratio was fine, every string was present, no box overflowed — no assertion or measurement in the repo could have caught it, which is the argument for occasionally rendering a surface and just reading it. jsdom does not lay text out, so `AICard.test.tsx` can only lock the declared `whiteSpace`; the one-line result was verified in a real browser at 390 px in fr AND de (`getClientRects().length === 1`).
│   │
│   ├── hooks/
│   │   ├── useAppUpdate.ts      # Update detection, countdown & SW update flow
│   │   ├── useBackNavigation.ts # (transport-only). Wires the two SYSTEM back inputs — browser/hardware back (popstate) + left-edge swipe — to a single `onBack` callback: installs listeners once, 400ms debounce, mount-time history seeding, latest-closure ref. The ROUTING moved to App.tsx's `goBack` + the pure `decideBack` (utils/navHistory.ts) — a DYNAMIC back stack, not the old fixed parent-mapping. In-app back arrows call nav() directly (not this). See "Back navigation & gestures".
│   │   ├── useAiAutoFill.ts     # AI auto-fill (multi-provider)
│   │   ├── useGdriveSync.ts     # Google Drive backup/restore/auto-save (sync domain; composes useGdriveAuth internally)
│   │   ├── useGdriveAuth.ts     # Google OAuth + token lifecycle (step 2 of the Drive split) — tkGet/tkSet/tkClear, account hint, gdriveGetToken, reconnect, iOS save-tap reauth, pendingOAuth capture
│   │   ├── useDropboxAuth.ts    # Dropbox OAuth + token lifecycle — PKCE + refresh token (silent renewal on every platform incl. iOS), wired into useGdriveSync via the provider switch
│   │   ├── useExportImport.ts   # JSON/CSV/ZIP export, file import, photo upload
│   │   ├── useOrphanPhotoGC.ts  # Orphan-photo garbage collector extracted from App.tsx. Mount-once, gated on `loading === false`, 4s-deferred; walks the latest `data` (entities + session snapshots) for `local-photo-*` refs and calls `gcOrphans` to drop unreferenced IndexedDB keys. Own internal data-ref. INVARIANTS (dep `[loading]` + `if (loading) return`) locked by imgGcGating.test.tsx, which now exercises the real hook (mocked gcOrphans) instead of a mirror.
│   │   ├── useStorageQuotaWarning.ts # Proactive storage-quota warning extracted from App.tsx. Probes navigator.storage.estimate() at mount + on `data` change; raises the `saveWarn` banner at ≥80% usage; 7-day dismissal via `cave-quota-warn-dismissed`, auto-cleared below 80%. Takes (data, lang, t, setSaveWarn).
│   │   ├── useLotIntegrityProbe.ts   # Startup lot-integrity probe extracted from App.tsx. Mount-only, 1.5s-deferred; runs checkAllInvariants/assertLotInvariants on the current data to feed/stand-down the diagnostic counter for users who open with pre-corrupted data (no save event yet). Faithful 1:1 (same `[]`-capture as before).
│   │   ├── useTrashOps.ts       # Trash operations extracted verbatim from App.tsx — restoreFromTrash / restoreSelectionFromTrash / restoreAllFromTrash / permanentlyDelete / emptyTrash. Takes { data, save, weightUnit }; returns the 5 actions wired into appCtx unchanged. Functions stay plain (not memoised) so they always close over the latest `data`. Operate on RAW `data` (which still contains the trash). Reduced App.tsx by ~280 lines. **A LOT ID IS NOT AN IDENTITY — the pair `tobaccoId|lotId` is, and all four lot paths here used to ignore that.** `permanentlyDelete("lot", id)` filtered `t.lots` on EVERY tobacco and then cleared `lotId` on every session carrying the id; `restoreFromTrash("lot", id)` cleared `deletedAt` on every match; `emptyTrash` and `sweepExpiredTrash` collected purged lot ids into ONE FLAT SET. That is safe only while lot ids are globally unique, **and `migrateData` deliberately leaves a pre-existing cross-tobacco duplicate alone** — re-stamping a valid lot id orphans every session referencing it, which is data loss in the other direction (see `lotIdGlobalRepair.test.ts`; the migration protects the MINT, not the existing pair, and named these call sites as the remedy). **So one tap of the × on tobacco A's trashed lot 42 hard-deleted tobacco B's LIVE lot 42 and stripped its sessions of their weight bookkeeping** — and the 30-day sweep did the same unattended, 6 s after launch, with no message. `restoreFromTrash` / `permanentlyDelete` take an OPTIONAL `scopeTobId` (the TrashModal builds its rows inside a loop over tobaccos, so it always has one; a caller without a tobacco to hand behaves exactly as before, and that is asserted so the scope cannot become a silent no-op), while the two BULK paths key their sets on `lotRefKey`. **`Entry.tobId` on the trash row is what carries it**, so adding a new lot row means carrying the parent tobacco. Locked by `trashLotScope.test.tsx`, probed at all four sites independently.
│   │   ├── useImportConfirm.ts  # Replace/Merge picker — shared by JSON import + Drive restore
│   │   ├── useTobaccoStore.ts   # Tobacco + lot CRUD, grouping state
│   │   ├── usePipeStore.ts      # Pipe CRUD, grouping state
│   │   ├── useWishStore.ts      # Wishlist CRUD, wishToInv, grouping state
│   │   ├── useAccessoryStore.ts # Accessory CRUD, grouping state
│   │   ├── useSessionStore.ts   # Session CRUD, lot weight deduction, grouping
│   │   ├── useDbSync.ts         # Shared "Sync with DB" hook used by TobaccoFormView + WishFormView (replaces ~80 LoC of inline duplication). Returns { dbSync, applied, applyDbSync, dismissDbSync } given form state + a stable entryId driving the dismiss scope. Syncs name / brand / category / cut / blend / force / roomNote / taste / agingMax **and `description`** (description + name + brand were added once the editorial pass made the catalogue prose authoritative — the earlier doc claiming description is excluded was stale). **`tastingNotes` is the ONLY field it never touches** — that is the user's own prose. Because the lookup resolves the catalogue description via `pickLang(e.description, lang)`, this path doubles as the answer to "my description is still in the old language": a stored description is user data frozen in the language active when it was filled, and re-opening the edit form under a new UI language surfaces it as a diff that one tap rewrites. **Ghost-click defence**: applyDbSync flips `applied` on for ~420 ms; while true the form renders an inert "✓ Synchronisé" Notice + a full-screen invisible tap-catcher overlay (`position:fixed inset:0 zIndex:9998`). On mobile the ~150 ms synthetic tap trailing the PressCard release used to land on a form `<select>` that shifted up under the finger (the banner collapses on apply) and pop its native option list open — the overlay absorbs that tap. Component-scoped (unmounts with the form), so NO leaked document listeners (an earlier document-capture-swallow attempt broke test isolation — the 450 ms window outlived each test and ate the next test's first click). The activeElement.blur() stays for the keyboard-focus case.
│   │   ├── useUserCatalogue.ts # The seam between `utils/userCatalogue.ts` (parse), `utils/catalogueStore.ts` (store) and the Settings UI. Exposes `catalogueMeta` / `catalogueBusy` / `catalogueOutcome` plus `loadCatalogueFile` / `clearCatalogue` / `downloadCatalogueTemplate` / `exportCatalogueCsv`. **Two things it must get right, both of which look like plumbing and are not.** (1) It calls `tobaccoDbInvalidate()` after EVERY change: that module caches for the whole session, so without it the app keeps answering from the previous catalogue — or the bundled fallback — until a reload, and the user, seeing the old data, concludes the import did not work. (2) It KEEPS the parse counts, because a catalogue that silently dropped a third of its rows looks exactly like one that loaded fine; `CatalogueStatus` renders them under the button that produced them (the action→feedback adjacency rule). **`catalogueMeta` has THREE states, not two** — `undefined` while the first IndexedDB read is in flight, `null` when nothing is stored, an object when one is loaded; collapsing the first two would flash « Aucun catalogue chargé » on every open of the one screen where the user manages their data. `dlFile` is INJECTED from `useExportImport` so a download behaves identically (iOS share sheet included) instead of growing a second anchor-click implementation, and the clock is owned here (`Date.now()` passed into the clock-free store). **« Vérifier mon catalogue » (`auditCatalogue` / `catalogueAudit` / `catalogueAuditBusy` / `clearCatalogueAudit`).** The status panel has reported the COUNTS — *N lignes sans identité, valeurs non reconnues : Zigzag Cut* — and on a 1594-row file that is a fact the user cannot act on: they have to find the lines in a spreadsheet and nothing tells them where. `parseCatalogueCsv` now also returns per-row `issues` (`{row, kind, brand, name, value}`, `row` counting the header so it matches a spreadsheet gutter), and this hook re-parses the STORED RAW CSV on demand to produce them. **Re-parsing rather than storing them is deliberate**: the meta carries counts and never rows, the parse is what the current code agrees with (a normalisation fix reaches an already-loaded catalogue for free), and nothing new is persisted so `CATALOGUE_PARSER_VERSION` does not move. **THE SCOPE IS THE FEATURE AND IT WAS SET BY THE USER IN ONE SENTENCE** — « Juste les champs obligatoires et les valeurs imposées. That's IT » — so it checks the two MANDATORY columns (`brand_key`, `blend_name`, which are also the row identity, hence duplicates) and the two whose values are IMPOSED (`category`, `cut`), and nothing else: no prose, no length, no per-language coverage, none of which the app judges at all any more (the Node checker that did was deleted with the bundled catalogue — see the tombstone section). A report that quietly grew a prose rule would start answering a question the button does not ask, so `catalogueAudit.test.ts` pins the ABSENCES as hard as the presences, and **`cat_audit_scope` renders in BOTH branches** — « aucun problème » over a check that looked at four columns would read as a verdict on the whole file (the `plan.locked` rule, other subsystem). A label the import map converts (`Navy Cut`, `Cigar`) is NOT reported: it is the contract, and flagging it would send the reviewer 'fixing' rows the app already understands. The detail list is capped at `MAX_CATALOGUE_ISSUES` (500) while the COUNTS stay exact, and the panel says when it truncated; there is a copy-to-clipboard button, because a phone cannot fix a spreadsheet and thirty row numbers are not something anyone transcribes. Verified in a real browser against a seeded catalogue carrying one of each defect — which is what found the last one: a `no-identity` row has neither brand nor name, so the fixed-separator concatenation rendered « Ligne 4 · » with nothing after the dot. **Probed five ways; the gate probe stayed GREEN and the absorbing layer is worth knowing — `ActionBtn` drops `role="button"` when it has no handler, so a rendered-but-ungated button was invisible to `getAllByRole` and the case passed for the wrong reason.** It asserts on the text now. **The panel was GENERALISED into `IssueListPanel` and the CELLAR csv import was given the same treatment** — a second copy of a list-with-counts-and-a-copy-button is the drift this repo keeps paying for, so only the COPY differs and the chrome keys were renamed out of the `cat_audit_*` family (`issues_row` / `_more` / `_truncated` / `_copy` / `_copied`) at the extraction. `scope` is a REQUIRED prop, not optional: both callers check a few named things and nothing else, so « aucun problème » must never stand alone. **The finding that build cost most was invisible to every test: `_runImport` closes the Settings modal for `source === "file"`, so the CSV panel rendered perfectly into a tab that had just shut.** Found by driving the REAL file picker in a browser; the panel renders fine in isolation, which is exactly why no unit test could see it. `stageImport` gained `keepModalOpen`, set by the caller ONLY when there is something to report — a clean import still closes, because the recap's « Voir » chip takes you to your tobaccos, which is where you want to be. The JSON paths are untouched. **Two more things came from LOOKING at the result rather than asserting on it.** The recap toast grew to four paragraphs and, at 360 px in German at the « L » text size, covered the Settings modal *while telling the reader to look at a panel it was hiding*; since `keepModalOpen` fires on exactly the condition that raises those lines, the panel with the counts AND the rows is always on screen, so the toast collapsed to ONE pointer sentence with no numbers (285 px, 37 % of the viewport, down from over half). And the first version of the six new i18n values was written through `.encode("utf-8").decode("unicode_escape")`, which decodes each UTF-8 BYTE as latin-1: the whole FRENCH set and every em dash in all six languages shipped as mojibake into the dictionaries. Caught by reading the rendered German panel. **Never round-trip text through `unicode_escape` — write UTF-8 directly.** **The prose caught up with the code, and MEASURING each claim is what made the pass short.** An audit listed nine items here; four survived contact with the repo, and the corrections to the LIST are worth as much as the fixes. **What was real:** `catalog_intro` still opened the catalogue page with « Catalogue INTÉGRÉ / Bundled / Integrierter » in all six languages, false; the three `csv_issues_*` strings hardcoded the FRENCH « Autre » in all six, so a German reader was told about a value their own dropdown never shows (`CATS_DE.Autre` is « Andere ») — they carry a `{v}` fed from `xl("Autre", CATS_EN)` now, the same map the dropdown reads, so it cannot drift; **FIVE** keys still said « base locale » / « DB » after the setting was renamed (the audit said three, and `ai_compare_identical` was the one nobody had counted); and the four panel closes measured **30×26**, under the house 44 AND under WCAG 2.2's 24, on the only way out of a panel opened from a non-toggle button. **What was NOT real, and the measurement that settled it:** « the French dictionary is tu » — it is **41 *vous* against 11 *tu*, mixed**, so the register was left alone and only the CONTENT of each string was changed; « section 4 contradicts section 11 » — **already resolved**, and this file's own note says so, which is exactly the stale-to-do failure that note was written about. **What the measurement RESHAPED:** Spanish is genuinely 50 *tú* to 18 *usted*, but the *usted* is not « the new strings » — it is the **catalogue family** (six strings, one of which mixes both in a single sentence), while every other `su` is a third-person possessive (« su cantidad », « en su punto ») that must NOT be touched; and Italian had four INFINITIVE buttons against a dictionary unanimous on the imperative (`Importa`, `Esporta`, `Rimuovi`) — with `cat_confirm_remove` deliberately left alone, because an infinitive in a confirm QUESTION is idiomatic Italian. **`privacy.html` disclosed the catalogue NOWHERE** — not the dedicated `cave-catalogue` IndexedDB (a real catalogue measured 3.77 MB), not the `cave-tabac-catalogue-*.csv` cloud writes — in the page a Google OAuth reviewer reads; a section per language now covers both, and says the file leaves the device only on an explicit tap. **THE GATES CAUGHT THREE OF MY OWN EDITS**, which is the argument for having written them: doc:check's dev-fallback rule found three `AICard` literals left on the old vocabulary, `helpQuotesAppLabels` found the Italian guide still quoting « Verificare il catalogo » immediately after the button became « Verifica », and the identical-value rule asked for an allowlist entry on each new fr/en cognate. **CLAUDE.md's own false statements were corrected in place rather than deleted** — the `tobaccoDbRetry` row, the `cave-autofill-source` default (documented `"ai"`, shipped `local` since 243), « all five gates » when `deploy.yml` has four, and two whole performance sections describing chunks since deleted — each now records what it used to claim, because a silently-corrected doc teaches nothing about how it went wrong. **An audit's live findings, and four of the six are the same shape: the CORRECT version of the rule was written one file over.** (a) **`badCategory` / `badCut` were derived by filtering the CAPPED `issues` list**, so a file with more than `MAX_CATALOGUE_ISSUES` defects under-reported — and one whose cap filled with no-identity rows first reported **ZERO** bad categories, the reassuring number, on the panel whose entire job is to say what is wrong. `parseCatalogueCsv` now keeps exact counters beside the capped detail, exactly as `parseTobaccoCsv` has, and the hook reads them. (b) **The reported ROW drifted on a file padded with blank lines** — the index was into the FILTERED body, and the old comment STATED the drift instead of fixing it; a reviewer opens their spreadsheet at that number, so a quietly-wrong one is worse than none. Each kept row now carries its true grid line. (c) **The audit report outlived its catalogue**: neither « Retirer le catalogue » nor loading a DIFFERENT file cleared it, so the panel described a file that was gone — or, worse, reported the previous file's rows under the new file's name. (d) **`IssueListPanel.copy()` said « Copié ✓ » for a copy that never happened**: `writeText` returns a PROMISE, so the synchronous try/catch could not see a refusal (denied permission, insecure context, Safari without a gesture), the rejection went out unhandled and the success state had already been set. Both other clipboard call sites in the app handled the promise; this one, the newest, did not. **THE METHOD NOTE: the four suites over this area were GREEN before any fix**, so nothing covered any of it — and two of the six probes stayed green after the fix, because the tests were on the PARSER while the defect had moved to the HOOK (the lesson: the wiring is what rots). `useUserCatalogue.behaviour.test.tsx` drives the real hook for exactly that reason, and `emptyCatalogueFixture()` was added because `useCatalogueCsv` rightly REFUSES a catalogue that parses to nothing, so « no catalogue » and « an empty one » are different states a fixture has to model separately. **The tests the audit said were missing, and one of the four was NOT.** Probed before written, which is the only way to tell a gap from a stale note: reverting the `useDbSync` fix reddens `catalogueValueNotRepresentable.test.ts`, so that one was already guarded and the audit's entry was wrong. **The other three were real, and each stayed green under a mutation of the whole suite.** (a) `_hasIssues` is `skipped > 0 || coerced > 0` and every fixture COUPLED the two, so deleting the coercion term changed nothing across 4961 cases — while a file whose rows all have an identity but whose taxonomy the app cannot read is the ordinary case for a spreadsheet exported from another app, and precisely the one the panel exists for. (b) **`CuratorCatalogueApplyModal` had no test at all** — the only confirmation between one tap and a write across every tobacco and every wish, including the two branches written specifically so a reassuring message cannot become a misleading one (the `locked` counter, the `missing` count); both were argued at length here and asserted by nothing. (c) **`catalogueSave`'s comment promises « One transaction, so a partial write is not possible » and a probe splitting it into three sequential `put()` calls left the suite green.** The rollback cases proved a FAILED write leaves no trace; nothing proved the three records were atomic in the first place, which is what makes that guarantee mean anything — the new case counts transactions on the fake, and the one after it shows the consequence (a failure on the LAST record must not strand a CSV with no meta, nor replace a working catalogue with half a new one). **The pattern across all four: a promise stated in a COMMENT is not a promise, and a fixture that couples two conditions tests neither.** **The Node catalogue tooling is DELETED (3 894 lines), and the reduction went one step further than the instruction.** The ask was to cut `catalogueChecks.cjs` back to what `enumMapsSingleSource.test.ts` needs. **Measured, the answer is NOTHING**: the maps' only two Node readers were `catalogue-check.cjs` and `catalogue-preflight.cjs`, so with both gone the parse mechanism had no consumer but its own test — it was verifying that a parser nobody runs parses correctly. **A guarantee whose subject has been deleted is not a cheap guarantee, it is a misleading one**, so the three cases that locked the two-sided arrangement went with it, their removal recorded in that file rather than left to be rediscovered. `catalogue-preflight.cjs`'s own header still described guarding `src/data/tobacco-db.master.csv`, a path deleted twelve releases earlier — the tell that the whole tree had outlived its premise. **The section it replaced is now a TOMBSTONE**, which states the loss plainly instead of quietly banking the deletion: nothing reads catalogue PROSE any more, so a German word in the French description or a percentage disagreeing across languages is caught nowhere — acceptable only because the prose is no longer OURS, and **if this app ever ships a curated catalogue again the checker must be restored BEFORE the first delivery, not after.** What survives is the part that was never about Node: `CAT_MAP` / `CUT_MAP` in `constants.ts`, read by the browser importer, with the cases that matter when the key comes from a CSV the USER wrote — null-prototype, a forged key passing through verbatim, an unknown label returned as-is so the caller can report it, every target canonical, nothing shadowing an already-canonical value. **A number worth recording because I got it wrong twice in one conversation**: I quoted ~3 900 lines from the audit, then « corrected » it to 2 064 having counted only the `.cjs` files, then measured 3 894 with the 1 830-line test included — the first figure was right and my correction was the error.
│   │   ├── useProgressiveList.ts # A FLAT list renders a bounded PREFIX, not every row. MEASURED at 390x844 on a 300-tobacco / 200-pipe / 5000-session cellar with a 20 000-row catalogue: the FLAT states are catastrophic — journal **185 654 nodes / 149 MB / 670 455 px / 13.3 s**, catalogue **200 613 / 93 MB / 1 220 601 px / 13.2 s**, inventory 21 757 / 35 MB. Both 13-second states are ONE TAP away (the « Grouper par mois » toggle, the catalogue's grouping toggle) and « Listes groupées par défaut » OFF makes three lists flat permanently. `useProgressiveList(items, step?)` → `{visible, hidden, revealMore, sentinelRef}`; `ProgressiveMore` renders the sentinel + the labelled button. After: journal **2 877**, catalogue **1 141**, inventory 4 480. **It is NOT windowing and the residual is disclosed on the module**: nothing is recycled, so a reader who genuinely travels 1.2 million pixels still ends with every row — what changed is that the ceiling is reached gradually and deliberately instead of instantly on a tap. Real windowing needs measured row heights (variable-height cards) against a WINDOW scroll with no inner container, i.e. a hand-rolled virtualiser that jsdom could not exercise at all — an unverifiable component is the worse trade. **THE COUNT IS DELIBERATELY NOT RESET when the source array changes identity, and the first version's opposite choice is the entry**: keying it to identity means a caller that builds its list inline (a `.filter()` in the render body — what several views do) never has its count recognised, so `revealMore` is discarded on the next render and the list is stuck at one step FOR EVER, with a button that visibly does nothing. Its own test caught that. Not resetting is safe because the count only grows by an explicit gesture, so a narrowed list shows at most what the reader already revealed and already paid for — there is no path back to a 13-second frame. **The hook must be called ABOVE each view's early return** (hook-order rule), which is why `PipesListView` hoists `pipesBase`/`pipes` above it. Locked by `progressiveList.test.ts` for the mechanism and by `listDomBudget.test.tsx` for the PROPERTY (a view handed N rows renders O(1) of them — see its own section); verified in a browser (60 rows → Enter on the footer → 120 → scrolling → 362, footer counting down from 19 940). **`useProgressiveGroups` IS ITS COUNTERPART, AND THE NOTE THAT USED TO STAND HERE — « the grouped defaults are fine (journal 4 142 nodes, inventory 437, catalogue 2 935) » — WAS MEASURED ON A FIXTURE WITH NINE GROUPS, so it measured nine collapsed headers and called the branch safe.** Re-measured with ONE group EXPANDED: journal 86 → **185 093** nodes (5 000 sessions in a month), inventory 92 → **140 093** (5 000 tobaccos under a brand), catalogue 128 → **200 073** — the same order as the flat states, one tap away, on a header that exists to be tapped. And the CATALOGUE has a second shape the cellar lists cannot have: its group key is the `brand_key` of the user's own CSV, so a file keyed one brand per row renders one header per row — **140 065 nodes at 20 000 brands, everything collapsed**. Both are capped now: `useProgressiveGroups()` bounds each expanded group's CONTENT in all five lists, and `useProgressiveList(groupedView)` bounds the catalogue's HEADERS. **It is a second hook rather than the first one reused because `useProgressiveList` keeps ONE count and a view has as many expanded groups as the reader opens** — which would mean one hook per group, called from inside a `.map()`, i.e. the rules of hooks broken outright; it keeps one `Record<key, number>` instead, the shape the `collapsed` maps already have, NULL-PROTOTYPE because the key is a brand from a user file (`__proto__` there makes a group that can never be expanded, however many times it is tapped — the defect already met on `CatalogView`'s own `collapsed` map). **RESIDUAL, disclosed: no IntersectionObserver inside a group** — the button is the way through, the same trade the Home's maintenance section makes; giving each group a sentinel means a callback ref per key feeding a shared observer, machinery for the accelerator rather than for the guarantee. The pre-existing « every ProgressiveMore is handed a sentinel » case was WEAKENED on exactly that half and the reversal is recorded on it: what stays universal is `onMore`, since a cap with no way through is a dead end. **One linear term survives on purpose** — the catalogue's brand `<select>` renders one `<option>` per brand (~1 node each, 20 000 at the app's own cap, against the ~7 each the headers cost); capping it would make the filter INCOMPLETE, a control lying about what it can select, so it is excluded from the budget assertion and pinned by its own case instead. Verified in a browser on a 20 000-blend single-brand catalogue: 142 nodes collapsed, **746 expanded**, « Afficher la suite (19940) » on screen, 1 346 after one tap with the count down to 19880.
│   │   ├── useStartupNotice.ts  # Fetches public/notice.json at mount (SW-bypass cache-bust) and surfaces a fresh broadcast message via CuratorStartupNoticeModal; tracks the dismissed id in localStorage["cave-notice-seen"]. See the Custom Hooks table for details.
│   │   ├── useUnsavedFormGuard.ts # The "unsaved changes" guard for the add/edit forms. Registers { isDirty (JSON snapshot compare, taken on entry), onSave, onDiscard } into App's `formGuardRef` (ctx.setFormGuard) while the form is active; goBack opens the `unsavedConfirm` modal when a MODIFIED form is being left. Latest-ref pattern (refs updated in an effect, read only from the goBack event). Used by all 5 form views (Tobacco/Pipe/Accessory/Session/Wish), add AND edit.
│   │   └── useTastingSession.ts # Live "tasting mode" — timer, pause, persistence, wake lock. **A forgotten session recorded TEN HOURS, and the auto-end was firing all along.** Reported as « l'arrêt automatique après 90+5 minutes ne fonctionne pas ». Reproduced: the auto-end DOES fire, it recorded **600 minutes**, and it said **nothing**. Two defects, and the first one is a mistake about what the check can observe. **(a) THE DURATION WAS THE WALL-CLOCK SPAN, NOT THE ONE THE RULE PROMISES.** `tastingEnd` computed `minutes` from `tastingElapsedMs()` on BOTH paths. The check can only run while JS runs, and an installed PWA is suspended outright once the phone locks — so a session forgotten overnight is only noticed at the next launch, hours after the moment it was due to close. Recording that span states as fact something the user demonstrably did not do, poisons « Durée moyenne par séance », and **contradicts the app's own notice, which has always read « clôturée automatiquement après 95 min »** — the app displayed 95 and stored 600. The auto path now caps at `tastingOvertimeThresholdMs() + OVERTIME_AUTO_END_MS`, i.e. the duration it WOULD have recorded had the app been awake. **The cap is the threshold ACTUALLY in force**, so two « Continuer » give 90+90+5 and not 95 — postponing is a deliberate act. The MANUAL path is untouched: the user was present, and the elapsed time is the truth. **(b) IT WAS SILENT.** `setSaveError` fired only when the lot had vanished; in the ordinary case the tasting simply disappeared and a session appeared in the journal — **indistinguishable from « the auto-stop does not work », which is exactly how it was reported.** A new `tasting_autoend_notice` (six languages, `{n}` interpolated so it can never disagree with the constants) goes out on the **amber** `setSaveWarn` channel, threaded in from App.tsx: an auto-end is a designed outcome, not a failure. The oxblood channel stays for the degraded case where no weight could be charged. **The residual disclosed here — a PAUSED session never auto-ending — was RESOLVED on the user's decision (« on applique la même règle, après 90+5 de pause »), and the earlier « a paused user is engaged with the app » rationale is REVERSED: true of a five-minute pause, false of a ten-hour one.** `tastingOvertimeMs()` is now THE overtime clock — the LONGER of the elapsed smoking time and the pause duration — and the prompt, the countdown and the auto-end all read it. **One value, three readers, because three readers computing their own would be free to disagree**, and a close with no banner before it is the harsher rule, not the same one. **What gets RECORDED is deliberately NOT capped here**: `tastingEnd` still caps on `tastingElapsedMs()`, frozen at the pause — so a 20-minute session paused and forgotten overnight saves as **20 minutes**, which is the truth. Capping it at 95 would invent 75 minutes that did not happen; the cap exists for the opposite error, and applying it blindly here would have been the same mistake mirrored. **The tick had to resume during a pause**, and that is the part a reader will want the reason for: it had been stopped on the grounds that a frozen timer has nothing to re-render, which died with this rule — the prompt and its countdown now advance while paused, and with no tick NO dependency of the auto-end effect changes, so a session paused with the app left OPEN would never close. Its deps still key on `stage` + `pauseStartTs`, so a keystroke in the notes field does not churn the interval (the finding the gate shipped with). Probed in both directions: neutering the pause clock reddens 5 cases, restoring the tick gate reddens 1. The six help blocks carry the new clause. From audit #5: the auto-end effect is `loading`-gated (`if (loading) return`) — a cold start with an overtime zombie tasting used to fire `tastingEnd` against the empty `INIT` data before `load()` resolved and `save(INIT+phantom)` OVERWROTE the on-disk cellar (🔴 data-loss, the exact post-regression class); it's now the 4th `loading`-gated startup path. Also: a soft-deleted lot counts as "missing" in `tastingEnd` (no phantom deduction), `readTasting` rejects a >24 h-future `startTs`, and the tick/wake-lock/visibility effects key on `[stage, pauseStartTs]` not the whole `tasting` (no per-keystroke wake-lock churn). `useTastingSession` now takes `loading` from App.tsx.
│   │
│   ├── utils/
│   │   ├── lotUtils.ts        # Pure lot pipeline (locateLotIdx, stepApplyDelta, stepAutoFinish, stepAutoReactivate, applyLotWeightDelta, applyLifecycleDates, pickJarLot). `makeLotDuplicate(lot, nextBox?)` — a FRESH full copy of an existing lot (for logging another tin of the same purchase): strips id/deletedAt, resets weightG→weightInitial, clears finished/disposed end-state (a finished lot returns to its origin status, a cellar copy drops its dateOpened), and sets the next box number. Pure — the store's addLotToTobacco stamps the fresh id. Drives the "Dupliquer" action in LotFormModal edit mode.
│   │   ├── appSettings.ts     # The app's PREFERENCES travel with every backup and export (`_settings`). `collectSettings()` reads them, `sanitizeSettings(raw)` validates an incoming block (untrusted — a backup file can be hand-edited), `applySettings(raw)` writes them. **`SETTINGS_KEYS` IS AN ALLOWLIST AND MUST STAY ONE.** The obvious implementation — sweep every `cave-*` key — would be a SECURITY DEFECT, not a shortcut: the same storage holds LIVE CREDENTIALS (`gdrive-tk`, the long-lived `dropbox-rt`, the OAuth CSRF state, the three AI API keys), and a JSON export is a file people mail to themselves and paste into support threads. A blanket sweep would put a working credential in it and **no test would notice, because the file would look correct**. So `FORBIDDEN` names every class that must never appear, and `appSettings.test.ts` is half NEGATIVE: it feeds each forbidden key in one by one, asserts the two lists are disjoint, refuses any key whose NAME smells of a secret, and — the load-bearing one — **reads the credential key literals out of `useGdriveAuth`/`useDropboxAuth`/`dropboxAuthCore` and asserts each is forbidden**, so the list stays COMPLETE rather than merely long (probed: putting `dropbox-rt` on the allowlist reddens 4 cases). The API key is deliberately NOT here — it has its own `_apiKey` channel with its own opt-out defaulting to EXCLUDED, and carrying it twice would silently defeat that. The device-local cloud routing keys (`cave-device-id`, the four fids, `cave-device-name`) are excluded for a different reason: copying them onto a second device makes both claim one auto-file, which is the convergence bug three earlier releases were spent fixing. **APPLIED ON REPLACE ONLY** — a replace adopts the backup's preferences (the user asked to make this device look like that backup, and the cellar is being wiped anyway) while a MERGE does not, because combining two cellars is no reason to inherit the other device's language, theme and units; that split lives in `useImportConfirm` and is asserted. Several keys are read once pre-mount (`cave-lang`, `cave-theme` in main.jsx) so they take effect on the reload the restore path already performs. Carried by all FOUR outputs — JSON export, ZIP, manual cloud save, and the SILENT auto-save (the one that would be missed, since nobody would notice its payload lacked the block); the import strips `_settings` out of the data blob so it can't be saved as a phantom field on the cellar.
│   │   ├── appStorage.ts      # Promise-based localStorage wrapper (get/set/delete) extracted from App.tsx — used by save(). set() rejects on failure (feeds the QuotaExceeded migration); get/delete resolve null. The synchronous crash-safe helpers `lsGet` / `lsSet` / `lsRemove` (try/catch — `setItem`/`removeItem` throw in Safari private mode / on quota) are THE app-wide path for ordinary Web-Storage writes. The `tabac-local/no-raw-storage-write` ESLint rule (error) bans direct `localStorage.setItem`/`removeItem`/`clear` everywhere except this file + the OAuth/token/CSRF domain (useGdriveAuth / useDropboxAuth / dropboxAuthCore / oauthReturn), which keep their dedicated guarded helpers (tkSet/hint*/dbx*) so the `no-storage-read-after-remove` rule can still see their literal removeItem calls. Do NOT route token/CSRF storage through lsSet. **`isAppOwnedKey(k)` + `wipeAppStorage()` — the factory reset's sweep, and the one place in the app whose rule points the OPPOSITE way to `appSettings.SETTINGS_KEYS`.** That list is an ALLOWLIST because it governs what LEAVES the device: a key missed there ends up in a file the user mails to themselves, so the safe failure is to omit. A reset governs what STAYS: a key missed here is a credential left on a phone that was meant to be wiped, so the safe failure is to OVER-delete — and over-deleting costs nothing, since everything in these namespaces is being reset anyway. **An allowlist here would rot silently in the dangerous direction, which is precisely the bug it would be written to fix.** So the rule is namespaced prefixes (`cave-` / `gdrive-` / `dropbox-` / `pipe-cellar-` / `ai-`) plus the `-api-key` suffix, verified against every storage-key literal in `src/`; a foreign key on the same origin is left alone, so this is not `localStorage.clear()`. Both storages, because `gdrive-tk` is in sessionStorage on every platform but iOS standalone. **Keys are COLLECTED before any removal** — `Storage.key(i)` re-indexes as the store shrinks, so removing inside the loop skips every other entry (probed: the naive version leaves five of ten). `resetWipesCredentials.test.ts` is the mirror of `appSettings.test.ts`: it reads the credential literals out of the same auth modules and asserts each is REMOVED rather than refused, so the two guarantees stay complete together. Probed in four directions (drop the suffix rule, mutate while iterating, revert the warning text, unwire the call) — each reddens.
│   │   ├── safeJson.ts        # `safeJsonParse(raw, fallback)` — crash-safe JSON.parse for UNTRUSTED input (corrupt localStorage markers, forged import files, AI responses); returns `fallback` on any parse error / null source instead of throwing. Use ONLY for untrusted input — a `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(x))` deep-clone of the app's own data can never throw and doesn't need it.
│   │   ├── imgCache.ts        # IndexedDB image cache (open, get, put, clear) + URL-safety helpers (isSafeExternalUrl, safeBgUrl). The network `dlImg` fetcher was removed (local-photos-only).
│   │   ├── lotInvariants.ts   # Runtime invariants (lot/session/pipe/accessory/tobacco/wishlist id-unique + balance + maintenance) + assertLotInvariants. From audit #4: added `checkTobaccoInvariants` + `checkWishInvariants` (tobacco/wishlist id-present + id-unique — previously only pipes/accessories had uniqueness) and a `session-id-unique` rule; these are the safety net for the silent duplicate-id path that migrateData's counter reconciliation now closes. Untracked lots (empty `weightG`) no longer emit weightG/weightInitial noise. `checkMaintenanceInvariants` — `maintenance-id-present` + `maintenance-id-unique` within a pipe's `maintenance[]`; flags the id:0 collision class (the ≤addMaintenance bug where a form-supplied id:0 overwrote the fresh id so update/remove hit every entry at once) loud at save() instead of corrupting silently. Mirrors lot-id-unique.
│   │   ├── diagnostic.ts      # Persisted violation counter (localStorage `cave-diagnostic-v1`)
│   │   ├── cryptoBackup.ts    # AES-GCM-256 + PBKDF2-SHA256 envelope for optional Drive backup encryption
│   │   ├── oauthDiag.ts       # Single-slot OAuth diagnostic event — `cave-oauth-diag`
│   │   ├── oauthReturn.ts     # OAuth-return handler extracted from App.tsx IIFE — locks the read-before-clear invariant via processOAuthReturn.test.ts
│   │   ├── platform.ts        # Single source of truth for IS_IOS + IS_IOS_STANDALONE (were duplicated in App.tsx + useGdriveAuth.ts). Pure module-level constants with SSR-safe typeof guards. useGdriveAuth re-exports IS_IOS_STANDALONE so useGdriveSync's import is unchanged. Do NOT widen IS_IOS_STANDALONE to Android PWA (breaks Android Drive auth — see iOS/Android parity).
│   │   ├── stats.ts           # Pure chart aggregators (computeTopTobaccos, computeTopPipes, computeChartStats, monthKeysLast12, safeNonNeg, computeSpendingStats — split per kind: monthly tobacco-lot spend + yearlyTobacco/yearlyPipes/yearlyAccessories series + per-kind & all-time/this-year totals; pipes & accessories are year-precision so only the yearly charts include them. ComputeLocationStats — session counts by commune + by country from the structured session location, spot/POI ignored, top-10 each; rendered as a two-column "Lieux" section in StatsView, display-only. ComputeAgingSweetSpot — average session rating bucketed by the tobacco's age (session date − lot dateProduction||datePurchased) into fixed cellaring bands (<1y / 1-2 / 2-4 / 4-7 / 7-10 / 10y+); returns per-bucket avg+count, a total, and a peakKey (best-avg band with ≥2 samples, else null). The rating is the SESSION's rating, or — since many users rate tobaccos but not individual sessions — a fallback to the tobacco's own rating when the session is unrated. Optional `category` param narrows the curve to one tobacco family (families age very differently — Virginias improve for years, aromatics fade — so a global curve averages contradictory behaviours); the StatsView card carries a per-family FilterChipSimple row (Tous + families present in the journal, shown only when ≥2 appear). Pure derivation from existing session/lot data, no new stored field; rendered as a "Note par âge du tabac" chart in StatsView — always visible with a diagnostic empty-state showing the usable-session count when < 3 usable or < 2 bands. ComputeAromaProfile — tallies the aroma-wheel tags across sessions (session.aromas), returns the most-frequent aroma keys + total taps + tagged-session count; rendered as an always-visible "Profil gustatif" bar chart in StatsView with a per-family FilterChipSimple row (mirrors the aging curve; families present among aroma-tagged sessions only). Reused per-tobacco (sessions pre-filtered by tobaccoId) for an "Arômes perçus" fingerprint section on InventoryDetailView — the aggregated aroma profile of one blend across the user's sessions of it. computeCostPerSession(tobs, sessions, {max}) — the real per-bowl cost of each blend. Each session's cost = **grams smoked × (lot price ÷ lot weight)** — the price PER GRAM of the exact lot smoked, times the session's recorded `weightG`. A blend's `costPerSession` is the mean of those over its sessions. (An earlier version naïvely divided total lot spend by session count, ignoring grammage + lot size — since corrected.) Per-item returns `{valueSmoked, grams, sessions, costPerSession}`; globals are `{globalValueSmoked, globalGrams, globalSessions, globalCostPerSession}`. A session contributes ONLY when fully measurable: grams > 0, a resolvable `lotId`, and that lot has price > 0 AND weight > 0 (`weightInitial`, falling back to `weightG`). So accounting-off sessions (grams 0) and price-less lots never distort the average; scoped to LIVE tobaccos + LIVE lots. Pure, no stored field; rendered as a clickable "Coût par séance" section in StatsView (rows → tobacco fiche via crossOpenDetail, back returns to Stats))
│   │   ├── gdriveApi.ts       # Pure Drive wire helpers (step 1 of the Drive split): makeBackupName, parseBackupCounts, classifyBackup, fetchWithTimeout, pruneByType, fetchRetry, findNewerCloudBackup (multi-device guard decision table), autoFileDeviceId / ownAutoFiles / pickKeepAuto (device-scoped sweep helpers), chooseAutoSaveTarget (picks the auto-file to reuse from THIS device's own files only: it matches the stored fid against `ownAutoFiles` and NEVER adopts a foreign device's fid, closing a multi-device data-loss path)
│   │   ├── cloudProvider.ts   # CloudProvider interface + gdriveProvider + dropboxProvider — the 5 wire ops (list / uploadNew / overwrite / download / remove); WireResponse contract (Drive-flavoured JSON is the normalised shape)
│   │   ├── dropboxAuthCore.ts # Pure Dropbox OAuth helpers: PKCE S256 challenge, auth URL, processDropboxReturn (fail-closed state + action whitelist + read-before-clear)
│   │   ├── rotation.ts        # Pure pipe-rest helpers: lastSessionDateByPipe, pipeRestDays, computePipeRest, isPipeRested, PIPE_REST_TARGET_DAYS (2 days) — feeds the rest chip on pipe cards/detail. Sub-day helpers sessionStartMs (date + optional HH:MM, noon fallback), pipeHoursSinceLastSession (hours since the pipe's most recent session strictly before a reference ms, excluding the edited session), PIPE_REST_MIN_HOURS (24) — drive the "pipe not rested (< 24 h)" warning in SessionFormView + TastingView.
│   │   ├── bowlEstimate.ts    # Pure bowl-weight estimation — chamberVolumeCm3 (cylinder from Ø×depth mm), CUT_DENSITY table + DEFAULT_CUT_DENSITY (0.25), estimateBowlWeightG, estimateSessionWeight(pipe, tobacco, sessDefaultWeight, weightUnit). Drives the session-time default weight = pipe chamber volume × tobacco-cut density (SessionFormView + TastingView); falls back to sessDefaultWeight when the pipe has no chamber dimensions. **CUT_DENSITY (effective packed g/cm³, keyed by the canonical CUTS value)**: Loose Cut 0.20 · Cube Cut 0.21 · Coarse Cut 0.22 · Ribbon 0.22 · Rough Cut 0.23 · Shag 0.24 · Broken Flake 0.25 · Flake 0.25 · Sliced 0.25 · Ready Rubbed 0.26 · Crumble Cake 0.28 · Coins 0.30 · Curly Cut 0.30 · Pressed 0.30 · Plug 0.30 · Rope 0.36 · Twist 0.36 · (Autre / unknown / no cut → DEFAULT_CUT_DENSITY 0.25). These are single effective values (not "brim-full"), calibrated against real per-bowl weights so a medium 19×40 mm bowl (~11.3 cm³) lands near reality (Ribbon ~2.5 g, Flake ~2.8 g, Rope ~4.1 g). **Flake/Broken Flake/Sliced share 0.25** (aligned to Broken Flake, then all three nudged 0.27→0.25 per a density research pass): a flake is rubbed out before packing, so it behaves like ready-rubbed, not a pressed block (an entire-flake density of 0.32 over-weighted a large bowl). **Cube Cut 0.21** (below Ribbon): cube cut packs deliberately airy for easy draw. **Coarse Cut 0.22** — the catalogue's reviewer used it on 33 rows and `CUT_MAP` was quietly folding it into `Loose Cut`, so a genuinely distinct cut both vanished from the form's dropdown and borrowed the wrong density. Sources describe it as *ribbon with small chunks*, coarser than loose cut and sitting between ribbon and wild cut — hence Ribbon's value, since it IS ribbon. **Reasoned from trade definitions, NOT calibrated against real bowl weights like the rest of the table**; the honest mitigation is that it replaces a 0.20 mapping, so the shift for those 33 blends is small. Two neighbours were checked and deliberately NOT promoted: `Cake` (15 rows) is the same object as Crumble Cake — a steam-pressed ribbon block you pinch and crumble, per smokingpipes and Paykoc — so the mapping is right; and `Mixture` (10 rows) is not a cut at all but a blend type, which is a data question for the catalogue's reviewer, not an enum gap.
```
