To change a cut's estimate, edit this table (the sole source of truth); the values + range (0.15–0.5) are locked by `src/__tests__/bowlEstimate.test.ts`. `chamberDimsPlausible(Ø, depth)` + the `CHAMBER_DIAMETER_MIN/MAX` (8–40 mm) / `CHAMBER_DEPTH_MIN/MAX` (12–90 mm) bounds — drives the advisory "dimension du foyer inhabituelle" warning (`chamber_dims_warn`) in PipeFormView so a mis-entered chamber size (cm typed as mm) can't silently blow up the estimate; empty/zero never warns. `shapeVolumeFactor(shape)` trims the cylinder volume for conical bowls (chamber narrows toward the heel), verified against pipe-shape references (Pipedia/smokingpipes/Peterson). Two tiers keyed by the canonical SHAPES value: MILD twin-cone `CONICAL_SHAPES_MILD` (Bulldog/Bullmoose/Rhodesian) → 0.90, STRONG flare `CONICAL_SHAPES_STRONG` (Acorn/Cutty/Dublin/Horn/Pear/Pickaxe/Tulipe/Woodstock/Zulu) → 0.80; everything else (incl. Churchwarden/Calabash/Hawkbill — stem/shank-defined or rounded, NOT tapered) → 1.0. `estimateBowlWeightG`/`estimateSessionWeight` take the shape (read from `pipe.shape`); densities are unchanged (calibrated for a straight wall). The two session forms are UNCHANGED — they already pass the pipe object, so shape flows through automatically.
│   │   ├── pipeMaint.ts       # Pure pipe maintenance-reminder helpers — lastMaintDate, pipeSessionsSinceMaint, isPipeMaintenanceDue, computePipeMaintenanceReminders, isCleaningMaint, PIPE_MAINT_SESSIONS_THRESHOLD (5). Usage-based: sessions smoked since the last CLEANING entry ≥ threshold → "à entretenir". Only `kind` light/full entries count (isCleaningMaint) — a "none" entry (repair/wax) never resets the counter; lastMaintDate filters on it. Feeds the amber chip on the pipe card (PipesListView) + fiche Notice (PipesDetailView) + the "À entretenir" section at the bottom of HomeViewV2. **All three now go through `isPipeMaintenanceDue`, and until they did that helper had NO production consumer at all**: `PipesDetailView` carried its own `sessionsSince >= threshold`, so the shared predicate read as dead code while a second copy of the rule shipped. The fiche keeps `pipeSessionsSinceMaint` beside it because the Notice states HOW MANY sessions, which a boolean cannot answer — so that ONE screen pays a second pass over the sessions (MEASURED: 1.96 ms at 5000, 0.4 ms at 1000), knowingly, against the 62.7 ms the same shape cost inside the collection loop, which is why the loop takes a precomputed index and the fiche does not. `maintRemindersEnabled` and the `active` test stay at the CALL SITE, ahead of the helper: the helper excludes `finished` while the fiche requires `active`, and short-circuiting also skips the extra pass on a retired pipe. **The ORDER decides which pipes the Home shows, and its tiebreak used to be meaningless.** `computePipeMaintenanceReminders` sorts by sessions-since-cleaning DESC, and the second rung was `String(pipeId).localeCompare(...)` — deterministic, so the list never flickered, and arbitrary: `"11" < "3"`, so it systematically favoured pipes whose id begins with a low digit (measured in a browser on twenty equally-overdue pipes: **11 · 15 · 19 · 03 · 07**). **A tie is the NORMAL case, not a corner** — at the default threshold most overdue pipes sit on the same small session count, and at a threshold of 1 nearly all do — so the id was deciding the Home's five and the real criterion decided nothing. The rungs are now sessions DESC → **never-cleaned first** → **oldest `lastMaintDate` first** → id, which is a meaning a user can follow: among pipes smoked the same number of times since their cleaning, the one cleaned longest ago is the most due. The id survives as the LAST rung so the order stays total and stable — two pipes cleaned the same day would otherwise sit in input order, which shifts whenever the collection is re-saved and would reshuffle the list between launches for no visible reason. DATE rather than `lastMaintMoment`: the reminder already carries the date (it is what the fiche shows) and two cleanings within one day is not a difference worth ordering a chore list by. **Nothing covered the order before this** — every case used distinct session counts, so the tie branch was unreachable; `pipeMaint.test.ts` now pins all four rungs, including that sessions still outrank the date so a future pass cannot promote it to the primary key. Probed three ways. **The delegation that unified the count also made it QUADRATIC, and that is worth knowing before the next such consolidation.** Merging the two implementations into `pipeSessionsSinceMaint` was right — one rule, one code path — but it dropped the session index the loop used to HOIST, so the map was rebuilt once per pipe: measured on one machine, 30 pipes × 5000 sessions went from **3.8 ms to 62.7 ms**, recomputed on every data write and on views the user is not looking at (`CuratorApp` mounts them all and both memos evaluate above their `return null`). `pipeSessionsSinceMaint` takes an OPTIONAL precomputed `momentsByPipe` — the private `sessionMomentsByPipe(sessions)` index, pipeId → session moments in ms — which keeps the single implementation AND the hoist. The other half of the same pair is `lastMaintMoment(pipe, todayStr)`: `lastMaintDate` gives the DAY (and is still what the fiche displays), this adds the TIME, so a bowl can be ordered against a cleaning logged the same day. It resolves the LATEST cleaning carrying that date, so two cleanings on one day give the later moment rather than whichever came first, and a cleaning with no `time` reads as NOON — the same fallback an untimed session gets, which is the load-bearing choice for legacy entries: it splits the day instead of swallowing it. The guard counts WORK, not time — a getter on `pipeId` tallies index reads, so it is stable on shared CI where a millisecond assertion would flake (probed: the regression reads 8400 rows for 200 sessions instead of 400). **A number worth stating honestly: the pre-same-day version measured 0.4 ms, and this one floors at 3.8** — that version compared date STRINGS, while this one builds a moment per session via `sessionStartMs`, which is the price of ordering a cleaning against a bowl smoked the same day. **That Home section asks for the FULL set (`topN` 0) and CUTS AT RENDER — five rows, then a button naming the remainder that opens all of them in a modal.** It shipped capped at 5 in the MEMO, reported from the app as « je ne vois toujours que 5 pipes à nettoyer »: a block titled *à entretenir* showing five of seven says the work is done when it is not, with nothing on screen admitting the truncation — the shape of the tile that counted one set and opened another. **The intermediate state was UNCAPPED, and this paragraph asserted that was the only right answer («  it is the one Home block that must stay so »); the user asked for the cap back with a way through, which is better than either extreme and is why the wrong claim is corrected here rather than deleted.** The distinction that survives: a cap is legitimate, a cap that hides its own existence is not — so `MAINT_HOME_ROWS` (5) and the « voir les N autres » button are **one decision and must move together**, which `HomeViewV2.test.tsx` asserts in both directions (the cap without the button is the reported defect; the button without the cap is a control that reveals nothing). The label names the **TOTAL still to clean** (`maint_see_all_n`), not the remainder it reveals — **and that REVERSES the first version of this button, on the user's instruction, for a better reason than the one it replaces**: « voir les 2 autres » answers *what is hidden*, a question about the screen, whereas this block is a CHORE and the fact worth carrying is *how much work is waiting*. The count on a checklist is the size of the job. Pinned by a case passing a `t` that carries the real `{n}` placeholder, since the shared harness's `mockT` returns the KEY and under it `{n}` never interpolates — so a label built from the wrong number would read identically and the arithmetic would be pinned by nothing. **The modal's inner gutter is 12px, the PAGE gutter, not the 18 every other modal uses**: this one mirrors a page section rather than presenting a form, and its rows carry a name that truncates, so a pixel taken there truncates it earlier than the same row on the Home for no gain. **Exact width parity is NOT reachable and the residual is disclosed rather than tuned away**: the shared backdrop already eats 12px a side, so the rows are 342px against the Home's 366, and a pipe name within ~26px of the Home's limit ellipsises in the modal and not on the Home (measured: « Savinelli Marte Rusticated 320 KS » clips by **2px** at 390px). Shaving the gutter to rescue one string is the "shorten the German" mistake; widening it means changing a primitive every modal shares. What IS guaranteed is that the ROW is byte-identical — height, padding, gap, ground, radius, border, photo box and type scale all measured equal on both surfaces. The MODAL replaces the earlier version of this button, which set a global `pMaintFilter` and navigated to the pipe list: that needed a boolean filter in ctx which every other drill had to clear or the list silently narrowed, and it dropped you on another page — the modal renders the SAME rows from the SAME memo, adds no global state, and leaves you where you were. `MaintRow` is extracted for exactly that reason: the section and the modal would otherwise be two copies of one row, eighty lines apart in one file. Fixtures give each pipe a DISTINCT name — with seven identical ones a cap of five still satisfies a "contains the label" assertion.
│   │                     # (`recommend.ts` is GONE. It held the │   │                     # "Vous pourriez aimer" engine, which scored the catalogue against
│   │                     # the taste profile and printed six rows at the top of the
│   │                     # unfiltered catalogue page. REMOVED on the user's instruction —
│   │                     # do not re-add it. The catalogue has been the user's OWN file
│   │                     #, so a section ranking it for them is advice
│   │                     # about data they curated themselves, and the page exists to be
│   │                     # browsed and searched rather than to make suggestions. Its two
│   │                     # i18n keys and `recommend.test.ts` went with it.)
│   │   ├── tasteProfile.ts    # "Votre profil" pure engine: computeTasteProfile(tobaccos, sessions, topN=3) — synthesises favourite families (categories ranked by average tobacco rating), signature aromas (from ≥4★ sessions, falling back to all), and the top-N tobaccos best matching that profile (score = 2×familyRank + 2×signature-aroma-overlap + rating). Pure derivation, no stored field; rendered as a "Votre profil" Home section (family chips → filtered inventory, top-3 rows → tobacco fiche).
│   │   ├── suggest.ts         # "Ce soir ?" pure suggestion engine: computeSmokeSuggestions (aging urgency / nearly-empty jar / recency / rating → top-3 tobaccos + reason codes), suggestRestedPipe (a rested active pipe, never-smoked = infinitely rested) — rendered as a Home section. **THE POOL IS EVERY PIPE THAT IS RESTED, and it was a flat five.** Reported from the app — « la pipe du jour c'est un peu toujours la même » — and MEASURED before anything was changed: twelve pipes smoked in turn over two months yielded **5 distinct picks over 14 launches, with SEVEN never proposed once**. Two compounding causes. `pool = min(5, n)` is an arbitrary number that cannot grow with the collection; and a never-smoked pipe carries INFINITE rest, so a handful of untouched pipes own the top of the ranking permanently and squat that pool for ever. `REST_SATURATION_DAYS` (14) fixes both at once by saying what the feature means: past a fortnight a pipe is rested and more rest buys nothing, so **every** pipe at or beyond the threshold deserves a turn and the pool is exactly that set. Same fixture after: **9 distinct**, the three left out being those smoked 1, 6 and 11 days ago — genuinely not rested. **The floor of five applies ONLY when nothing has saturated** (a heavily-rotated collection, where the five most-rested genuinely ARE the answer); written first as `max(saturated, 5)` it pulled UNRESTED pipes into the pool on a small collection — offering the pipe smoked yesterday beside one rested three weeks — and a test caught it. **A second half of that fix turned out to do NOTHING and was removed rather than kept:** a clamp pinning Infinity down to the threshold. The probe stayed GREEN without it, and the layer absorbing it is the pool rule itself — `Infinity >= 14` exactly as `90 >= 14`, so the pool holds the same pipes and the clamp only reordered a set the rotation traverses whole. *When a probe stays green, find out which layer absorbs it before assuming the test is wrong.* `restDays` still REPORTS the true rest (a pipe rested 90 days says 90) — the threshold ranks, it never rewrites. **`preferIds` is THE ACCORD, the positive counterpart of `excludeIds`.** The second half of the same report — « tu accordes aussi la pipe avec le style de tabac ? » — and the answer was no: there was only a NEGATIVE filter (avoid ghosting tonight's tobacco) and nothing that ever said *this pipe suits this bowl*. The caller passes the ids of pipes whose usage profile is dominated by tonight's family (`pipeAccordsWithFamily`, which owns the dedication thresholds so the rule is not written a second time), and it is applied **to the POOL, never before it** — rest stays a HARD constraint and the family match only decides among pipes already rested enough; preferring a pipe smoked yesterday would contradict the one thing this function promises. **A single accorded pipe is then returned every time, and that is WANTED, not the pinning bug this engine was fixed for twice**: the pin is to TONIGHT'S TOBACCO, which itself rotates, so the pipe follows it. An accord matching nothing in the pool is ignored (a suggestion beats none), exactly as `excludeIds` degrades. The result carries `matched` so the Home can SAY the accord happened (`home_pair_accord`, six languages) — an accord applied silently is a behaviour the user cannot see. Locked by `restedPipeAccord.test.ts`, probed in six directions. Previously: rotates among the TOP-N (min 5) most-rested pipes via `rotateDailyHero(sorted, now, pool, bucketMs)` instead of `pickDailyTie` (which rotated only the EXACT top-rest tie group and so PINNED a uniquely most-rested pipe — the user saw the same pipe on every relaunch while the tobacco kept changing). It gained a `bucketMs` param; HomeViewV2 passes the per-launch-shifted clock (`featNow` = Date.now() + homeRotationSeed()×FEATURE_ROTATE_MS) + FEATURE_ROTATE_MS so the pipe now varies every app launch + every 12 h, matching the "Ce soir ?" tobacco. `pickDailyTie` is no longer used in production (kept + tested for potential reuse).** Rotation helpers rotateDailyHero / dailyWindow / pickDailyTie all take an optional `bucketMs` cadence (the Home picks pass FEATURE_ROTATE_MS = 12 h). ComputeSmokeSuggestions gained `openOnly` (jar-lot-only eligibility) + `ignoreRating` (skip rating score/favorite) options, and the module exports `seededShuffle` / `mulberry32` (deterministic PRNG shuffle) — the Home "Ce soir ?" draw is now a seeded random shuffle over the open pool rather than a score-ranked window
│   │   ├── formSession.ts    # **L'identité d'une SESSION de formulaire — ce qui distingue deux ouvertures successives du MÊME formulaire d'ajout.** Une réponse d'IA dispose de 60 s, n'est pas annulée à la navigation, et se pose sur la copie de travail courante ; la garde par `id` règle les fiches en ÉDITION et laissait un trou **énoncé** : deux formulaires d'AJOUT portent tous deux `undefined`, donc ouvrir « nouveau tabac », lancer la recherche, ressortir, rouvrir, et la première réponse atterrissait dans le second formulaire. `bumpFormSession()` / `currentFormSession()` les séparent. **DEUX SOLUTIONS ONT ÉTÉ ÉCRITES PUIS REJETÉES, consignées pour que personne ne les retente** : se rabattre sur marque + nom — la chaîne scan-photo → auto-remplissage construit sa requête à partir du RÉSULTAT DU SCAN pendant que le formulaire porte encore la valeur précédente, donc cette règle jetterait une réponse attendue ; et estampiller un jeton DANS la copie de travail — les stores construisent l'entité par `Object.assign({}, source, …)`, donc le jeton serait PERSISTÉ comme champ fantôme sur la cave, un défaut pire que celui qu'il corrige. **LE SITE D'INCRÉMENT EST `nav()` LUI-MÊME, SANS LISTE DE VUES** : une liste de noms est la forme qui pourrit ici, « l'utilisateur a navigué » suffit, et un incrément superflu ne fait que jeter une réponse en vol — le côté prudent. Ce n'est PAS de l'état de formulaire : l'invariant dur interdit à `nav` de réinitialiser un `editXxxId` ou une copie de travail, et il n'y touche pas. Le formulaire d'ENVIE est le seul que `nav` ne voit pas (c'est un calque, pas une vue), donc ses deux ouvreurs l'incrémentent eux-mêmes. Verrouillé par `aiFormSessionGuard.test.ts`, qui épingle le mécanisme ET le câblage — et qui COMPTE les trois écrivains, parce qu'une garde sur deux sur trois est un correctif pour les deux tiers du défaut. Sondé trois fois. Le cas pré-existant de `staleWriteTargets.test.ts` a rougi et le renversement est consigné sur son assertion : la garde a été RENFORCÉE, pas remplacée — la moitié `id` reste requise, sans quoi une réponse pourrait se poser sur une AUTRE fiche ouverte dans la même session de navigation.
│   │   ├── homeRotation.ts    # Per-launch rotation seed for the Home suggestions. homeRotationSeed() bumps + persists localStorage["cave-sugg-rot"] once per JS context (app launch/reload) and memoises it; HomeViewV2 adds `seed × FEATURE_ROTATE_MS` to the rotation clock so each app open surfaces fresh picks on top of the 12 h time bucket. `_resetHomeRotationForTests` clears the memo. Never in backups.
│   │   ├── cellarInsights.ts  # Pure dashboard aggregators for HomeViewV2 — computeCellarMaturity (active lots bucketed young/optimal/peak/tooOld), computeYearConsumption (grams this year vs last + trend %), computeActivityHeatmap (weeks×7 level grid ending today) + activityHeatmapMonths (month index per column for the calendar's month ticks). ComputeCellarPeaks(tobaccos, {max}) — the POSITIVE counterpart to computeWatchlist: one entry per tobacco with ≥1 lot in the `optimal` maturity band AND no `peak`/`tooOld` lot (kept DISJOINT from the watchlist's aging signals), sorted by optimal-lot count. Drives the Home "À point" section (tabacs ready to enjoy at their best). LotMaturityBucket(lot, agingMax) — the shared per-lot classifier (young/optimal/peak/tooOld/null) used by computeCellarMaturity AND the inventory chips/cards. **An UNWEIGHED lot is classified, not skipped, and getting that wrong made one screen contradict itself.** The bail read `safeNonNeg(lot.weightG) <= 0`, and `safeWeight("")` is 0 — so a lot the user never weighed was treated as an EMPTY TIN and got no band, while `lotAgingStatus`, which has no weight test at all, went on calling it overaged. The fiche printed « 1 lot trop vieux » over lot rows wearing no badge; the « Trop âgé » chip listed a card showing 0 g and no band chips; and the Home « À fumer rapidement » tile counted lots the list it opens excluded — verbatim the defect `smokeSoonDrill.test.ts` was written for, through a different door. **The app already draws this distinction**: `isUntrackedWeight` exists because `safeWeight("") === 0` once made unweighed jars vanish from the session picker (reported from the app), and its own comment calls an empty weight *an ABSENCE of data, not corruption*; `addTobacco`'s starter lot is created with `weightG: ""` and `parseTobaccoCsv` blanks an unparsable number, so the state is ORDINARY. `cellarInsights.ts` simply did not import that helper. **An EXPLICIT zero still bails and must** — an empty tin has nothing to mature — which is the whole width of the exception, asserted in both directions. The young→optimal boundary is 40% of the effective agingMax (family default when unset — see effectiveAgingMax in utils.ts), so the bar, the filters, the cards and the fiche all agree. ComputeCellarDepletion(tobaccos, sessions, nowMs, windowDays=180, floorDays=30) — estimates how long the cellar lasts = remaining active stock (cellar+jar grams) ÷ recent consumption rate (grams smoked in the trailing window ÷ the effective span, floored at 30 d so a couple of recent sessions don't over-extrapolate, capped at the window). Returns null when unmeasurable (no stock, or zero weighed consumption — e.g. accounting off). Rendered as an "Autonomie estimée" footer line in the HomeViewV2 "Cave à maturité" tile (formatted weeks/months/years via plural()). No stored field; derived from live tobaccos/sessions. **LOT SCOPE — the whole story in one place.** When the inventory list is filtered to a LOT-LEVEL slice, every figure the card and the fiche show must describe the lots IN THAT SLICE. The API: **`scopeFromStatusFilter(statusFilter)`** maps the active filter to a `WeightScope` — the two statuses `jar`/`cellar`, the four maturity bands, `recent` ("Achats récents", `RECENT_PURCHASE_DAYS` = 90 via `isRecentPurchase(lot)`), and **`smokeSoon`, the ONE scope that is a union of two bands** (`peak` + `tooOld`); any other filter (Actifs, Tous, Épuisé, Stock bas…) selects TOBACCOS not lots → `null` scope → every figure keeps its full total. **`lotInScope(lot, scope, agingMax)`** is THE predicate (live, non-finished, then the scope test); **`scopedHeldWeight`** and **`scopedOldestAgeDays`** are built on it, and **`scopeLabelKey(scope)`** is the one label map. **The rule, app-wide and now ESLint-enforced (`tabac-local/no-unscoped-lot-read`): any figure derived from a tobacco's lots — card, fiche, sort key or banner — goes through those helpers.** WHY it needs enforcing: the same leak was fixed SEVEN times (18→24) before a systematic sweep of both views (mine + an independent audit agent) found five more sites — the fiche's aging banner ("1 lot trop vieux" about a CELLAR lot on a jar-filtered fiche), the `activeButUnusable` hint, the `nFinished`/FINI pill, the group-sort keys, and the main list sort in `App.tsx` (under "En pot" sorted by quantity the order looked arbitrary because it ranked on total stock). The two SORT keys are the reason prose wasn't enough: an order is invisible as text, so no amount of looking at the screen finds it. **Four decisions here must not be re-litigated.** (1) The filter→scope mapping lives in ONE place because it has to agree exactly with `App.tsx`'s `filtered` predicates; `overaged`/`approaching` are filtered through `lotAgingStatus`, whose verdicts `lotMaturityBucket` reuses verbatim for `tooOld`/`peak`, so one classifier covers all four bands with no second age formula. (2) **No local COPY of the predicate** — the fiche carried one, it had to be hand-synced with every new scope, and it silently wasn't: `"recent"` fell through to the maturity branch and matched nothing, which would have shown an EMPTY fiche. (3) This **REVERSES an earlier rule** ("show the full maturity distribution regardless of the active filter — transparency"): with a filter active the card describes the filtered lots and NOTHING else; unfiltered, the full distribution is unchanged. The reversal and its reason are recorded on the test that used to lock the old rule, so it isn't "fixed" back. (4) The **cellar-only maturity rule STANDS** — it was questioned and upheld: an opened jar is being smoked, so its age no longer qualifies it; it carries "ouvert depuis N" instead. **The FICHE follows the list's scope** (`InventoryDetailView`): same predicate for the hero weight, the lot count, "Plus ancien", the aging banner and `visibleLots`; because a fiche that silently hides lots is confusing it names the slice in a chip on the "Les lots" header with a **"Tout afficher"** override — LOCAL state (`lotScopeOff`), so it never touches the global filter and going back keeps it. Finished-lot mentions (the "· N terminés" sub-count, the "Voir terminés (N)" button) stand down under any scope. **Labels**: `scopeLabelKey` returns `lbl_in_stock` ("En stock") for the unscoped total — **"En cave" appears ONLY for the cellar slice** (the hero label was hardcoded to `f_cellar`, so an unfiltered fiche announced "EN CAVE" over a total including the jars, and a jar-filtered one said "EN CAVE" over a jar weight) — `f_jars`/`f_cellar` for the statuses, the `mat_*` labels for the bands, `f_recent` for recent, and `stat_smoke_soon` — the HOME TILE'S OWN key — for `smokeSoon`. **That union scope exists because the tile lied about what it opened, and the shape is worth knowing because it is the maturity-chip defect on a third surface.** The Home "À fumer rapidement" tile's value is `lotsApproaching + lotsOveraged`, i.e. the two right-hand bands of the "Cave à maturité" bar directly above it — and its tap set the filter to `overaged` ALONE. Reported from the app with two screenshots: the tile read **7** and the list held **1**. The COUNT was right for the label (smoke these soon covers the window you want to be in and the time past it, urgent for opposite reasons); the DESTINATION was the half that lied — a control naming a set and selecting a subset, exactly like a chip reading "At peak" selecting the band before it. Fixed by giving the tile a slice that HOLDS what it counts, never by shrinking the count to match the drill: that would have deleted the more actionable of the two signals. The chip wears `stat_smoke_soon`, the tile's own key, so the user can see they landed where they tapped, and it overlaps the two chips beside it on purpose — it is their union. **The tile counts LOTS while the list shows TOBACCOS, and that asymmetry is deliberate and asserted**: it is the established shape of every tile in that row (128 "Boîtes" likewise opens far fewer rows), and each card carries its own per-band lot counts, which sum back to the tile's figure. Verified in a browser end to end — tile 7, three cards, chips `PIC PROCHE · 1` + `TROP ÂGÉ · 1` / `PIC PROCHE · 2` / `PIC PROCHE · 3`. **The guide described this tile in all six languages and was wrong on BOTH axes** ("the number of TOBACCOS carrying at least one OVERAGED lot") — written from the drill rather than from the code, which is how a sentence can be false twice over and read as authoritative; rewritten, and `computeStats`'s own header comment, which claimed those counters count tobaccos, was corrected in place. Locked by `smokeSoonDrill.test.ts`, which pins the agreement between the tile's arithmetic, the filter and the scope, and probes in both directions. **`crossOpenDetail` takes an optional `scope`, and the SAME reasoning reached the Home ROWS one report later.** « À point » named a blend, the fiche opened on ALL its lots, and the four that had earned the chip were somewhere in the list — reported as *« ça m'ouvre le détail d'un lot… tu devrais ouvrir la fiche tabac avec les lots concernés »*. Unlike the tile's drill, nothing was hidden and nothing lied; it simply handed you a list and left you to find what the row meant, which on a blend held in a dozen tins is the same work the row was there to save. `computeCellarPeaks` selects on `optimal`, so that band IS what the row is about: the row now passes `scope: "optimal"` and the fiche opens on those lots, names the slice, and offers « Tout afficher ». **No new mechanism** — the fiche has followed the list's scope for a long time; what was missing was a caller handing it a value. **The scope MUST be applied after `nav()`**, which resets `statusFilter` to `"active"`, or it is silently discarded and the row looks wired while doing nothing (asserted positionally). **A row whose chip is NOT a maturity band passes nothing** — « jamais fumé », « favori », « pot presque vide » describe the tobacco, not a subset of its lots, and « stock bas » likewise; only `overaged`/`approaching` map through on the watchlist rows. Both render paths forward it (the list row AND the big hero card — a scope dropped at one of them is invisible except on the screen it fails on). Verified in a browser: hero `OPTIMALE 400g / LOTS 4`, the four lots listed, `FILTRÉ · OPTIMALE` + « Tout afficher ». Locked by `homeRowOpensItsLots.test.tsx`, probed three ways; an existing `HomeViewV2.test.tsx` case asserted the whole payload and duly caught the change, which is why it asserts the whole payload. The card qualifier, the fiche chip and the fiche hero all read from it, so they cannot drift. The hero's lot COUNT counts exactly the lots backing the weight beside it (it used to count finished AND soft-deleted ones: "945 g / 19 lots" mixed a stock figure with a historical count). **Under the JAR filter** the card also shows how long the OLDEST open jar has been open (`Ouvert · N`, largest `daysSince(dateOpened)` in scope) — the one figure that still made opening the fiche necessary from that list, and only under that filter (elsewhere it is noise on every row). `fmtLotAge` lives in `utils.ts` so the card and the fiche render the SAME string (11-month cap). **Testing notes**: the card's scope qualifier carries `data-scope` because the page's own filter chips render the same labels, so a text-level assertion was ambiguous; the hero weight animates from 0 via `AnimNum`, so view tests assert WHICH lots are listed and leave the weight maths to the pure-helper tests. `scopedOldestAgeDays` also fixed `oldestAge(tob)`'s counting of soft-deleted lots; `heldWeight` briefly gained an optional `status` argument for this and lost it again (nothing passed one once everything filtered through `lotInScope`).
│   │   ├── sortBrandName.ts   # `compareByBrandThenName` / `sortByBrandThenName` — THE brand-then-name order, in one place. The inventory list has sorted this way since long before (`sortBy === "brand"` in App.tsx) and the collection REPORT was later given the same order, on all three of its sections: a document you print and file is scanned by looking a maker up, which insertion order (the id counter) does not support. **Writing it a second time is the failure this repo keeps paying for** — the tag predicate lived in four copies, `FAMILY_AGING_MAX` was mirrored into the importer, `CATS` into the validator, each until it was unified — so the extraction swept ALL of it, and the sweep is what found the interesting part. There were FOUR copies: the `brand` mode, the tie-breaker at the end of the same sort, the **pipes** list, and the wishlist's brand mode. **The pipes one was not accent-aware**: it compared LOWERCASED strings with `<`/`>`, so « Éclipse » sorted after « Zippo » there while the tobacco list, on `localeCompare`, put it under E. One rule now, and the printed report reads in that order too. Its OWN module rather than `utils.ts` because `collectionReport.ts` is deliberately dependency-free and language-neutral — that is its design premise — and `utils.ts` imports `LANG`; a four-line comparator must not drag the i18n machinery into a string-only module. Comparison is on the RAW value, not a lowercased one, because that is what App.tsx always did and matching it is the point; a missing brand becomes `""` and sorts FIRST, again matching the app (asserted, so it is a decision and not an accident). `sortByBrandThenName` returns a COPY. Locked by `sortBrandName.test.ts`, whose wiring case COUNTS the report's three call sites rather than merely finding one — probed, and a containment check passed while a section had silently reverted to insertion order.
│   │   ├── shopping.ts        # "Liste de courses" engine — computeShoppingList(tobaccos, wishlist, {lowWeightThreshold}) returns { restock, wishes }: `restock` = OWNED tobaccos running low (active weight ≤ threshold) that you'd rebuy (`rebuy !== false`), `wishes` = the wishlist; each item carries a stable `key` ("restock:<id>" / "wish:<id>") for the persisted "got it" checkbox. shoppingCount(list) = total rows (drives the top-bar cart badge). Pure; tested in shopping.test.ts. Rendered by the CuratorShoppingModal. **Also exports** `tobaccoActiveWeight(tob)` (sum of non-finished, non-trashed lot weights) + `isLowStock(tob, threshold)` — the single low-stock definition shared by the shopping list `restock`, the inventory **"Stock bas"** filter chip (`statusFilter === "lowstock"`, App.tsx filter + InventoryListView count — moved to sit right after Wishlist and before Jeunes) and the inventory cart-icon count. The cart icon sits on the InventoryListView TopBar (shown when shoppingCount > 0). (It was also added to the HomeViewV2 top bar and then REMOVED from the Home per user request — the cart lives ONLY on the tobacco inventory page now.)
│   │   ├── collectionReport.ts # Pure `buildCollectionReport(data, {currencySymbol, weightUnit, dateStr, labels})` → a self-contained, printable HTML document string of the whole LIVE collection (soft-deleted rows + lots excluded) with per-item PURCHASE values + per-section subtotals + a grand total. Tobacco value = sum of its lots' `price`; **the WEIGHT is reported in two columns — `En cave` (sealed) and `En pot` (opened), each totalled on the subtotal row.** A single figure said how much you own and not how much is still SEALED, which is the question an inventory document filed with an insurer is actually asked, and the app distinguishes the two everywhere else. The split is `status === "jar"` versus **everything else that is not finished** — deliberately not two exact matches, because a hand-edited backup carrying an unknown status would then silently REDUCE the reported total; unopened is both the safer reading and the `BL` template default. The finished-lot rule is UNCHANGED and still settled (see below): a finished lot is in neither weight column and stays in the value. The `section()` helper gained an optional `extraSubtotals` whose cells SHRINK the label's colspan by the same count, so the row still spans the table exactly — asserted, since a mismatch renders a visibly broken table in a document the user prints. Labels reuse the inventory chips (`f_cellar` / `f_jars`), so the report says what the app says and no new key was minted; pipe / accessory value = its `price`. String-only (no React/DOM/network), every user string HTML-escaped (`esc`), garbage/negative prices coerced to 0. All labels arrive pre-translated in `opts.labels` so the module stays language-neutral. Downloaded via `useExportImport.doCollectionReport()` (blob → `cave-tabac-rapport-YYYYMMDD.html`) from Settings → Données → Export ("Rapport de collection"). Values are an acquisition-cost baseline (the disclaimer says so), not a live appraisal — no photos embedded. Tested in collectionReport.test.ts. **FOUR defects in the document handed to a third party, and the first one is a rule about where a guarantee gets verified.** `money()` carries a 1e21 guard whose comment promises « above it the digits are written out in full » — true in this module, and undone one line later: the string it built is handed to `formatNumber`, which is `fmtNum`, which **RE-PARSES it**. MEASURED on the document the app really produces: **`1e+24` THREE times** — the row, the tobacco subtotal and the grand total. Its own test could not see it, because that test injects no formatter and falls through to the identity default, **so the guarantee was checked on a path production never takes**. Fixed in `fmtNum` rather than in `money()` — that is where a string becomes a number again, so the leak was open everywhere else too (any big price, anywhere in the app) — and the new case drives the REAL `fmtNum`, with a permanent non-vacuity sibling proving the default alone cannot establish it. **The rule: a guarantee must be exercised through the collaborator production injects, never through the module's own default.** (2) **The « Valeur » column was 0 % visible on a phone.** MEASURED in Chromium: at 390 px the document overflowed by **79 px** and that column — the one carrying the report's whole reason to exist — was entirely off screen; at 360 px « En pot » went with it (109 px). Seven columns, three of them `nowrap`, cannot fit 326 px and never will, so the answer is not to shrink them but to make them REACHABLE: each table now scrolls inside its own `.tw{overflow-x:auto}` container, and `@media print{.tw{overflow:visible}}` because at 794 px the table fits and a scroll container would CLIP what the screen lets you reach. Verified after: **0 px of page overflow at both widths, the column at 100 % once the table is dragged**, print `overflow:visible` with the table at 718 px and nothing clipped. The 32 px body gutter also drops to 16 under 600 px — 64 px is a sixth of a phone screen on a document made of tables. (3) **`count + " " + items` short-circuited `plural()`**, so a one-item section read « 1 articles » in five languages of six (English escaping by luck); `itemsOne` joins `items` in the labels and the module picks, staying language-neutral. (4) **All three subtotal rows wore `totalValue`**, so the document showed « Valeur d'achat totale » FOUR times against four different numbers, three of which are not a total — `subtotal` is its own label now. Same build gave `<html>` a validated `lang` (it had none); the validation is `public/reset.html`'s lesson, which wrote `lang="constructor"` from a corrupt `cave-lang`. Probed five ways, each reddening exactly one case.
│   │   ├── compareBlends.ts # The pure engine behind the side-by-side blend comparison (`views/curator/CompareModal.tsx`, reached from the Catalogue page's TopBar). `compareItemFromTobacco` / `compareItemFromCatalogue` / `compareItemFromWish` normalise the THREE sources into one `CompareItem`, and `buildComparison` turns 2-3 of them into rows, each flagged `differs` — a comparison whose rows all look alike makes the reader do the diffing. **TWO rules carry the feature.** (1) **An unknown value is `null`, NEVER 0**: a Force never filled in must not read as "very mild" and a blend with no sessions must not read as "rated 0" — and in the stored data those are the same byte, because `BT` seeds `force: 0`. The view renders `null` as "—"; the test fixtures are built FROM the templates so a field that gains a 0 default later is covered by default. Note the deliberate exception: `sessions` legitimately reports **0**, because "you have never smoked this" IS an answer, unlike an unfilled field. (2) **The columns are ASYMMETRIC on purpose.** A catalogue or wishlist column answers the FACTUAL rows (category, cut, composition, F/RN/T, ageing, description — where a purchase decision is made) and none of the EXPERIENCE rows (your rating, stock, oldest lot, sessions, average session rating, cost per bowl, aromas perceived), which exist only for what you own. A row NO column can answer is dropped rather than printed as a line of dashes, and when no pick is owned the modal says so ONCE instead of letting the empty half read as a bug. `agingMax` falls back to the FAMILY default on every source, so a catalogue column does not show "—" against a cellar column showing that same family's default and look like a difference that is not. Pure: `computeAromaProfile` is composed, and the cost-per-bowl index is INJECTED (the caller runs `computeCostPerSession` once) so no aggregate is re-derived here. Locked by `compareBlends.test.ts` (20 cases).
│   │   ├── catalogueApply.ts # The BULK catalogue pass, reached from the **`copy` icon in the Catalogue page's own TopBar** (`CatalogView`). **It MOVED there from Settings → Données, and it must not go back**: it is an action ABOUT the catalogue, so the catalogue page is where someone thinking about catalogue data already is, and a page-level TopBar action is the app's established pattern (search / trash / cart / + all live in that slot). Icon-only is acceptable for a whole-cellar action ONLY because nothing happens on the tap — it opens the confirm modal, which states the counts AND what is never touched before a single field is written. It is on the MAIN TopBar only, not the loading/failure one, since you cannot apply a catalogue that is not in memory; and being on that page means the catalogue chunk is already loaded, so the plan step is effectively instant and needed no busy label (`cat_apply_busy` was deleted with the Settings button, and the ctx key `catalogueApplyBusy` with it — `startCatalogueApply` keeps the state internally as its re-entry guard). Locked by the « the catalogue-apply action lives in the header » block in `CatalogView.test.tsx`, which asserts the trigger reaches `startCatalogueApply`, carries an accessible NAME, is absent before the catalogue is in memory, and **no longer appears in `SettingsModal`** — two entry points for one whole-cellar action is how it came to sit in the row above « Effacer toutes les données ». `planCatalogueApply(data, lang, lookup)` returns what WOULD change (per-entity field diffs + counts of matched / unmatched / already-current) and `applyCataloguePlan(data, plan, nowIso)` returns the new data — two steps on purpose, because a pass over every tabac and every wish must never be a single unexplained tap: the confirm modal states the counts AND names what will not be touched, at the point of decision rather than in the help. It is the bulk counterpart of `useDbSync` (one fiche, at form-open) and shares its field set with ONE deliberate difference: **`name` and `brand` are excluded.** The per-fiche sync offers identity because the user is looking at that fiche and can see what they accept; a bulk pass reviews nothing, and silently rewriting the label the user recognises their tin by — across hundreds of rows — is both the hardest change to spot and the merge identity (`dupKey` is `brand|name`, CSV re-import dedups on it), so a bulk rename would quietly move rows on the next import. **The whole value of this module is one promise — personal data is never overwritten — so `PROTECTED_FIELDS` is EXPORTED and `catalogueApply.test.ts` asserts it field by field**, plus a general sweep asserting nothing outside `APPLIED_FIELDS` + `updatedAt` moved, with fixtures built FROM the `BT`/`BW` templates so a new personal field is covered by default rather than silently exposed. `description` IS applied, which is the one judgement worth stating: it is CATALOGUE prose, `useDbSync` has synced it and refuses only `tastingNotes`, and the merge rules make the same split — the user's own writing about a blend belongs in `tastingNotes`, which is inviolable. Pure: no clock (`nowIso` injected), no catalogue load (`lookup` injected), so the caller owns persistence and App.tsx's snapshot-undo can restore the whole cellar wholesale. Trashed rows are skipped; a no-op plan returns the SAME data object so a second run cannot dirty the cellar, trigger a cloud save or bump a single `updatedAt`; and it is idempotent (numbers and strings compared by value, so `3` and `"3"` are not an endless diff). Probed with six sabotages — leaking `tastingNotes` reddens 2 cases, applying identity reddens 3. **`catalogueLock`, the per-fiche opt-out.** A tobacco or wishlist row carrying it is skipped ENTIRELY by `planCatalogueApply` — **before the lookup**, so the catalogue is not even consulted for it, which is what makes "never overwritten" a property of the walk rather than of the field list. Set by a `CheckboxField` at the very bottom of `TobaccoFormView` and `WishFormView`; the field is on `Tobacco` + `WishlistItem`, seeded `false` by `BT`/`BW`, and **absent === false** (a legacy cellar must not read as globally locked — the worst failure here, since a pass that silently does nothing looks like success). **WHY A ROW-LEVEL LOCK when `PROTECTED_FIELDS` already exists**: that list answers *which columns*, and some users curate a WHOLE fiche by hand — a corrected composition, a strength they measured, a description they rewrote — for which the right unit of protection is the row. **SCOPE IS THE BULK PASS ONLY, and that is the design, not a gap**: the bulk pass reviews nothing, whereas `useDbSync`'s per-fiche offer puts the diff on screen and waits for a tap, so locking a row must not disable a control the user is looking at. `catalogueLock` is nonetheless listed in `PROTECTED_FIELDS` — unreachable today, but the pass must never be able to clear the flag that keeps a row out of it. **`CataloguePlan.locked` is a THIRD counter, deliberately not folded into `unmatched` or `alreadyCurrent`**, and the confirm modal renders it **in both branches**: without it, a cellar whose every matching fiche is pinned would read « Tout est déjà à jour » — true of the rows the pass looked at, false about the ones it deliberately did not, i.e. the reassuring message becomes the misleading one. Probed in three directions (neutering the skip, removing either form's checkbox) — each reddens. **An adversarial review of 206 found the promise failing on the path 206 never looked at, and the fix is a THIRD merge semantics.** `applyEntityLww` copies every UNPROTECTED key from a strictly-newer imported row, and `catalogueLock` was in no list: tick the box on device A, change a rating on device B whose copy predates it, merge — and the lock is gone, reported as « 1 fiche mise à jour ». **The obvious fix, adding it to `LWW_PROTECT_*`, is the SAME defect mirrored, and a test caught it before it shipped**: protect means local always wins, so a lock set on one device never REACHES the other, whose next bulk pass then overwrites the very fiche the box was ticked to protect — silently, since that device sees no lock. So `applyEntityLww` gained `stickyTrueKeys` (`LWW_STICKY_TOB` / `LWW_STICKY_WISH`): **a flag that may turn ON across a merge but never OFF.** `false` is the TEMPLATE default, so most `false` values are not a decision at all — verbatim the finding that a device overwrites fields it never touched. The asymmetry is affordable BECAUSE `plan.locked` is on screen: a lock you no longer want is visible and one tap away, a lock silently lost is neither. Same review found `wishToInv` copying the CURATED data (composition, force, description) and dropping the shield over it, and `migrateData` not normalising the field — `=== true`, never `!!`, since a hand-edited `"no"` is truthy. All three probed in both directions. **A CATALOGUE VALUE THE CELLAR CANNOT REPRESENT IS NOT APPLIED. It took THREE writers to close — and that count was WRONG: a fourth was found later, `CatalogView.addFromCatalog`, the most direct of them all.** The catalogue is the user's OWN file and `parseCatalogueCsv` keeps an unrecognised taxonomy label VERBATIM on purpose; `planCatalogueApply` copied `category` / `cut` straight across, as did `useDbSync`'s per-fiche offer and `useAiAutoFill.applyDbHit`. Reproduced: a catalogue row saying `Pipeweed` / `Zigzag Cut` landed in the cellar, where it is the known defect — no `CUT_DENSITY` for the bowl-weight estimate, no `xl()` translation so the raw string renders in all six languages, no `FAMILY_AGING_MAX` so the blend loses its maturity band entirely, and no matching option in the form's fixed dropdown, so the first save rewrites it anyway. **`applyDbHit` is the sharpest instance: its comment justified skipping the check with «the DB values are already validated by scripts/validate-tobacco-db.cjs» — a script DELETED with the bundled catalogue**, so the guarantee it named had not existed for seven releases. A comment asserting a mechanism that no longer exists is worse than no comment (the lesson, other axis). **SKIPPED, not snapped to `Autre`**: this pass's promise is that personal data is never overwritten, so replacing a correct category with the catch-all because the catalogue is approximate would be a downgrade, and on an empty field `Autre` adds nothing. A row whose ONLY diff is unrepresentable therefore counts as `alreadyCurrent` rather than as a change that applies nothing — the «banner that never leaves» shape. **WHY NOT AT THE JSON IMPORT, which is where the question started.** MEASURED over the whole history of `constants.ts`: `CATS` has had 5 successive values and `CUTS` 3, and **each is a strict SUPERSET of the one before** — no value ever removed or renamed — so an app-generated backup cannot carry a label the app no longer knows, and the catalogue is the only door. Guarding `migrateData` instead would run on EVERY load rather than on imports: a wrong table would silently rewrite the whole cellar at launch with no undo, a worse failure than the gap. That superset property is PINNED by a test, so removing an enum value re-opens the question loudly. Verified end to end in a browser (the fiche keeps `Virginia` / `Flake` while `blend` and `force` apply, and the confirm says 4 fields, not 6); probed five ways.
│   │   ├── tags.ts           # Pure user-tag / collection helpers — `sanitizeTags(raw)` (strings only, trim, collapse ws, drop empty, cap each 30 chars, dedup case-insensitively first-spelling-wins, cap 20/item), `tobaccoHasTag(tob, tag)` (case-insensitive), `tagCounts(tobaccos)` (distinct tags across LIVE tobaccos with usage counts, sorted count-desc then alpha, display label = most-frequent spelling), `allTags(tobaccos)` (flat sorted spellings). `Tobacco.tags?` / `Pipe.tags?` / `Accessory.tags?` (all `string[]`, tags were extended from tobaccos to pipes + accessories) sanitised on load by migrateData. The helpers are generic (iterate `.tags` + skip `deletedAt`), so `allTags(pipes)` / `allTags(accessories)` work directly. Drives the **shared** tag editor (`components/curator/TagEditor.tsx` — used by the Tobacco/Pipe/Accessory forms), the tag-filter chip row on all three list views (Inventory/Pipes/Acc), the three fiche chip rows (→ `navToInvByTag` / `navToPipesByTag` / `navToAccByTag`, each records the drill origin so system-back returns to the fiche), the three ctx filter states (`tagFilter` / `pTagFilter` / `aTagFilter`, reset in `nav()`), the list text filters + the global SearchModal (`searchAll` matches tags on tobaccos/pipes/accessories). Tested in tags.test.ts. **`tobaccoHasTag` was DEAD in production and the rule was written FOUR times.** `knip` could not see it: a test file counts as a consumer, so an export used only by its own unit test reads as live (the same blind spot recorded for `findParityGaps`). Meanwhile App.tsx inlined the predicate three times — tobaccos, pipes, accessories — byte-identical apart from the helper's extra `trim()`, which is defensive since `sanitizeTags` already trims on write. All three now call the helper, so the one tested implementation is the one that runs. **Routing them through it is what exposed the real defect: the ACCESSORY collection chip had never filtered anything.** `AccListView` is the one list that does NOT consume its `ctx.filtered*` memo — `PipesListView` reads `ctx.filteredPipes`, which is exactly why pipes worked and accessories did not, and why reading either file alone made the wiring look complete. The chip lit up and the "# tag" pill appeared while the rows never moved: worse than a dead control, because it claimed to have filtered. **MEASURED in a browser on all three lists** (tobaccos 2→1, pipes 2→1, accessories 2→2, then 2→1 after the fix) and confirmed identical against HEAD first, so the swap is provably behaviour-preserving and the bug provably predates it. Nothing in the suite covered any of the three — probed: neutering the helper reddened ONE unit test and no view test — which is why the fix ships with the four cases in `AccListView.test.tsx`. **The residual disclosed here — `filteredAccessories` with no consumer at all — was later REMOVED**, memo, ctx key and `AppCtxType` entry together, along with the stale key three test fixtures were still passing. It was left standing at 190 on the grounds that deleting a ctx key drags its type entry along and that a locally-filtering view is a defensible shape; both are true, and neither outweighs what the copy had just cost — the tag clause lived in the unread memo while the RENDERED view lacked it, which IS the bug. A defensible shape does not license a second copy of the rules. If a ctx-level accessory list is ever wanted, wire the VIEW to it in the same commit.
│   │   ├── csvImport.ts       # Pure `parseTobaccoCsv(text, {idBase?})` → `{tobaccos, rows, skipped, lots, headers}`. Reads the SAME per-lot CSV the app EXPORTS (`buildCsvLines`) — one row per lot, tobacco fields repeated — so an export edited in a spreadsheet round-trips. Columns matched by HEADER NAME (accent-/case-insensitive, `(…)` units stripped, FR+EN aliases), not position; only Marque+Nom required. Rows sharing brand+name (case-insensitive) collapse into ONE tobacco with several lots. Auto-detects `;`/`,`/tab delimiter, full RFC-4180 tokeniser (quoted fields, `""` escapes, quoted newlines, BOM, CRLF). Coerces defensively: category/cut → canonical or "Autre", ratings clamp 0–5, status → cellar/jar/finished (accepts Cave/Pot/Fini), rebuy Oui/Non→bool, dd.mm.yyyy→ISO, EN-locale export dates ("Mar 15, 2024" → ISO — so a CSV exported in EN date format round-trips its lot dates), garbage numbers blank. **STOPS at the first `=== SECTION ===` marker** (the full CSV EXPORT is multi-section — tabacs then `=== PIPES ===`/`=== ACCESSOIRES ===`/`=== JOURNAL ===` with different columns) so those rows aren't misread as tabacs (used to mint a junk tobacco named "=== PIPES ==="); returns `sectioned:true` so the caller warns "only tabacs imported". **The section-stop strips a leading `'`** — the export's `csvEsc` prefixes an apostrophe to any cell starting with `=` (CSV formula-injection guard), so the real marker arrives as `'=== PIPES ===`; without the strip the stop was silently defeated and the pipe/accessory/journal rows became junk tobaccos (caught by the export→import round-trip test). **There is also a `MAX_ROWS` (50000) soft cap** — returns `capped:true` when the file exceeds it (surfaced via `csv_import_capped`), defence-in-depth against a pathological file. Prototype-safe (own-property alias lookup, `Object.create(null)` maps). Pure + string-only (fuzzable). Consumed by `useExportImport.doImportCsvFile()` which feeds a **tobaccos-only** payload to `stageImport(payload, "file", {autoApply:"merge"})` — MERGE-only (never replace: a tobaccos-only replace would wipe pipes/sessions; merge dedups by brand+name so re-import can't duplicate). **Explicit errors**: a JSON file fed to the CSV importer → `csv_import_json` ("this is a JSON backup, use Import .json"); no Marque/Nom columns or no data → `csv_import_empty`; multi-section export → success + `csv_import_sections` note. The CSV export + template now carry a **Site vendeur** (`sellerUrl`) column. **The IMPORT CONTRACT applies here too, and the parser says WHICH ROW it could not read.** Two gaps and one silence, all measured before being fixed. **(1) `CAT_MAP` / `CUT_MAP` were never consulted**: `canonEnum` did a fold-equality against `CATS`/`CUTS` and nothing else, so a cellar CSV saying `Navy Cut` became `Autre` while the SAME word in a catalogue CSV becomes `Flake`. Those tables ARE the import contract — they exist for the trade labels a source writes — and a hand-typed cellar CSV is at least as likely to carry one as a curated delivery, since it is copied off the tin or exported from another app. An oversight, not a decision. The lookup is a precomputed FOLDED index (`_CAT_FOLD` / `_CUT_FOLD`), deliberately more tolerant than the catalogue's exact `mapCut`: a delivery is curated and its gate depends on exactness, whereas this module has been accent- and case-tolerant against the enums, so tolerating the same on the map is its own contract rather than a new invention. **(2) An unrecognised value is still snapped to `Autre`, and that asymmetry with the catalogue is deliberate**: a cellar fiche is edited in a form whose dropdown is FIXED, so keeping the value verbatim would have it silently rewritten the first time the user opens and saves it (the known defect); the catalogue has no such editing surface, which is why it keeps the value and reports it. What was wrong was only the silence. **(3) `skipped` had been computed and READ BY NOBODY** — so a file that lost rows for want of a brand or a name reported exactly the same success as a clean one, verbatim the lesson one importer over. The result now also carries `badCategory` / `badCut` (EXACT counts) and `issues: CsvImportIssue[]` — `{row, kind: "no-identity"|"category"|"cut", brand, name, value}` — capped at `MAX_CSV_ISSUES` (500, with `issuesTruncated`) while the counts stay exact. `row` counts the header as line 1 so it matches a spreadsheet gutter, and it is the LOGICAL grid row (a quoted newline shifts the physical line; the catalogue side reports the same way). A row saying literally `Autre` is NOT reported — it was understood — and neither is an empty cell nor an all-separators line (the blank-line guard fires first, which is right: reporting it would put phantom line numbers in the panel for every trailing newline a spreadsheet leaves behind). Enum values are read from the FIRST row of a brand+name group, so a coercion is reported against the row it was actually read from. Locked by `csvImportIssues.test.ts`. Tested in csvImport.test.ts.
│   │   ├── catalogueStore.ts  # **Where a user's catalogue lives between sessions** — `catalogueSave(csv, name, nowMs)` / `catalogueLoad()` / `catalogueGetMeta()` / `catalogueGetCsv()` / `catalogueClear()`, over a dedicated `cave-catalogue` IndexedDB. **IndexedDB and not localStorage because a real catalogue MEASURED 3.77 MB of CSV** — the app already warns at 80 % of the localStorage quota and the cellar itself lives there. **TWO records are kept and the duplication is deliberate**: the RAW CSV (the user's own file — what a backup carries, what an export hands back, and the only thing that makes a re-parse possible) and a PARSED cache stamped with `CATALOGUE_PARSER_VERSION`. **MEASURED: parsing 1594 rows takes 0.5-1.2 s on a desktop**, several times that on a phone, so paying it on every catalogue surface would be a visible hang — it is paid once, at load. They are separate KEYS so the hot path never drags 3.77 MB of CSV into memory to read a cache. A version mismatch RE-PARSES from the stored CSV and rewrites the cache best-effort, because otherwise a normalisation fix would never reach a catalogue already loaded and the app would go on serving a mapping the current code does not agree with; a failed rewrite costs a re-parse next session, never correctness. **The write contract is the one that has bitten this codebase twice**: an `IDBTransaction` failure is NOT a rejection — a quota-exceeded write ABORTS, and depending on the engine that surfaces as `abort` rather than `error` — so every write RESOLVES a boolean and callers inspect it (cover photos were lost exactly there once, and imported ones on the other side of the same mistake). **A parse yielding zero blends writes NOTHING**: replacing a working catalogue with an empty one because the user picked the wrong file is the failure that guard exists for. Clock-free (`nowMs` injected, the `applyCataloguePlan` convention). Locked by `catalogueStore.test.ts`, which drives the REAL module against a minimal fake IndexedDB rather than mocking the store away — **and that fake ROLLS BACK on abort**, because without it an assertion that a failed write left no trace passes on the fake instead of on the code.
│   │   ├── userCatalogue.ts   # **The BROWSER half of the catalogue importer** — `parseCatalogueCsv(text)` turns a user's catalogue CSV into the same in-memory shape `tobaccoDb.ts` has always served, plus `buildCatalogueTemplateCsv()` (the fill-in model) and `CATALOGUE_COLUMNS`. Pure and string-only, so it is fuzzable like `parseTobaccoCsv` beside it, and it REUSES that module's RFC-4180 `tokenize` / `detectDelim` (both exported for this) rather than growing a second tokeniser — the four-copies failure this repo keeps paying for. **It must normalise the taxonomy, and the frozen fixture `src/__tests__/fixtures/catalogue-excerpt.{csv,expected.json}` is what pins that** (the expectation is the output the removed Node importer emitted for those rows, frozen — not a re-derivation of what this parser happens to do): a catalogue whose cut says `Zigzag Cut` is one the app half-understands — the value misses `CUT_DENSITY` (the session bowl-weight estimate silently falls back), has no `xl()` translation, and matches no option in the form's dropdown, so opening and saving that tobacco REWRITES the user's cut (the known defect). Hence `CAT_MAP`/`CUT_MAP` moved to `constants.ts` — see that entry. **WHERE IT DELIBERATELY DIVERGES, and it is not a shortcut:** the Node importer DROPS a row missing any of category / cut / composition / F / R / T / description_fr / description_en, which is right for a delivered master where a partial row signals a defect. Here the input is a spreadsheet a person filled in by hand, so refusing every incomplete row would make the feature useless on the first attempt and say nothing about why; only IDENTITY (`brand_key` + `blend_name`) is required, the rest takes neutral defaults, and the result REPORTS what it could not read (`skippedNoIdentity`, `duplicateKeys`, `unknownCategories`, `unknownCuts`, `langs`, `error`). An unmappable enum label is kept VERBATIM and named — silently rewriting a user's vocabulary is how a catalogue starts disagreeing with the file it came from. Descriptions are OPTIONAL and per-language (`pickLang` resolves requested → en → fr → first present, so a French-only catalogue works and an English reader sees the French prose). **The cross-check in `userCatalogue.test.ts` is what makes the agreement a MEASUREMENT rather than an intention**: the browser parser was run over the real 1594-row master and compared against the Node importer's own output — zero differences on specs, aliases and brands, and the prose matched all six desc chunks. It earned its keep immediately: the header index is keyed on the NORMALISED name and the lookup was not normalising its own argument, so every camelCase column read as empty — `roomNote` came back 0 on all 1594 rows, and **`agingMax` was wrong on none of them ONLY because that catalogue's every `agingMax` is dropped by the family rule anyway**, a defect the data happened to hide. **The FORMAT is documented in the guide, and the taxonomy clause is the point.** The catalogue is the user's own file, so the column contract is the most important thing the guide can carry — and it carried none of it: it said "download the template, fill it in" and never named the two MANDATORY columns (`brand_key` + `blend_name`, which are also the row IDENTITY, so duplicates collapse on them), nor said that **`category` and `cut` must use the app's own taxonomy**. That last one is what makes the gap costly rather than merely thin: an unrecognised value is **NOT rejected** — it is kept verbatim and reported, deliberately, because silently rewriting someone's vocabulary is worse — so the row loads and the blend is then half understood (no `CUT_DENSITY` for the bowl-weight estimate, no `xl()` translation, no matching option in the form's dropdown, so opening and saving that tobacco REWRITES the user's cut — the known defect). Nothing in the app can warn about that at load time without guessing; **the guide is the only place it can be said.** A per-language `<h3>` block in section 11 now carries a column table + a `warn` block, in all six. It **links to the enum lists in section 4 rather than repeating them** — those two tables are what doc:check gate 22 already guards, and a second copy would be a second thing to keep in step. Locked by `helpCatalogueFormat.test.ts`, whose load-bearing case walks `CATALOGUE_COLUMNS` and demands every name appear in every language block, so **a 21st column cannot ship with the guide silently behind**; the four middle `description_*` columns are exempted BY NAME (the ellipsis form covers them) so the exemption is a decision rather than an accident. Required-ness is asserted STRUCTURALLY (`<strong>` in the cell) because the word differs across the six. Probed in both directions. **The block is a LIST, and the two rounds it took to get there are the entry.** It shipped as a three-column table (name / required / expected) and `i18n:layout` at 360 px in German at the "L" text size failed on it: the `category` / `cut` rows painted **169 px** past their hidden-overflow ancestor. Narrowing it to the guide's own two-column convention took that to **57 px** and no further; MEASURED, the table is **446 px inside a 334 px `.help-body`** and cannot fit even at min-content, because the first column holds unbreakable identifiers — `description_fr … description_pt` alone measures **252 px**. A `<li>` is normal-flow text: it wraps, it has no column to squeeze, and it cannot overflow. **The general rule: a term-and-definition block whose left-hand term is an IDENTIFIER wants a list, not a table** — the guide's five other tables are fine because their left column holds ordinary words. Two FACTUAL errors were made during the narrowing and both were caught, which is worth more than the layout fix: the branch adding the "required" lead-in fired on `Non — <strong>taxonomie imposée</strong>` (that cell also contains a `<strong>`), relabelling two OPTIONAL columns as mandatory in all six languages — found by READING the generated table — and then the Italian repeat of it, because the guard matched `taxonom` while Italian writes *tassonomia*, found by the new test. `helpCatalogueFormat.test.ts` now asserts that `category` and `cut` do NOT carry the required wording, deriving that wording per language from the `brand_key` item rather than listing six translations. Verified green afterwards over **all six languages × 35 screens at 360 px / "L"**. **CONVERTED, as the stage note required.** Both sides of that comparison left the repo with the bundled catalogue, so the block now runs against `src/__tests__/fixtures/catalogue-excerpt.{csv,expected.json}`: 20-odd rows lifted VERBATIM from the last shipped master over as many brands, paired with the exact specs / aliases / brands / six-language prose the importer emitted for them. **The expected side is still the importer's own output, frozen — not a re-derivation of what this parser happens to do**, which is what would have made the check circular. The rows were chosen to keep the cases the suite names — a multi-blend brand, punctuation and `&`/`and` tolerance, numeric aliases, a merged row, accent folding on both a brand and a blend, tokenised search — so the excerpt doubles as the fixture every lookup test now loads through `catalogueFixture.ts`. What the conversion LOSES is breadth — a defect on one row in a thousand can no longer surface here — and that is stated on the block rather than left to be discovered. Brand aliases in the expectation are RESTRICTED to what the excerpt's own rows declare, because the shipped list was the union over all of a brand's rows.
│   │   ├── watchlist.ts       # "À surveiller" passive reminders: computeWatchlist — one WatchItem per tobacco, most severe signal wins (overaged > approaching > low_stock), capped at 5 — rendered as a Home section. Low-weight threshold is user-configurable (Settings → Préférences, `cave-watch-low-weight`, default 25g). The stale-open-jar signal + its `cave-watch-stale-months` threshold were removed — the Home alert was noise. **Scope broadened** — the low-stock signal (renamed `low_favorite`→`low_stock`) now flags EVERY tobacco under the threshold EXCEPT those marked "à ne pas reprendre" (`rebuy === false`); the rating≥4 gate is gone. The overaged/approaching signals still flag ALL tobaccos INCLUDING "à ne pas reprendre" (goal: finish them before they're too old).
│   │   ├── geo.ts             # **`_COUNTRY_ROWS` is the SEVENTH per-language site of the class that produced `LANG_ASSETS` — 79 rows × one column per UI language, plus `_LANG_COL`. It is not scalar data, so it can't live in `LANG_ASSETS`; a missing column fails the same way instead, and silently: `iso2ToCountryName` drops to English, and a place name Nominatim returns in that language resolves to NO iso code, so the Stats "Pays" chart splits one country into a second row rather than summing it. `geo.test.ts` now walks `LANGUAGES` and asserts both directions per code (probed: removing `pt: 6` turns it red).** Pure geolocation helpers: isValidCoords (WGS84 range check), formatCoords (5-decimal display), osmEmbedUrl (OpenStreetMap iframe URL with bbox + marker), osmLinkUrl (external openstreetmap.org link). Used by SessionFormView + TastingView (capture) + JournalView SessionDetailModal. CountryNameToFlag / iso2ToFlag — country name → flag emoji for the Stats "Pays" chart, no extra stored field. The country table gained es/de/it name columns (`[iso, en, fr, es, de, it]`) so a country captured in any UI language resolves, and a new `countryNameToIso2(name)` exposes the canonical ISO code. `computeLocationStats` (stats.ts) now groups countries by that ISO so the SAME country logged under different languages ("France" / "Frankreich" / "Francia") sums into ONE row (label = the most-frequently-captured variant) instead of splitting; unknown/exotic countries still group per raw string. The JournalView "Pays" click-thru filter matches ISO-canonically to stay in lock-step. `iso2ToCountryName(iso, lang)` renders the country in the ACTIVE UI language, so the Stats "Pays" chart shows "Frankreich" for a German user even if the sessions were captured in French — the click-thru passes that localised name (the ISO-aware filter still catches every variant, and the journal chip reads the same as what was tapped). Stored session values + per-language journal LIST display are untouched (each session keeps the name in its capture language). **Reverse geocoding** — `parsePlace` (pure Nominatim-jsonv2 → the three parts) + `joinPlaceParts` (the displayed label), nominatimReverseUrl, reverseGeocode (**`formatPlaceName`, the convenience wrapper composing those two, was DELETED — nothing called it: `reverseGeocode` returns the PARTS, because the session stores name / city / country separately and the Stats « Pays » chart groups on the country alone, so the label is joined where it is displayed. Its eight assertions were KEPT, rewritten to compose the two live helpers, since that composition IS what the app does across the save/load boundary**) (coords → place name via nominatim.openstreetmap.org, no AI/key, resolves "" on any error). Powers the optional `Session.locationName` field: auto-filled on capture (both session types), editable in the session form. (the retroactive "Mettre à jour les noms de lieux" batch in Settings → App — `enrichSessionLocations` — was REMOVED. It was a one-time migration tool to backfill place names on pre-feature sessions; that's long done, capture always fills the name now, and the button nagged permanently + would overwrite manual edits.) The journal LIST shows the name only; the per-session OSM iframe map stays in the detail fiche. The Stats global map was removed — only the per-session iframe remains, so the multi-point helpers (osmBboxEmbedUrl, osmStaticMapUrl) and the staticmap.openstreetmap.de host were dropped.
│   │   ├── aromas.ts          # Aroma wheel taxonomy: AROMA_WHEEL (6 families × 5 descriptors = 30 aroma keys), ALL_AROMAS flat list, isValidAroma, sanitizeAromas (drop unknown/non-string, de-dup, canonical wheel order), buildTobaccoAromaIndex + tobaccoMatchesAromas (power the InventoryListView aroma filter: index tobaccoId→Set(aromas) from sessions, AND-match a wanted key list). Keys are STABLE canonical ids ("vanilla"/"leather"); labels resolve via t("aroma_<key>"), group headers via t("aroma_group_<key>"). Adding an aroma = edit AROMA_WHEEL + add aroma_<key> to all 5 dicts (no view code changes — picker/journal/stats all iterate the wheel). Session.aromas stores the selected keys; migrateData sanitizes on load.
│   │   ├── ghosting.ts        # Pure pipe-ghosting advisory: computePipeGhostingRisk(pipeId, tobaccoId, sessions, tobaccos) — counts the categories a pipe has smoked (sessions → tobacco → category), and when the pipe is clearly dedicated (≥3 sessions, dominant family ≥60% share) to a ghosting-prone family (GHOSTING_FAMILIES = Latakia/Balkan/Anglais/Aromatique/Oriental) AND the incoming tobacco is a DIFFERENT family (with a ghosting family on either side), returns {dominant, count, total} to drive a soft `Notice tone="warn"`. Returns null otherwise (not enough history, no dedication, same family, both mild). Consumed IDENTICALLY by SessionFormView + TastingView (the two session entry points must behave the same). Display-only advisory — never blocks save. No new stored field. ComputePipeUsageProfile(pipeId, sessions, tobaccos) — the persistent "usage profile" (families smoked, most-used first, dominant + share + a `ghosted` flag reusing the same ≥3/≥60%/ghosting-family test), rendered as a "Familles fumées" bar section on PipesDetailView with a dedication Notice. Ghosting is a property OF THE PIPE, so the profile lives on the fiche; the session-time warning is the point-of-action echo. **`pipeAccordsWithFamily(pipeId, category, sessions, tobaccos)` is the POSITIVE counterpart** — same dedication test (≥3 sessions, dominant family ≥60%), opposite direction: ghosting asks whether the incoming tobacco CLASHES with the pipe's habits, this asks whether it MATCHES. It deliberately does NOT require a ghosting-prone family — a pipe dedicated to Virginia accords with Virginia just as much as a Latakia pipe does with Latakia, though Virginia leaves no ghost to worry about. It lives HERE and not at the call site because the thresholds are the same ones, and a rule written a second time is what this repo keeps paying for. Drives `preferIds` in `suggestRestedPipe` (see suggest.ts). The two integers it shares with the guide are pinned by the `pipe-dedication-min-sessions` label contract.
│   │   ├── tobaccoDb.ts       # THE catalogue LOOKUP. `loadTobaccoDb` reads the user's catalogue from `catalogueStore` and caches it in module memory; `tobaccoDbLookup` does a case-insensitive <brand>|<name> lookup with fuzzy fallback + brand aliases + ASCII fold for accents/ligatures + tokenized search via `tobaccoDbSearchMatch`. `tobaccoDbInvalidate()` drops the session cache after the user loads or clears a catalogue — without it the module would serve the previous one for the rest of the session and the user would conclude the import had not worked. **THE USER'S FILE IS THE ONLY SOURCE.** The app once SHIPPED a catalogue (1594 blends by the end) as a content-hashed JS chunk plus one lazy prose chunk per language, and this module resolved between the two. All of it is gone: the `import("../data/tobacco-db.json")` fallback, `ensureLangDescriptions` + the `tobacco-db.desc-*.json` glob, `loadTobaccoCatalogue(lang)` (one phase now, so it was redundant with `loadTobaccoDb`), `tobaccoDbSource()`, and the whole failure-kind apparatus — `isChunkFailure` / `tobaccoDbFailKind` / `tobaccoDbRetry`, written to tell an offline chunk fetch from a corrupt one. **That apparatus was not merely unused, it was MISLEADING**: `null` used to mean "the download failed" and now means "you have not loaded a catalogue", which is the ordinary state of every fresh install — so a "Retry" button would send the user to a remedy that cannot help, and the surfaces show `CatalogueMissing` (Réglages → Données) instead. `catalogueLoad` never rejects (no IndexedDB → null), so a caller only ever handles null, never a throw. Locked by `tobaccoDbUserSource.test.ts`; every lookup test now runs against `__tests__/fixtures/catalogue-excerpt.csv` through the REAL parser (see `catalogueFixture.ts`). See "AI Integration" for the catalogue-first / AI-first integration.
│   │   ├── bannerStack.ts   # `pickTopBanner(state)` — THE single ordered decision for the five `position:fixed; top:0` overlays (save error > quota warn > photo error > newer-cloud-backup > export reminder). They all occupy the SAME rectangle, so any two visible at once are stacked; mutual exclusion used to be PAIRWISE and incomplete — `exportReminder` yielded to saveError+saveWarn but not photoErr or cloudNewer, `photoErr` yielded to saveError but not saveWarn, `cloudNewer` yielded to all three errors but not exportReminder. **`cloudNewer` and `exportReminder` therefore rendered TOGETHER at the same z-index (489)**, where DOM order decided: the export reminder renders last so it painted OVER the cloud-newer offer. On a wide screen it covered it entirely and nothing looked wrong; at 390 px the cloud-newer bar is taller (date + a Restaurer button, and it wraps) so its lower edge stuck out — two bars reading as one message, and the green one's ENTIRE surface opens Settings → Données. Reported with exactly that width dependence (« le popup de mise à jour est bien apparu sur mon iPad mais sur l'iPhone le fait de cliquer sur le message a simplement lancé la sauvegarde »). **Do NOT add another pairwise yield — that is the thing that rotted; add the banner to `TOP_BANNER_ORDER`.** Locked by bannerStack.test.ts (incl. an exhaustive 2^5 sweep asserting the pick is always null-or-one-declared-id) + the « only ONE top:0 banner renders » block in Overlays.test.tsx. **The banners were given the gate the pill already had: `anyModalOpen` — no top banner renders while `importModal` / `searchOpen` / `trashOpen` / `lightbox` is set.** They sit at z489-492, the shared `Modal` at z200 and the lightbox at z250, so a banner raised over an open modal covers its header INCLUDING the 44 px close X, and sits outside the modal's focus trap. The reachable case is destructive: with Settings open, « Vérifier les sauvegardes cloud » can raise the cloud-newer banner, whose « Restaurer » calls `stageImport(…, {autoApply:"replace"})` with NO confirmation — landing exactly where the user reaches to close Settings. Standing down costs nothing (the state persists, the banner appears on close). `CuratorUpdatePill` had checked `importModal` for this exact reason; the banners were simply never given it. **THAT GATE LISTED FOUR MODALS BY NAME, WHICH IS THE SAME MISTAKE AS THE PAIRWISE YIELDS IT SITS BELOW — and one paragraph up in this very entry is the sentence forbidding it.** `importModal` / `searchOpen` / `trashOpen` / `lightbox` are the four App-level overlays; every OTHER modal in the app was invisible to the gate — the lot form, the maintenance form, the catalogue fiche and its QuickAdd, the comparison, the shopping list, the encryption prompt, the unsaved-changes confirm, the countdown dialog, the welcome and startup-notice pop-ups — so a banner painted straight over it. **Reachable by the most ordinary route there is**: the export reminder appears on any device 30 days without a backup and MEASURES 110 px at 390 px, while the modal backdrop pads 8 % (≈ 68 px) from the top, so the panel's first ~42 px — its title and its close X — sit under it. Nothing exotic required. The gate now asks **`modalStack`, the ONE registry every `Modal` reports to and the same one `goBack` consults**, so the two can never disagree about whether a modal is open.
