Module state does not re-render React, so `subscribeModalStack(fn)` was added (it reports the CURRENT state on subscribe, then on every push / unregister / `closeTopModal` / reset) and App mirrors it into `stackModalOpen` on ctx. **The four named states STAY**: `importModal` gates a LAZY chunk, so it is set before that modal has mounted and registered. `_resetModalStack` notifies too — a leftover `true` would silence every banner in the next test. **When you add a banner, add it to `TOP_BANNER_ORDER`; when you add a modal, do NOTHING — the registry already knows.** Locked by `bannerOverModal.test.tsx`, which drives the REAL `Modal` (a hand-pushed handler would pass even if `Modal` stopped registering) and asserts the App wiring at source level, since the gate and the registry can both be perfect while nothing joins them. Probed three ways.
```
│   │   ├── bottomToast.ts # `pickBottomToast(state)` + `BOTTOM_TOAST_OFFSET` — the mirror of `bannerStack.ts` for the OTHER end of the screen. Four overlays are `position: fixed` near the bottom at `zIndex: 500` (undo · import recap · just-updated · language-detected) and **three of them shared `bottom: 40`**, which puts a z500 toast ON TOP of the z30 dock pill — its top edge sits around 56 px from the bottom once the home-indicator safe area is added. Only the undo toast ever had clearance, and it had it as a LOCAL literal, which is exactly how the other three drifted away from it. Two defects, then: they cover the dock, and any two visible together stack with DOM order silently picking the winner — the finding verbatim, at the opposite end. **There is deliberately NO modal gate here, unlike `pickTopBanner`**: a top banner covers the modal header including its 44 px close X, these do not — and the import recap IS the feedback for an action taken inside Settings, so hiding it would break the same action→feedback adjacency rule that put the catalogue offer under the brand field. Different end of the screen, opposite answer, on purpose. `BOTTOM_TOAST_OFFSET` is the ONE offset all four use so the clearance cannot drift again; verify any change to it on the INSTALLED iOS PWA, since the safe-area inset is precisely what headless cannot reproduce. Locked by bottomToast.test.ts (incl. an exhaustive 2^4 sweep asserting the pick is always null-or-one-declared-id, and a source assertion that no `bottom: 40` survives).
│   │   ├── duplicates.ts    # The duplicate FINDER + the merge that heals it. `findDuplicateGroups(data, kind)` groups LIVE rows sharing `brand|name` (per kind) with the facts a human needs to choose — lot count, active weight, session count, `updatedAt`, inventory numbers; `duplicateCount(data)` totals the rows awaiting a decision; `mergeDuplicates(data, kind, keepId, dropIds)` carries a tobacco's LOTS / a pipe's MAINTENANCE onto the kept row, repoints sessions (and each session's `lotId` onto the moved lot's fresh id, clearing it when the lot did not come across — the rule), and SOFT-deletes the dropped rows so the 30-day trash is the undo. Pure; the caller owns persistence. **WHY IT EXISTS**: an earlier release made the pre-uid cross-device doubling VISIBLE but could not heal it — nothing in the data separates "one row whose identity diverged" from "two genuinely different items", so only the user can decide. **THE LOAD-BEARING RULE is which inventory number may pair two tins, and it was wrong TWICE before the user corrected it**: "non-empty" is wrong because jars are not numbered, and "unique among THIS row's lots" is wrong because the add-form default (`"1"`, on most of the user's early jars) appears exactly once per row and on dozens of rows. The rule that holds is **CELLAR-WIDE RARITY** (`boxNumberTally`): a number pairs only if ≥2 members of the group carry it AND it occurs nowhere else in the cellar. **VERIFIED in the same build: a lot promoted cellar→jar KEEPS its number** (`changeLotStatus` → `applyLifecycleDates`, which rewrites only `status` + the dates), so a promoted jar keeps its rare incremented number and stays identifying while a directly-created jar carries the shared default and is excluded — the rarity test separates them without knowing either lot's history. **The merge NEVER rewrites an inventory number**: it is written on the physical tin, so renumbering would desynchronise the app from the shelf; two moved lots may end up sharing a number, which is precisely the signal that they are the same tin. Both wrong rules and the no-renumber rule are pinned as fixtures in `duplicates.test.ts`.
│   │   ├── dockVisibility.ts  # Pure `shouldShowDock(view, {showWishForm, editWishId})` + `NO_DOCK_VIEWS` — the bottom-dock visibility decision extracted from CuratorApp so it's unit-testable. Doc/reading pages (help/changelog/privacy/licenses) + tasting + full-screen forms hide the dock. Locked by dockVisibility.test.ts.
│   │   ├── chipRowScroll.ts  # Pure `chipRowScrollTarget(geometry)` — where a horizontally-scrollable chip row must scroll so a given child is fully visible, or `null` when it already is. Drives `ScrollableChipRow`'s `revealChildIndex` (the Settings tab strip). Minimum move, never centred; `null` is load-bearing so the row never fights a swipe the user made deliberately. Pure because jsdom reports every layout offset as 0, so the arithmetic is unreachable through a render. Locked by chipRowScroll.test.ts.
│   │   ├── modalStack.ts      # Global open-modal stack (pushModalClose/hasOpenModal/closeTopModal). Every open shared `Modal` registers its onClose; goBack closes the top-most modal on system-back/swipe so ALL view-local modals (catalog QuickAdd/fiche, lot & maintenance, cellar-confirm, encryption prompt, unsaved-changes confirm) are dismissed without per-modal wiring. Locked by modalStack.test.ts.
│   │   ├── navHistory.ts      # Pure dynamic back-navigation engine — `NavLoc` screen snapshot, `pushLoc` (immutable, skips forms/tasting, de-dups, caps at 40), `decideBack(state)` → `BackAction`, `fallbackParent`, `isFormView`/`isRestorable`/`sameLoc`/`locIdentity`. Replaces the old fixed parent-mapping with a real back STACK so system-back/edge-swipe returns to the ACTUAL previous screen. App.tsx owns the stack ref + executes the actions; useBackNavigation is transport-only. Locked by navHistory.test.ts. See "Back navigation & gestures".
│   │   └── docPage.ts         # Language-agnostic extraction for the in-app doc pages (changelog / privacy / licenses, consumed by DocPageView; HelpView shares only `pickLang`). extractLangSection(html, lang) slices the `<div id="sec-<lang>">` block resolved dynamically (any 2-3 letter code) with the DOC_LANG_FALLBACKS chain (requested → en → fr → first present); extractBody(html) is the monolingual fallback (licenses is English-only, no sec-* wrapper); stripDocChrome removes the wrapper open tag + `<script>` + back link + lang-toggle button + leading `<h1>` (rewritten to use DOMParser node-removal instead of regex tag-filtering — CodeQL fix; falls back to the trimmed raw HTML in a non-DOM env); extractDocContent = lang-resolve then chrome-strip. pickLang(map, lang) is the shared fallback resolver reused by HelpView's per-section title/body maps. **Adding a UI language is content-only**: author a `sec-<code>` block in each public/*.html (+ `<code>-…` h2 ids in help.html + doc-check's HELP_SECTION_IDS + a src/i18n/<code>.ts dict) — NO view code changes.
│   │

│   │       ├── HomeViewV2.tsx         # THE Home (sole layout — the classic HomeView was removed). 3 zones: Agir / Tableau de bord / Les moments. Uses the pure bricks (computeCellarMaturity / computeYearConsumption / computeActivityHeatmap + activityHeatmapMonths from utils/cellarInsights.ts). Sections: merged suggestions hero, "Cave à maturité" bar (clickable bands, + "Autonomie estimée" footer line = computeCellarDepletion), bento tiles, interactive smoking calendar (month ticks + tap-a-cell → date + session count → journal), "Familles", "Votre profil", "Les moments" (featured tobacco/pipe, 30 d — the "Tabac du moment" / "Pipe du moment" tile labels are enlarged, Lbl size 8→11), and an "À entretenir" pipe-maintenance reminder at the page end. Name kept as "V2" (matches the user's mental model); there is no V1 in the app.
│   │       ├── InventoryListView.tsx
│   │       ├── CatalogView.tsx          # Read-only browser of the catalogue the user loaded (the app bundles none; with no catalogue the page renders `CatalogueMissing`, which points at Réglages → Données, instead of the chunk-failure screen and its reload button). Reached via the 📖 icon in InventoryListView's TopBar. Search + category filter (chips) + brand filter (select) + group-by-brand toggle. "Already owned" / "wished" badge via tobaccoDbCanonicalKey. Tap "Ajouter à mon inventaire" opens a compact **QuickAdd modal over the catalog** (user stays in the catalog after save, never navigates to addT). Surfaces a Retry button if the dynamic chunk import fails (e.g. stale SW mid-deploy, iOS chunk-load error) — Notice tone="error" + `tobaccoDbRetry()` call. Catalog is reference data — never edited, never in backups. **The fiche is a `Modal capHeight` with the standard inner scroll shape** (`flex: 1; minHeight: 0; overflowY: auto; overscrollBehavior: contain`) — it was the last modal on the old `85vh` + `overflowY: auto` shape and it reproduced the very report `capHeight` was written for; see the `Modal.tsx` entry for the measurement and for why a `vh` cap cannot be the fix. **The fiche carries an « Aussi appelé » line** under the title, from `displayAliases(entry, brandDisplay)` — the entry's aliases minus the ones already visible in the heading. Without the subtraction the line would repeat the title on most blends. This is what makes a merged row's retired name visible to a reader rather than only to the fuzzy matcher. **The "Vous pourriez aimer" section is REMOVED** (see the `recommend.ts` note in the tree) — do not re-add it; the case in `CatalogView.test.tsx` records the reversal and asserts the section is absent even for the cellar shape that used to produce it. **The comparison also has a LABELLED entry in the page body** — a full-width row (`cmp_title` + the chart icon) between the intro Notice and the search field. The TopBar icon stays; this is a SECOND door, which is fine here and NOT a contradiction of the one-entry-point rule, because that rule protects a whole-cellar WRITE while a comparison writes nothing. It exists because the feature was reported missing TWICE by someone looking straight at it (« c'est pas possible », then « comment comparer… ? »): the bar carries FOUR unlabelled 44 px buttons, two of them adjacent on the right, and a bar-chart glyph does not say "compare". **The argument for an icon-only trigger is about SAFETY — the tap opens a confirm and writes nothing — which is a different question from FINDABILITY, and answering the first does not answer the second.** Placement is the point: above the fold, before the search, because at the bottom of a 1220-row list it would be unreachable. Verified rendered at 390 px and at 360 px in German at the "L" text size (no overflow). Locked by the « the comparison is reachable by name » block in `CatalogView.test.tsx` — whose positional case had to compare NODES, since an `indexOf` over the markup is also satisfied by the TopBar icon's `aria-label` and stayed green under probe. **The TopBar's `trailing` slot carries the bulk catalogue-apply action** (a `copy` IconBtn → `ctx.startCatalogueApply`), moved here from Settings — see `utils/catalogueApply.ts` for why, and do not add a second entry point elsewhere. The catalogue stays read-only: this action writes to the USER'S cellar, never to the catalogue. **Two defects, both about the cellar rather than the catalogue.** (a) **`addFromCatalog` was the FOURTH writer** and the most direct of the four: one tap put a catalogue `category` / `cut` straight into the cellar, where an unrepresentable value is the known defect (no `CUT_DENSITY`, no `xl()`, no `FAMILY_AGING_MAX`, no matching option in the form's fixed dropdown, so the first save rewrites it). It falls back to the TEMPLATE default (empty) rather than to `Autre`, because this is a CREATION — no personal value to protect, and the catch-all on a blank field adds nothing. **CLAUDE.md's entry said « it took THREE writers to close » and was therefore false**; corrected in place. (b) **A catalogue the user had REMOVED stayed browsable — and addable — for the rest of the session.** The missing-catalogue screen is gated on `!db`, and the load effect never set `db` back to null. `catalogueMeta` joined its deps, which is the load-bearing half: Settings is a MODAL over this view, so removing or loading a catalogue changes no other dependency here — and the same dep fixes the mirror case, where loading a DIFFERENT catalogue left the old one on screen.

│   │       ├── TastingBanner.tsx      # **La branche « en cours » est un `<button>`, et pendant longtemps c'était un `<div onClick>` que le clavier ne pouvait pas atteindre.** Elle s'affiche sur les cinq vues où la dégustation n'est pas à l'écran et se présente comme le RACCOURCI pour y revenir ; vérifié, elle ne portait ni `role`, ni `tabIndex`, ni gestionnaire clavier, et **aucun descendant focalisable** — une icône, un libellé, un chrono. Le détour par l'Accueil et son propre appel à l'action restait praticable, donc ce n'était pas une impasse : c'était un contrôle qui promet un chemin court et ne l'offre qu'à la souris. `<button>` plutôt que `role="button"` + `tabIndex` + `onKeyDown` — Entrée ET Espace viennent avec, l'anneau de focus aussi, et il n'y a rien à synchroniser ; les valeurs par défaut du navigateur sont écrasées explicitement pour que le rendu reste identique au pixel. **PAS d'`aria-label`, délibérément** : un libellé sur le conteneur REMPLACE son sous-arbre dans le nom accessible, donc on entendrait « Reprendre » au lieu de « Séance en cours 12:34 » — c'est-à-dire qu'on perdrait l'état et le chrono, la seule chose que cette bannière apporte. Son contenu EST son nom. La branche DÉPASSEMENT reste un `<div>` : elle contient déjà deux vrais boutons (« Continuer » / « Terminer ») et les convertir en un seul lui ferait perdre ce choix — asserté dans les deux sens. La ref de mesure est devenue une ref de RAPPEL, parce que `Ref<T>` est invariant en TypeScript et que les deux branches ne rendent pas la même balise. Verrouillé par le bloc « le raccourci est joignable au clavier » de `TastingBanner.test.tsx` ; sondé, le retour au `<div>` rougit 3 cas.
│   │       ├── HelpView.tsx
│   │       ├── DocPageView.tsx
│   │       ├── TermsGate.tsx           # First-run consent screen. **Its inner column is centred with `margin: "auto 0"`, NEVER `justifyContent: "center"`, and that is the whole entry.** The root is `position: fixed; inset: 0; overflow: auto`; a centred column inside a scroll container overflows its content EQUALLY in both directions the moment it is taller than the viewport, and `scrollTop` cannot go below 0 — so everything above the start edge is UNREACHABLE, not merely scrolled away. MEASURED in Chromium with `scrollTop` forced to −9999 (it stayed 0) and `elementFromPoint` returning nothing: the language toggle sat at **y = −55 at 390×844 in French — the app's own reference width** — −67 in German, −167 on an iPhone SE, and **−273 at 360×640 in German at the "L" text size**, taking the title with it. Only ≥ 412 px wide escaped. So the FIRST screen of the app opened with its top cut off on essentially every phone, and the control lost was the **language switcher** — the one thing a non-French speaker needs before anything else works, and the one a whole release was spent making cover all six languages. iOS is worse still: the safe-area inset adds to the padding below. Cross-axis `auto` margins are the fix rather than `safe center` because they need no modern keyword — per flexbox an item with an auto cross margin is NOT stretched and absorbs the free space, and when that space is NEGATIVE the auto margins compute to zero, so it is centred when it fits and flush-to-top-and-scrollable when it does not. Verified after the fix at all five phone sizes (toggle at y = +23, reachable) AND at 834×1400, where the content fits and the column is still centred to the pixel. Locked at SOURCE level by `termsGateReachable.test.tsx` (jsdom neither lays out nor scrolls), comments blanked first per the documented trap.

Mirrors the full-screen FormScreen top-bar pattern.

Opened from PipesDetailView; wires to ctx.addMaintenance / updateMaintenance / removeMaintenance. Fields: date / cleaning kind (SegmentedField over MAINT_KINDS: léger/complet/sans nettoyage) / tasks (multi-select checkbox chips over MAINT_TASKS) / notes. Only léger+complet count toward the reminder. Mirrors LotFormModal (same sticky-top-bar X+Save / scroll-body / bottom-delete layout).
│   │       ├── TrashModal.tsx             # Lazy-loaded chunk. **The ROW was reshaped**: the per-row restore action is icon-only (`Ico name="restore"`, aria-label + title keep the name) and the expiry line sits on its own full-width row. It was a TEXT button with `flexShrink: 0`, so its width was set by the translation and the label column beside it (`flex: 1, minWidth: 0`) absorbed the whole squeeze — MEASURED at 390px, German "Wiederherstellen" renders 155px (169px at the L text size), leaving the column 35px / 21px and clipping 72–84% of the item name plus 83–91% of the expiry line, on the one screen whose purpose is telling you WHAT you are about to delete for ever and WHEN it expires. Icon-only alone was not enough (0 → 124px): the row carries 180px of fixed chrome out of 326px while the date line alone needs 234px, so nothing here was a translation problem and shortening the German would not have helped (that class of fix was tried on the Italian card badge and reverted). Locked by the « TrashModal row layout » block in `TrashModal.test.tsx` — fixed 36px squares, an accessible name, and no ellipsis on either text line. Two pre-existing tests in that file selected the restore button by TEXT and silently RETARGETED to the "Tout restaurer" bulk button when the word disappeared (`toBeTruthy()` still passed; only the argument assertion caught it) — both now select on `aria-label`, which is the stable contract for an icon button.

Restock (low-stock rebuys) + wishlist via computeShoppingList, each row a persisted "got it" checkbox (`cave-shopping-checked`), a "Copier la liste" clipboard button, and tap-to-open (restock → tobacco fiche via crossOpenDetail, wish → the wishlist). Opened from the cart icon in the InventoryListView TopBar (shown only when shoppingCount > 0). Toggled by `ctx.shoppingOpen` / `ctx.setShoppingOpen`.
│   │       ├── CompareModal.tsx          # The side-by-side blend comparison (2-3 columns), rendered by `CatalogView` and reached from its TopBar (`chart` icon) or from the bottom of a blend's fiche. **It lives on the CATALOGUE page for two reasons, not one of convenience**: the question it answers is « est-ce que j'achète celui-là plutôt que celui que j'ai déjà ? », so it belongs where you browse blends you do NOT own; and the catalogue chunk is already loaded there, so offering catalogue blends as columns costs no extra fetch — the cold-start gating rule forbids pulling it anywhere else, which is also why the modal is mounted inside that view rather than in `CuratorApp`. The picker searches **all three sources at once** (your cellar, your wishlist, the catalogue) because splitting it into tabs would make the user answer "where is this blend?" before they can answer "how do these two compare?". The catalogue is only searched from **2 characters** and capped at 40 hits (~1200 rows would otherwise be a wall). Source labels come from an explicit `SRC_KEY` table, never a concatenated key — a built key is invisible to doc:check's "exists in every dictionary" gate and a missing one renders the RAW KEY on screen (the defect). **And that warning was written HERE while the same defect shipped one file over.** `compareBlends.ts`'s `ROWS` table invented `lbl_category` and `lbl_rating`, which exist in NO dictionary, so the comparison rendered **`LBL_CATEGORY` as its FIRST ROW** and `LBL_RATING` as the first experience row. The warning above had been applied to the SOURCE labels (which duly got a table) while the fourteen ROW labels were assumed to exist because they read like generic names: **a table is not the guarantee — resolving it against the real dictionaries is.** Gate 9 cannot see them (it matches a literal `t("…")`, and these are data), so `compareBlends.test.ts` now walks every row × every `LANGUAGES` code through the real `translate()` and fails when a label comes back equal to its own key. The two are now `lbl_type` / `lbl_rating_lbl` — the keys the FORMS already use for the same two fields, so the comparison speaks the app's vocabulary instead of a private synonym. Probed: restoring the invented keys names the offending row and language. **The rest of the class was swept** — every other i18n key the UI holds in DATA rather than in a literal `t("…")`: the four `*_FAMILIES` tables' `labelKey` fields and, riskiest of all, `SHAPE_FAMILIES`, whose key is not stored at all but BUILT (`"shape_family_" + f.key`) in `PipeFormView` and `PipesListView`, so a reader of `constants.ts` sees no i18n key there to check. All 23 resolve in all six languages, so `dynamicGroupLabels.test.ts` is the net written before the fall rather than after it; its third case asserts the PREFIX still reaches both views, since the resolution case derives that prefix from the same literal it guards and would stay green through a rename. Probed in both directions. **When you add a table of i18n keys — or concatenate one — resolve it against the real dictionaries in the same commit.** **Found by RENDERING the feature in a browser, not by reading it** — the mechanism worked perfectly and every test was green; what was wrong was only visible on screen, which is the same argument the `AICard` note makes. Two of the three "failures" chased on the way there were the PROBE selecting elements *behind* the modal (the catalogue list and the page's own search box also match `[role=button]` / `input`): when driving a modal, scope to `getByRole("dialog")` or you are measuring the page underneath. The engine is `utils/compareBlends.ts`; the view's own contract — `null` renders as "—" and never as 0, a row no column can answer is dropped, a differing row is marked, and the empty experience half is explained ONCE — is locked by `CompareModal.test.tsx`.
│   │       ├── EncryptionPromptModal.tsx  # Drive passphrase prompt
│   │       ├── Overlays.tsx               # DelConfirm, save-error, save-warn, update pill, auto-update countdown, just-updated toast, CuratorLangDetectedToast. **`CuratorUndoToast`'s overline must come from the KIND, and an unknown kind must say NOTHING.** This toast was written for deletes only, so the verb was the literal `t("lbl_deleted")` and the kind fell back to `t(undoToast.kind)`. A non-delete was then pushed through it — the bulk catalogue pass — and the app announced « CATALOGUE · SUPPRIMÉ » for an update, on a toast whose only button says « Annuler »: the one reading a user must not be given after applying a change to their whole cellar. Reported from the app. **The kind half was wrong too and looked right BY ACCIDENT**: `catalogue` is not a dictionary key, so `translate` fell back to the raw key and the screen rendered the internal kind NAME — which happens to be a French word. In German the same line reads « CATALOGUE · GELÖSCHT ». Same class as the `dict[k] || k` defect, where a fallback made a bug invisible. One module-level `UNDO_KIND` table now gives both halves (kind → `{kind, verb}` i18n keys), and **an unknown kind renders NO overline rather than a guessed one** — a future undoable action added without a row loses information, never states something false, and `restoreFn` still works. New keys `kind_catalogue` + `lbl_updated` (six languages). **The label is a SUBJECT, not a sentence**: that slot is `maxWidth: 240` + nowrap + ellipsis because a deleted item's name can be any length, and a full sentence was put there, so the user read « 29 fiche(s) mises à jour depui… » — `cat_apply_undo_label` is now just `{n} fiche(s)`, and a test caps every language's rendered length. The slot was right; the label was the wrong shape for it. The per-kind verb slot also makes the long-standing French agreement fixable (« Pipe · supprimé », « Séance · supprimé » have shipped because there was only ever one verb) — deliberately NOT done here, since it is a shipped-wording change in five languages and does not belong in a bug fix. Locked by the « UndoToast » block in `Overlays.test.tsx`, which passes a REAL `t()` (the shared harness's `mockT` returns the key, so under it the defect is invisible — "lbl_deleted" contains neither the French nor the German word, which is why the older cases in that file accept `/supprimé|lbl_deleted/`) and checks all six languages, since French alone passes on the raw-key fallback too. Probed in three directions.
│   │       ├── WelcomeModal.tsx           # One-shot welcome modal on first Curator open
│   │       └── StartupNoticeModal.tsx     # On-demand broadcast pop-up driven by public/notice.json
│   │
│   └── __tests__/        # Vitest + React Testing Library tests
│       ├── setup.ts
│       ├── drainScheduler.ts   # Lets React finish its queued work BEFORE Vitest deletes `window`. React's scheduler yields through Node's `setImmediate` (MEASURED: under jsdom it is Node's, exposed on the window too), which the jsdom teardown cannot cancel — so a concurrent render that yielded at least once can have a slice in flight when the environment dies between files, and it lands as an UNHANDLED `ReferenceError: window is not defined` **while every test reports as passed**. Exit 1 on a green suite. Wired as `afterAll(() => drainSchedulerQueue())` in `setup.ts` — `afterAll` because RTL unmounts on `afterEach` and unmounting itself schedules work, and the ARROW because Vitest passes hook callbacks a suite context that would otherwise land in `maxTurns`. Bounded on purpose (a runaway chain shortens the drain instead of hanging the suite); ~0.2 ms per file, and the full suite measured 148 s against 149 s before. **Do NOT add a second, local flush in a test file** — one was tried in `forgedImageUrl.test.ts` and it was the wrong scope AND carried a false reason (« RTL's auto-cleanup runs after this file » — it runs after each TEST); the mechanism is React's, so it belongs to every render. **THE DRAIN IS A MITIGATION AND THE RACE STAYED OPEN; WHAT CLOSES IT IS A GUARD IN THE WRAPPER.** This entry used to end « RESIDUAL, INTERMITTENT, disclosed: the drain does not always win … on a green suite that exits 1 with this signature, re-run before chasing it » — accurate, and exactly the shape of note that stops the next reader looking. A drain can only remove PRECONDITIONS: it is BOUNDED by design (a runaway chain must shorten it, never hang the suite), and work scheduled on a delay longer than the one tick `settleReactWork` spends is queued strictly after it. So a fully green suite went on exiting 1 at roughly one run in five. **The fix came from a detail of the reported stack nobody had used: the wrapper's own callback sits ONE FRAME above the crash** (`Immediate.<anonymous>` at `drainScheduler.ts`), so the leaked slice runs through code this module owns and can be stopped when it fires instead of raced to the exit. Each wrapped immediate records whether a `window` existed when it was queued and compares that when it runs; if the environment died in between, the callback is dropped and counted (`suppressedAfterTeardown`). **NOTHING OBSERVABLE IS LOST and it is NOT error suppression** — a callback firing after `window` is deleted belongs to a file that has ended, so no assertion can depend on it and running it yields a `ReferenceError` and an exit code, never a result; a genuine defect runs inside a live file, with a window, untouched. The condition is deliberately « the environment DIED », not « there is no window », so a runtime that never had one is unaffected. **The drain STAYS**: it is what lets legitimately-queued work FINISH for the file that owns it, and the guard only catches what it could not reach. **The probe is the evidence, and it is the strongest kind available here**: deleting the guard turns a one-in-five race into a deterministic failure with the identical three-frame stack — `ReferenceError` → `performWorkOnRootViaSchedulerTask` → `performWorkUntilDeadline` → this wrapper — reproduced by a case that renders a real concurrent root outside `act()` and then deletes the global under it. **DISCLOSED, and stated so nobody assumes otherwise:** React work rescheduled through `setTimeout` rather than `setImmediate` would crash the same way one frame lower, outside this wrapper. Wrapping timers too was REJECTED — every fake-timer test in the suite replaces them, so the probe would be installed over and torn out at random — and the observed signature has always taken the immediate path, which is the one React's scheduler picks when `setImmediate` exists. **ITS OWN PROBE CASE WAS FLAKY, AND THE FAILURE MESSAGE ACCUSED THE WRONG THING.** The case renders a real concurrent root, deletes `window`, and asserted after a FIXED 2 ms wait; observed once during a full suite run beside a build on a 4 vCPU box, it failed with « the React slice was let through: expected 2 to be 3 » — which reads as the guard failing and is the opposite: the count did not MOVE, i.e. the event loop had not reached the queued immediate before `finally` put the window back, so the slice ran into a live environment and there was nothing to suppress. **The diagnosis is MEASURED, not inferred** — shrinking the window to zero reproduces the identical message and the identical numbers, and 6 busy-loop CPU hogs do NOT reproduce it (a synthetic load starves arithmetic, not the event loop), so the honest statement is that the flake was seen once, under contention I created, and that the mechanism behind it is demonstrable on demand. The wait is now BOUNDED ON THE CONDITION rather than on a delay — the same rule this file states for `settle(page)`, *a fix is a CONDITION and not a longer sleep* — with the assertion untouched, so it is no weaker as a probe: with the guard deleted the case still fails AND the `ReferenceError` still surfaces (verified). **The general lesson is about the MESSAGE: an assertion that reads a counter cannot tell « the thing under test failed » from « the thing under test never ran », and it will confidently report the first.**
│       ├── viewTestUtils.tsx   # renderWithCtx() helper
│       ├── curator/            # Curator-specific tests
│       └── *.test.tsx / *.test.ts
│
└── public/                 # Static assets (copied as-is to dist/)
    ├── sw.js               # Service Worker (current cache name `cave-tabac-v1-0-1`)
    ├── manifest.json       # PWA manifest
    ├── version.json        # {"version":"1.3","build":"…"} — used by checkVersion()
    ├── help.html           # User guide (one `sec-<code>` block + `<code>-…` h2 anchors per UI language — fr/en/es/de/it/pt). Its in-page toggle DERIVES the code list from the `[id^="sec-"]` blocks actually present (`codes()` in the inline script), so authoring the section is all a new language needs here.
    ├── changelog.html      # Current major version changelog
    ├── privacy.html        # Privacy policy
    ├── licenses.html       # Third-party licenses (network-first)
    ├── reset.html          # The far end of index.html's « Rien ne se passe ? Réparer l'application → » link: unregister every SW, delete every cache, reload. **It REFUSES when the app cannot be re-fetched, and that guard is the entry.** It purged unconditionally — and it is reached when a load has ALREADY failed, whose commonest cause is the network rather than a corrupt cache, so on a bad connection it destroyed the offline copy that was the user's only remaining way in and reloaded into nothing. Reported from the installed iOS PWA (« même si je clique sur réparer »), during a mid-deploy CDN window. **`doUpdate` was given the same guard for the same reason** (*offline, an unvisited lazy chunk fails as a module-load error, so tapping Stats on a plane destroyed the installed app*); this page never got it — the sibling-page miss, again. **`cache: 'no-store'` is what makes the probe trustworthy**: `sw.js`'s third fetch guard returns early for such a request, so it genuinely reaches the network instead of being answered by the very cache it is deciding whether to destroy. `navigator.onLine` is only a cheap short-circuit — it reports that an interface exists, not that the internet works — so the probe ALSO reads the body for `id="root"`, because a captive portal answers 200 with its own page and `res.ok` alone would wave it through. **The strictness is settled by the asymmetry**: a probe that wrongly FAILS costs one tap on « Réessayer » and deletes nothing, while one that wrongly PASSES strands the user — so it errs toward refusing, and offers a retry plus a way back rather than a dead end. Locked by `resetPageGuard.test.ts`, which EXECUTES the page's own inline script against a stubbed world (a source assertion would pass on a guard that exists and is never reached) and probes in both directions, including that the guard is not a blanket refusal. Same file also carries the two defects this page keeps producing because it sits outside doc:check's source tree: a per-language row per `LANGUAGES` code (the `pt` row was once missing on the ONE page you reach when nothing works) — read from the EVALUATED table, since a first version counted quote characters and got 5.5, the fr/it rows using double quotes precisely BECAUSE they contain apostrophes — and a `lang` attribute VALIDATED against that table (it was written verbatim, so a corrupt `cave-lang` produced `lang="constructor"`; main.jsx was fixed for this and its sibling was missed).

        ├── dm-sans-var.woff2
        ├── newsreader-var.woff2
        ├── newsreader-italic-var.woff2
        └── ibm-plex-mono-{400,500,600,700}.woff2
```

## Key Data Structures

All application state is serialized as JSON and stored under the localStorage key `pipe-cellar-v6`.

**Primary-key invariant**: every top-level entity (tobacco, pipe, accessory, session, wishlist item, lot) is keyed by a numeric `id` assigned by the app (auto-incremented counters `data.nxT` / `nxP` / `nxA` / `nxJ` / `nxW`, or `Date.now()` for lots). **No user-entered field is ever used as a key** — names, brands, descriptions, etc. are mutable and user-facing, never identity. Update functions (`updateTobacco`, `updatePipe`, etc.) always match by `id`, never by display fields. If you add a new entity type, follow this pattern: auto-assigned numeric id, mutable user fields keyed off it. **This extends to LOTS (integrity fix):** `updateLotInTobacco` / `removeLot` / `changeLotStatus` locate the target lot by its stable `id`, NOT by a positional array index — because views hold the trash-STRIPPED `liveData` lots while the store mutates the RAW (trash-inclusive) `data.tobaccos[].lots`, so a view-computed index points at the wrong lot when a soft-deleted lot precedes a live one (silent corruption + duplicate ids). A `lot-id-unique` runtime invariant (`lotInvariants.ts`) now flags any duplicate lot id within a tobacco at `save()`. Never reintroduce index-based lot mutation.

### Tobacco Entry (`BT` template)
```js
{
  name, brand, category, blend, cut,
  force,        // 0–5 (nicotine strength)
  roomNote,     // 0–5 (room note intensity)
  taste,        // 0–5 (flavour intensity)
  rating,       // 0–5 (personal rating)
  rebuy,        // null | true | false
  tastingNotes, // free text
  description,  // free text
  imageUrl,     // "local-photo-{timestamp}" (IndexedDB key) or external URL
  lots,         // array of Lot objects
  agingMax,     // string (years, optional) — max recommended cellar age; "" if unset
  tags,         // string[] (optional) — user-defined collections ("voyage", "cadeaux"…). Sanitised by migrateData (sanitizeTags: trim/dedup/cap). Personal grouping axis, NEVER an identity key.
  catalogueLock // boolean (optional) — the user pinned this fiche against the BULK catalogue pass; `planCatalogueApply` skips it BEFORE the lookup. Absent === false. Strict boolean on load (`=== true`, not `!!` — a forged "no" is truthy). STICKY across a merge: a lock propagates, an unlock does not. Carried through wishToInv. NOT a CSV column.
}
```

### Lot / Batch (`BL` template)
```js
{
  uid,            // string (optional on legacy) — stable cross-device
                  // identity (crypto.randomUUID), minted ONCE at creation,
                  // distinct from the per-device numeric `id`. Powers uid-first
                  // lot merge (two identical-acquisition tins stay distinct).
                  // Backfilled by migrateData; NOT in the BL template (minted in
                  // useTobaccoStore, one per bulk clone; makeLotDuplicate strips it).
                  // CARRIED VERBATIM in JSON / Drive / Dropbox / ZIP backups (the
                  // data blob is serialized as-is). NOT in the CSV export/import
                  // (no uid column) — a CSV round-trip re-mints the uid; CSV
                  // re-import stays idempotent via lotMergeKey, not uid.
  status,         // "cellar" | "jar" | "finished"
  originalStatus, // "cellar" | "jar" — how the lot first entered the
                  // inventory. Set once at creation (mirrors `status`
                  // except finished→cellar fallback). Migration of
                  // legacy lots infers from dateOpened presence. Kept
                  // on the record for future migrations / analytics, but
                  // no longer drives any auto-status transition (the
                  // jar→cellar auto-revert was removed).
  weightG,        // string (grams or oz per user pref) — current balance
  weightInitial,  // string — recorded original weight; seeded from weightG
                  // at creation, back-filled via migrateData for legacy
                  // lots. Used only for display (LotRow "/ Xg initial"
                  // annotation) and the cellar/jar age recap — never to
                  // trigger a status change.
  datePurchased,
  dateProduction, // MONTH precision, free text "YYYY-MM" — a `type="text"` field
                  // with a `2017-09` placeholder, NOT a date picker. A tin is
                  // stamped with a month, not a day, and a picker would force
                  // the user to invent one. It is the PIPE's production date
                  // that is year-only; these two are different fields with
                  // different precisions, which is worth knowing before
                  // "unifying" them.
  dateOpened,     // auto-filled when status → "jar"
  dateFinished,   // auto-filled when status → "finished"
  boxNumber,
  storageLocation, // string (optional on legacy lots) — free-text
                   // physical location ("Armoire A · étagère 2"). Shown on
                   // LotRow + lot detail modal, exported to CSV, matched by
                   // the inventory search (along with boxNumber).
  price,
  seller, sellerUrl,   // sellerUrl: optional seller website URL
  disposed        // boolean (default false) — true = lot was thrown/given away unsmoked. Only the "Lots finis" counter excludes disposed lots; the per-session weight (and therefore the monthly consumption chart) is still counted because those sessions were genuinely smoked at the time. **Disposing a lot NO LONGER touches the parent tobacco's `rebuy` flag. "À ne pas reprendre" (`rebuy = false`) is a deliberate, MANUAL per-tobacco judgement — a blend you didn't like and won't rebuy — NOT a mechanical consequence of physically eliminating a tin (you can toss a moldy tin of a blend you love). The earlier auto-set-on-disposal behaviour was reverted; only the manual rebuy toggle on the fiche sets it. The disposed flag still marks the lot (Éliminés filter). The tobacco card shows the shared `FINI` pill for a disposed lot (it's status:"finished") — a separate `ÉLIMINÉ` pill was REVERTED per user preference (finished + disposed both read FINI).**
}
```

### Pipe (`BP` template)
```js
{
  name, brand, shape, courbure,
  length,               // stored in whatever unit the user typed (mm or in per global lengthUnit)
  weight,               // stored in whatever unit the user typed (g or oz per global weightUnit)
  filterType,
  chamberDiameter,      // mm
  chamberDepth,         // mm
  bowlMaterial, stemMaterial,
  finish,               // one of FINISHES (Lisse/Rustiquée/Sablée/Teintée/Autre); "" if unset (v1.1 b14-15)
  datePurchased,        // YEAR only ("YYYY") since v1.1 b13 — migrated from full ISO
  dateProduction,       // YEAR only ("YYYY") since v1.1 b13
  price, seller, sellerUrl,   // sellerUrl optional
  description, notes,   // notes = personal/private notes
  imageUrl,             // "local-photo-{timestamp}" (IndexedDB key) — the COVER photo, resolved eagerly into global imgLocal on load
  photos,               // string[] (PIPES ONLY) — additional local-photo-* keys (max 6). Loaded ON DEMAND (never into the global imgLocal) — the fiche gallery / lightbox resolve each key from IndexedDB when opened, so a large pipe collection stays cheap to load. Included in backups (gatherLocalImages walks it) and protected from orphan-GC (referenced set walks it).
  rating,  // 0–5
  status,  // "active" | "finished"
  maintenance, // MaintEntry[] — pipe maintenance log ("Carnet d'entretien"). Embedded like tobacco.lots, so it rides along in every backup. Optional on legacy pipes; migrateData defaults it to [].
  tags         // string[] (optional) — user collections, same as Tobacco.tags (sanitizeTags on load).
}
```

### Maintenance Entry (`MaintEntry`)
```js
{
  id,     // numeric, Date.now() at creation (never a user field)
  uid,    // string (optional on legacy) — stable cross-device identity (crypto.randomUUID), minted in usePipeStore.addMaintenance, backfilled by migrateData. The maintenance-entry analogue of the entity/lot uid.
  date,   // "YYYY-MM-DD"
  time,   // optional "HH:MM". EMPTY on every legacy entry and clearable by hand, which is why the absence has a defined meaning rather than being a hole: an untimed cleaning reads as NOON, the same fallback `sessionStartMs` applies to an untimed session, so both sides of the reminder comparison agree on what a missing time means. It exists because the counter compares MOMENTS, not dates — a pipe cleaned and smoked again the same day used to count zero sessions since its cleaning and drop out of the reminders entirely (reported at a threshold of 1, where that is the whole feature). Labelled `maint_time_label` ("Heure"), NOT the session form's `lbl_time` ("Heure de début"): a cleaning is an event, it has a time, it does not start. Carried in every JSON / cloud / ZIP backup, since it is part of the pipe.
  kind,   // "light" | "full" | "none" — cleaning intensity; ONLY "light"/"full" feed the reminder counter (see pipeMaint.isCleaningMaint). "none" = an intervention (repair/wax) logged WITHOUT resetting the reminder. Labels via t("maint_kind_<k>").
  tasks,  // string[] of MAINT_TASKS keys checked (swab/bowl/ream/saltalcohol/stem/wax/repair/rest/other) — descriptive only, never affect the counter. Labels via t("maint_task_<k>").
  notes   // free text
}
```
**The reshape.** Earlier releases stored a single `type` (canonical FR from the old `MAINT_TYPES` enum); it was split into a cleaning `kind` (radio: léger / complet / sans nettoyage) + a multi-select `tasks` checklist. `MAINT_TYPES` + its `_EN/_ES/_DE/_IT` maps + its `ENUM_TRANSLATIONS` row are GONE — kind/task labels resolve via plain `t()` keys (`maint_kind_*` / `maint_task_*`), NOT the `xl()` enum-translation path. `constants.ts` now exports `MAINT_KINDS`, `MAINT_TASKS`, and `MAINT_LEGACY_MAP` (the type→{kind,tasks} migration table). **Reminder rule**: only `kind` `light`/`full` reset the "à entretenir" counter — `pipeMaint.lastMaintDate` filters on `isCleaningMaint`, so a `none` entry never counts. **Migration**: `migrateData` maps every legacy `type` to a COUNTING kind (Nettoyage→light, Alcool+sel/Alésage→full, Cire/Réparation/Autre→light) + the matching task, so no pipe becomes retroactively overdue; unknown kinds normalize to `light`, unknown task keys are dropped, `type` is deleted.

Lives inside `pipe.maintenance`. CRUD via `usePipeStore` (`addMaintenance` / `updateMaintenance` / `removeMaintenance`), rendered as the "Carnet d'entretien" section on `PipesDetailView` (newest first: kind badge + task pills + notes) + edited through `MaintFormModal` (kind SegmentedField + task checkbox chips + notes). Hard-delete (no soft-delete/trash — a log entry is minor). Included in JSON/Drive/ZIP backups (it's part of the pipe); NOT in the CSV working snapshot. **That hard delete STANDS, and the SILENCE around it did not.** This was the app's only irreversible one-tap delete: every other delete is a soft-delete + an 8 s undo toast + the 30-day trash, while `removeMaintenance` hard-filters the entry out, was never wrapped in `withUndo`, and `MaintFormModal` fires it straight from the bottom bar with **no confirm**. The sentence above justifies the hard delete and is still right — a `deletedAt` + trash entry for a log row would be machinery out of proportion — but *"minor"* was doing double duty: it answered "does this need a trash?" and was then read as answering "does this need FEEDBACK?", and the entry carries a free-text `notes` field. So the one delete you cannot get back was also the only one that said nothing. `withUndo` snapshots the whole cellar and restores it wholesale, so it needs nothing from the store, which is why this is the cheap fix rather than a second delete model: `removeMaintenanceU = withUndo(removeMaintenance, "maintenance", _maintLabel)` and **the ctx key keeps its name** (`removeMaintenance: removeMaintenanceU`), so no view changed. It needed an `UNDO_KIND` row too (`kind_maintenance` / `lbl_deleted`, six languages): an unknown kind renders NO overline rather than a guess, so without the row this new undoable action would have lost its label **silently** — the exact trap that entry was written for, sprung by the next person to add one. `_maintLabel` follows the other rule — that slot is a SUBJECT (`maxWidth: 240`, nowrap, ellipsis), never a sentence — so it is `date · pipe`, like `_sessLabel`. Locked by `maintenanceUndo.test.tsx`; probed by unwiring the ctx key, which reddens the case asserting the WRAPPED variant is what views get (the wrapper existing is not the fix — being reachable is).

### Wishlist Item (`BW` template)
```js
{
  name, brand, category, blend, cut,
  force, roomNote, taste,  // 0–5
  description,
  agingMax,      // string (years, optional) — max recommended cellar age; "" if unset
  tastingNotes,
  imageUrl,
  notes,         // personal note (shown last in form and on card)
  priority,      // "medium" | ...
  catalogueLock  // boolean (optional) — see Tobacco.catalogueLock. Same field, same scope, same sticky merge rule.
}
```

### Accessory (`BA` template)
```js
{
  name, brand, type,   // type from ACC_TYPES
  fuel,                // for lighters
  datePurchased,       // YEAR only ("YYYY") since v1.1 b13 — migrated from full ISO
  price, seller, sellerUrl,   // sellerUrl optional
  imageUrl,
  rating,  // 0–5
  notes,
  status,  // "active" | "retired"
  tags     // string[] (optional) — user collections, same as Tobacco.tags (sanitizeTags on load).
}
```

### Session (`BJ` template)
```js
{
  date,
  time,               // v1.3: optional "HH:MM" start time (empty on legacy / retro-dated sessions). Orders same-day sessions in the journal (newest first: date desc → time desc → id desc; untimed rows sort to the bottom of their day). Auto-filled to the tasting start time on a live tasting, to the current local time on a fresh manual "+", editable in the form. Included in CSV export.
  tobaccoId, pipeId,  // references by id
  duration,           // minutes
  rating,             // 0–5
  notes,
  weightG,            // string — tobacco weight consumed (in user's preferred unit, default from user-configurable sessDefaultWeight setting)
  lotId,              // string — id of the lot deducted (lot.id from tobacco.lots[])
  aromas,             // string[] (optional) — tapped aroma-wheel descriptor keys (see src/utils/aromas.ts). Structured complement to `notes`, aggregated into the Stats "Profil gustatif". Sanitised to known keys by migrateData.
  updatedAt           // string (optional) — ISO edit timestamp stamped by useSessionStore on every add/edit. Drives multi-device merge LAST-WRITE-WINS in useImportConfirm: when BOTH the local + imported dup carry it AND the imported is strictly newer, its NON-KEY optional fields (notes/rating/lat/lng/location*/aromas) OVERWRITE the local ones. Absent on legacy sessions → falls back to fill-if-empty (never clobbers a pre-feature local edit). NEVER a key; weightG/lotId/duration/time are untouched (balance/identity).
}
```

## Enumerations

All enumerations live in `src/constants.ts` as `export var` (TypeScript `as const`).

```ts
CATS = ["Américain","Anglais","Aromatique","Balkan","Burley","Cavendish","Cigare","Dark Fired",
        "Écossais","Lakeland","Latakia","Oriental","Perique","Turkish",
        "VaPer","Virginia","Virginia/Burley","Virginia/Latakia","Autre"]
// "Anglais aromatique" added (20 values) — the English
// AROMATIC, i.e. a Latakia-bearing blend carrying a declared
// topping. Not a coinage: Smokingpipes runs a standing
// "English Aromatics" category and The Country Squire labels products
// "(English Aromatic)", so `CAT_MAP` accepts those spellings plus
// "Aromatic English" / "English/Aromatic" — the reviewer reads English
// product pages. The gap had already been MEASURED from the other
// side: 13 rows sat in `Aromatique` with Latakia in their `blend` column
// and were read closely and judged CORRECT under Family-prime, with the
// note that "the aromatic-English is a real genre". This is that genre
// getting its own value instead of borrowing one.
//   FAMILY: `cat_fam_aromatic`, per Family-prime (a declared topping wins
//   over the leaf family) and the Lakeland precedent. The counter-argument
//   is real and was weighed: the value is NAMED "Anglais aromatique", so a
//   reader scanning the grouped dropdown looks for it under
//   "Anglais & Latakia". It lost because the written doctrine beats an
//   ergonomic hunch, and because the risk is one-directional — a
//   topping-averse smoker scanning the English group could pick it by
//   mistake, whereas the word "aromatique" is in the value itself wherever
//   it is filed. Do not re-litigate without a user report.
//   COLOUR: do NOT read taxonomy into the hue. `CAT_COLORS` is a rotation
//   of distinguishable hues, not a semantic scheme (Burley is purple,
//   Cigare periwinkle), so the hue was picked on SEPARATION alone — see the
//   theme-curator note for the measurement and the candidate that failed.
// "Lakeland" added — the Kendal floral style
// (rose / geranium / tonquin top dressing, Samuel Gawith + Gawith Hoggarth).
// Added ON REQUEST for manual entry, not because the catalogue needed it: no
// row carries the label, so the usual test ("does it name an object the
// app cannot represent?") could not be answered from the data. It sits in
// `cat_fam_aromatic` because it is defined by a TOPPING, and its
// FAMILY_AGING_MAX is "5-10" — REASONED, not calibrated: the essence
// demonstrably fades over the years (old tins are described that way) so it
// cannot take Virginia's 15-25, while a pressed leaf-forward style is nothing
// like a Danish aromatic's 3. Sources: smokingpipes "A Love for Lakelands",
// the Oklahoma Briar Society on the essence. English keeps the proper noun,
// so it is an IDENTITY entry in CATS_EN and correctly ABSENT from the four
// sparse maps (doc:check gate 16).
// "Américain", "Virginia/Latakia", "Cigare" added. Adding a
// CATEGORY is never a one-line change — it touches SIX sites, and two gates
// exist because of it: CAT_FAMILIES must partition the enum EXACTLY
// (constants.test.ts), and CAT_COLORS needs a `--c-cat-<slug>` entry with a
// MODE_LIGHT override listed in THEME_VARS (catColorsLight.test.ts) or the
// label is unreadable on parchment. Also FAMILY_AGING_MAX (utils.ts).
// "Virginia/Latakia" is deliberately absent from CATS_ES/DE/IT/PT: English
// leaves it untouched, so it is jargon, and doc:check gate 16 REQUIRES the
// four sparse maps to omit it — the same rule that requires "Américain"
// (which English translates to "American") to be present in all four.
// The MAPPING was also fixed, which is where the cost was hiding: CAT_MAP
// in the Node checker (since deleted) sent "Cigare" -> "Autre", so 14 rows
// that had carried `category=Cigare` all along were shipping to users as
// "Autre". Making the category real fixed all 14 at once, and "Autre" fell from
// 15 rows to 1. CAT_MAP holds ALIASES onto canonical values — and they are not only ENGLISH ones. The row that first justified it ("Cigar", "Cigar Leaf", "American") came from a reviewer reading English product pages, but the map also folds FRENCH spellings the app itself does not store: `Périque` → `Perique`, `Americain` → `Américain`, `Anglais Aromatique` → `Anglais aromatique`. An accent or a capital is exactly what a hand-typed CSV gets wrong, so the map is the import contract for BOTH directions, not a translation table in one.
// And `scripts/validate-tobacco-db.cjs` no longer COPIES the enums: it kept a
// hand-mirrored CATS under a comment reading "must mirror src/constants.ts",
// which duly stopped mirroring it — adding Cigare made the validator reject 14
// rows the app accepts. It now parses CATS/CUTS out of constants.ts with a
// non-vacuity guard, the way the checker already did. A comment asking
// a human to keep two lists in step is not a mechanism.

CUTS = ["Broken Flake","Coarse Cut","Coins","Crumble Cake","Cube Cut","Curly Cut",
        "Flake","Loose Cut","Plug","Pressed","Ready Rubbed","Ribbon",
        "Rope","Rough Cut","Shag","Sliced","Twist","Autre"]
// "Twist" added, and its `CUT_MAP` alias onto `Rope` removed.
// Same construction as a rope — leaves layered and spun tight — which is an
// argument for the same DENSITY (0.36, the table's highest, shared with Rope)
// and NOT for the same name: the trade sells them as two products, and the
// fold left a twist unnameable in the entry form. Added on request; no
// catalogue row carries the label today.

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// CAT_MAP / CUT_MAP — the IMPORT CONTRACT, alongside the two
// enums above: the trade / retailer labels a source may write (`Cigar`,
// `Navy Cut`, `Krumble Kake`, `English Aromatic`), mapped onto the canonical
// value. They MOVED HERE from the Node checker, which the browser
// bundle cannot import, because a THIRD consumer appeared: the browser now
// normalises a catalogue CSV the user loads themselves, and it has to agree
// with the Node importer exactly. A TS copy beside the .cjs one is the drift
// this repo has paid for three times, so the checker PARSED them back out of
// this file rather than copying them — the mechanism it had used for
// CATS/CUTS — with a FATAL non-vacuity guard, because build
// 168's lesson is that a parse degrading to `{}` makes every check below pass
// while examining nothing. `enumMapsSingleSource.test.ts` asserts the two
// sides are observably EQUAL, that no literal copy has returned to the
// checker, that every map TARGET is canonical (the rule: an entry
// pointing at a non-canonical value launders a bad label past the gate it
// feeds), and that no entry shadows an already-canonical value.
//
// BOTH ARE NULL-PROTOTYPE, and here that is correctness, not house style: the
// key now comes from a USER-SUPPLIED FILE, so on a plain object a row carrying
// `category: "constructor"` resolves to a member of `Object.prototype` —
// truthy, not a category — defeating the `MAP[c] || c` fallback that
// `mapCategory` / `mapCut` are built on. An unknown label is returned VERBATIM
// so the caller can REPORT it: the catalogue gate refuses it, the browser
// importer names it to the user.
//
// **`canonCategory` / `canonCut` sit beside them and answer
// the OTHER question.** `mapCategory` / `mapCut` normalise and hand back an
// unknown label verbatim, which is right for a checker that must REPORT it.
// A writer needs to know whether the CELLAR can store the value at all, so
// these return the canonical value or **null** — fold-tolerant, because the
// catalogue is hand-filled, while the `map*` pair stays exact because the
// catalogue GATE depends on exactness. See the helpers for why null and not
// `Autre`, and `catalogueValueNotRepresentable.test.ts` for the three writers
// that needed them.

SHAPES = ["Acorn","Apple","Ball","Barrel","Bent Billiard","Billiard", ...]

BENDS = ["Droite","Semi-courbée","Courbée"]
FILTERS = ["9mm","6mm","Balsa","Métal","Hybride 6mm","Hybride 9mm","Autre"]  // + "Aucun" option in UI (Métal + the two Hybride sizes were added later)
BOWL_MATS = [...]
STEM_MATS = [...]
FINISHES = ["Lisse","Rustiquée","Sablée","Teintée","Autre"]  // pipe surface finish (v1.1 b14-15)
ACC_TYPES = ["Briquet","Blague à tabac","Bourre-pipe","Porte-pipe","Autre"]
LIGHTER_FUELS = ["Gaz","Essence","Électrique","Allumettes","Autre"]
MAINT_KINDS = ["light","full","none"]  // cleaning intensity of a maintenance entry; labels via t("maint_kind_<k>"). Only light/full feed the reminder counter.
MAINT_TASKS = ["swab","bowl","ream","saltalcohol","stem","wax","repair","rest","other"]  // checkable tasks; labels via t("maint_task_<k>"). Descriptive only.
// (The old MAINT_TYPES enum + its _EN/_ES/_DE/_IT + ENUM_TRANSLATIONS row were removed — kind/task labels are plain i18n keys, not xl(). The legacy type→{kind,tasks} migration table is inlined in migrateData.)
```

Each enumeration has a corresponding `_EN` variant (e.g. `CATS_EN`, `CUTS_EN`, `SHAPES_EN`, `BENDS_EN`, `ACC_TYPES_EN`, `FINISHES_EN`) used for the English UI translation.

## Application Views / Router

The app uses a custom `view` state string as a client-side router. No URL changes.
All views live under `src/views/curator/` and access ctx via `useAppCtx()`. Each view returns `null` when its `view` key (or required sub-state) doesn't match — `CuratorApp` mounts every view simultaneously and only one is visible at a time.

| View key | Component | Notes |
|----------|-----------|-------|
| `home`   | `CuratorHomeViewV2` | Dashboard (sole Home layout; classic HomeView removed). **Featured pipe / featured tobacco** = most-used over the **last 30 days**. Ties broken by highest rating, then by oldest record. With zero recent sessions, rotates among ALL active candidates (see next). **The top tie-group rotates every 12 h** (was a calendar day) — both featured picks call `pickDailyTie(..., FEATURE_ROTATE_MS)` (12 h, exported from `utils/suggest.ts`) so a tied set alternates twice a day. **The tie-key is the recent-USE COUNT ONLY** (rating dropped from it — it still ORDERS the sorted list so rotation starts from the best-rated). Before, the count+rating key made the common "no recent sessions" case (every candidate at count 0 but different ratings) resolve to a UNIQUE highest-rated leader → the feature was pinned and never rotated; count-only puts all equally-used candidates in one tie group so they cycle. Mirrors `suggestRestedPipe`'s rest-only tie-key rationale. The **"Ce soir ?" secondary list** also rotates on the 12 h cadence and holds back ≥ 1 candidate (`dailyWindow(pool, now, size, FEATURE_ROTATE_MS)` with `size = min(3, pool−1)`) so a modest cellar's runners-up cycle instead of showing the whole post-hero pool frozen. **The big "Ce soir ?" HERO (`rotateDailyHero`) also rotates every 12 h** (was calendar-day) — the whole home suggestions block now breathes on one 12 h rhythm (hero + secondary list + both "du moment" picks). **Two changes so the picks actually vary for a real cellar:** (1) both **"du moment" picks now rotate among the TOP TIER via `rotateDailyHero`** (pool = recently-smoked set when ≥ 2 exist, else all actives; sorted most-recent-then-best-rated, `pool = min(5, len)`) instead of `pickDailyTie` — `pickDailyTie` PINNED a unique most-used leader (size-1 tie group → `bucket % 1` never moves), so a user with one genuinely-most-smoked blend (e.g. Autumn Evening at 3 sessions) saw the SAME "du moment" forever. The user chose variety over a strict "single most-used" reading; the top pick still often surfaces (it's `pool[0]`) but the others cycle in. `pickDailyTie` stays in `suggest.ts` for `suggestRestedPipe`. (2) **a per-launch rotation axis** (`homeRotationSeed` / `cave-sugg-rot`, see the keys table) — the 12 h bucket is stable within its window, so reopening/reloading inside it showed identical picks ("toujours les mêmes"); `rotShift = homeRotationSeed() × FEATURE_ROTATE_MS` is added to the rotation clock of ALL FOUR picks so every app launch steps them forward, while in-session navigation stays stable. **Per user request ("toujours les mêmes" with many open tins): the "Ce soir ?" picks are now a RANDOM draw, and rating is dropped everywhere.** (1) The `tonight` memo calls `computeSmokeSuggestions(..., { openOnly: true, ignoreRating: true, max: 500 })` — eligibility is OPEN jar lots only (`openJarLots`: `status === "jar"` && weight > 0; sealed cellar tins excluded), `rebuy === false` still excluded, and the rating score + `favorite` reason are skipped. The render body draws hero + secondary from `seededShuffle(tonight, shuffleSeed)` (Fisher-Yates over `mulberry32`, both in `suggest.ts`) where `shuffleSeed = homeRotationSeed()×100003 + floor(now/12h) + sessionCount` — deterministic within a session, reshuffled each launch / 12 h / logged session. This REPLACED the score-ranked `rotateDailyHero`/`dailyWindow` path for "Ce soir ?" (those helpers stay in `suggest.ts` — `dailyWindow` still drives the "À surveiller" list, `rotateDailyHero` still drives "du moment"). Reason chips (trop âgé / jamais fumé / …) still render on whatever is drawn; the "À surveiller" section still surfaces aging/low-stock separately. (2) Both **"du moment" sorts drop the rating tiebreak** — ranked by recent session COUNT only (then stable date/id), still rotated among the top tier by `rotateDailyHero`. Also surfaces the interactive smoking calendar and the "À entretenir" pipe-maintenance reminder, and a footer privacy link (see the DocPageView Google/OAuth note). |
| `inv`    | `CuratorInventoryListView` | Tobacco inventory list (filter / sort / group / search). When `statusFilter === "wish"` the wishlist takes over the same view. |
| `inv` + `detail` set | `CuratorInventoryDetailView` | Tobacco detail (lots, profile, notes). Activated by setting the `detail` ctx state to the tobacco object — no view-key change. |
| `addT`   | `CuratorTobaccoFormView` | Add new tobacco form |
| `editT`  | `CuratorTobaccoFormView` | Edit existing tobacco |
| `pipes`  | `CuratorPipesListView` | Pipe collection list |
| `pipes` + `pipeDet` set | `CuratorPipesDetailView` | Pipe detail. Activated by setting the `pipeDet` ctx state. |
| `addP`   | `CuratorPipeFormView` | Add new pipe form |
| `editP`  | `CuratorPipeFormView` | Edit existing pipe |
| `acc`    | `CuratorAccListView` | Accessories list |
| `acc` + `accDet` set | `CuratorAccessoryDetailView` | Accessory detail. Activated by setting the `accDet` ctx state. |
| `addA`   | `CuratorAccessoryFormView` | Add new accessory form |
| `editA`  | `CuratorAccessoryFormView` | Edit existing accessory |
| `journal`| `CuratorJournalView` | Session journal |
| `addJ`   | `CuratorSessionFormView` | Add new session form |
| `editJ`  | `CuratorSessionFormView` | Edit existing session |
| `tasting`| `CuratorTastingView` | Live tasting mode (setup → running with timer). **The setup's "Lieu" block finally carries the 12 px page gutter.** Every sibling section (Tabac / Pipe / Poids / the CTA) is `padding: "0 12px …"` and this one was a bare `marginTop: 16`, so its label and card ran edge to edge and stepped out of the left margin on every phone. Its `SessionFormView` twin never had the bug because that one is a real `FormSection`, which owns the gutter; this block is hand-rolled and simply never got one. **`i18n:layout` structurally cannot see this class** — a block 24 px wider than its siblings but still inside the viewport overflows nothing and clips nothing, which is none of that checker's failure modes (four of them, and it is still none of them). Locked at source level by `layoutGutters.test.ts` (comments blanked first, per the documented trap). |
| `help`   | `CuratorHelpView` | In-app user guide. **The search row WRAPS.** The field shares it with the collapse-all button, which is `nowrap` and whose German label is the longest thing there ("Alle einklappen", uppercase + letter-spaced mono), so the field was squeezed to ~157 px while its own placeholder ("Anleitung durchsuchen") needs ~171 — the hint telling you what the box is for was the part cut off. `flexWrap: "wrap"` costs a line ONLY where the two do not fit and clips nothing, and the field's `flex: "1 1 220px"` floor is what makes the row actually BREAK rather than shrink further (probed: `flexWrap` alone changes nothing, since a `flex: 1` item is happy at any width). Shortening the German was the alternative and is the one tried and reverted. **`i18n:layout` cannot see this class either: it walks TEXT NODES, and a placeholder is an ATTRIBUTE** — verified, the script never reads one. Fetches `public/help.html` at runtime, source-slices the 18 sections by their per-language h2 ids, renders each as a collapsible card with search + expand/collapse-all. Opened from Settings → Help; back returns to home. **Language handling is generalised** — `SECTION_IDS` maps each section to a `Record<lang, id>` (localized ids like `fr-tabac` / `en-tobacco`) and the view resolves the active language via the shared `pickLang` (docPage.ts) with the same requested → en → fr → first-present fallback as the other doc pages. Adding a language = add a `<code>: "<code>-…"` id per section + author the anchors + extend `HELP_SECTION_IDS` in doc-check + ship a dict; no render code changes. |
| `changelog` / `privacy` / `licenses` | `CuratorDocPageView` | Unified in-app static-doc view (lazy-loaded). One component renders all three simple shipped pages, keyed by `view` via the `DOC_PAGES` registry (file + titleKey + optional `webUrl`). Fetches `public/<page>.html` at runtime; `extractDocContent(html, lang)` (in `utils/docPage.ts`) resolves the `<div id="sec-<lang>">` block (or the whole `<body>` for the English-only licenses) and strips the standalone chrome, rendered via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` re-themed with the curator tokens (`DOC_BODY_STYLES`). Links inside rewritten to `target="_blank"`. Opened from Settings (`nav("changelog"/"privacy"/"licenses")`); back → home. **Google/OAuth constraint**: the `.html` files stay published + served network-first, and `TermsGate` keeps a DIRECT `./privacy.html` link (it renders pre-app, before the views mount, and the privacy policy must be reachable at a public URL from a plain browser) — do NOT convert the TermsGate link to `nav()`. **`HomeViewV2` ALSO carries a footer "Confidentialité" link (`nav("privacy")`)** — Google OAuth verification wants a privacy link reachable from the app's home once the user is past the terms gate. The classic HomeView had it; it was dropped when HomeViewV2 became the sole home and restored in 169. Locked by the "privacy link" test in `HomeViewV2.test.tsx` — do NOT remove it. The privacy/licenses in-app views also surface an "Ouvrir la version web ↗" footer link to the canonical URL. Replaced the standalone `ChangelogView` + the new-tab `ExtLink href` privacy/licenses links. |
| `stats`  | `CuratorStatsView` | Statistics and SVG charts |
| (no view key) | `CuratorWishFormView` | Wishlist add/edit form. Gated by `showWishForm || editWishId` — not by `view`. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorSearchModal` | Global search across tobaccos / pipes / wishlist / accessories / session notes. Toggled by `searchOpen`. **Also searches** the bundled reference **catalogue** (~1200 blends) via `searchCatalog()` — the DB is lazy-loaded on modal open (never cold-start), matched with `tobaccoDbSearchMatch`, shown as a "Catalogue" group; tapping a catalog hit sets `catalogSeed` (ctx) + `nav("catalog")` so `CatalogView` opens pre-filtered on the query (consumed once then cleared). **A WISHLIST hit now resolves to its CARD.** It used to `nav("inv")` + set the `wish` filter and DROP `h.id` — its own comment said so (« the user can tap the card to edit ») — so a hit opened a 16-item list scrolled to the top, on a different item, with nothing saying where the match went (reported with two screenshots). Every other kind in that handler resolves to its specific row; this was the one that did not. `wishFocusId` (ctx, same consumed-once shape as `catalogSeed`) carries the id; `InventoryListView` expands the item's brand group when the list is GROUPED and that group COLLAPSED — the card is not in the DOM at all otherwise, which is why the reveal is an effect and not a scroll call in the handler — then scrolls it so its TOP sits just under the sticky TopBar and rings it in brass for ~2.6 s. **That scroll was fixed twice, and both failures are worth knowing.** It first used `scrollIntoView({block:"center"})`, which centres the BOX — and a wish card carries the full description, so it is routinely taller than the viewport and centring pushed the photo, the brand and the name off the top, i.e. the three things that identify it (« le haut de la fiche est masqué »). The offset is now the TopBar's MEASURED height (`data-topbar`), never a constant: that bar grows with the safe-area inset and with the text-size setting. Then a single `scrollTo` still landed 2 px under the bar, and a loop that exited once the position looked settled left a mid-list card at **1598 px, entirely off screen** — because every card fades in through `useEnter` with a delay PROPORTIONAL TO ITS INDEX, so the loop finished before that card's animation had begun, and expanding a group re-lays the list out afterwards too. It now corrects across the whole ~1 s animation window and ABORTS as soon as `window.scrollY` is not the value it set, so it cannot fight a deliberate swipe. Measured after the fix: card top at 75 px against a 66 px bar, in BOTH list modes. The collapse key is the STABLE brand (`w.brand || ""`, the rule). **The destination is the CARD, never the edit form**: a wish has no fiche (the card carries the whole record) and whole-card tap-to-edit was deliberately removed from `WishCard` because users expected the read-only behaviour of the other cards and got a form (« No surprise edits ») — a search result is that same expectation. Verified in a browser in BOTH list modes, the collapsed-group case being the one that can actually fail. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorSettingsModal` | All preferences + Drive + AI + export. Toggled by `importModal`. |
| (section) | `CatalogueStatus` (in `SettingsModal.tsx`) | The « Catalogue de référence » section in Réglages → Données — download the template, load a CSV, see what is loaded, export it back, remove it, and back it up to / fetch it from the cloud. **The SAVE action is gated on a catalogue being loaded and the RESTORE action deliberately is not** — the device that most needs to fetch one is the one that has none, so gating both would hide the button exactly where it is the answer. **It sits BEFORE « Export & Import », and that is not cosmetic**: placed after, it pushed « Effacer toutes les données » into the middle of the tab, which is the complaint one control over. **Found by RENDERING it**, not by a test. `catalogueMeta` has THREE states and the block tells them apart — `undefined` (first IndexedDB read in flight) renders NOTHING, `null` renders the hint, an object renders the counts; collapsing the first two would flash « Aucun catalogue chargé » on every open. It surfaces every count the parser reports (rows skipped for want of identity, duplicate keys, unrecognised category / cut labels kept verbatim) because **a catalogue that silently dropped a third of its rows looks exactly like one that loaded fine**. Verified end to end in a real browser through the actual file picker: « 3 blends · 3 marques », the load date, the file name and all three warning lines. Locked by `userCatalogueSection.test.tsx`, including the placement.
| (panel) | `SyncDiagView` (in `SettingsModal.tsx`) | The MERGED cloud panel — one button, one panel, doing both jobs the two former ones did (a verdict per file, plus its size and a bin). **Two wiring defects came out of that merge and neither was visible to any test**, because both live BETWEEN modules. **(a) It lost the `!gdriveConfirm` guard the old `BackupsListPanel` carried, while the comment fifty lines above went on PROMISING it** — worse than no comment, since it tells the reader the invariant holds. **The REASON first recorded here was WRONG and is corrected in place rather than deleted, because the mistake is the instructive part**: it said both panels carried a delete button over the same files, so a row binned in one survived in the other and its bin then 404'd. The picker's bin only ever rendered under `isDeleteMode`, a state whose ONLY producer (`gdriveManageBackups`) had lost its entry point in that very merge — so the conflict named was unreachable, and the correct rule had been written from the code as it USED to be rather than as it was. What the guard is really for is simpler and still holds: `gdriveConfirm` IS the restore picker, whose primary action replaces the entire cellar, so stacking a second list of the same files under it invites a tap on the wrong one. The guard is on BOTH renders. The whole delete-mode chain — `gdriveManageBackups`, the `mode` parameter, the thirteen `isDeleteMode` branches and `gdriveDeleteOption` — was subsequently DELETED as unreachable. **(b) On Google Drive no file size was ever fetched.** Drive applies the `fields` mask VERBATIM and the three listings feeding `buildSyncDiag` asked for `files(id,name,modifiedTime)` — so `f.size` was `undefined`, every row rendered sizeless and the total read "—", killing the whole file-management half of the merge. **Dropbox worked throughout, its adapter ignoring the mask**, which is exactly how a wiring gap survives a reading; and the existing panel tests build rows by hand, so they could never have caught it. The mask is now the single named `SYNC_DIAG_FIELDS` — it had been written out three times and all three forgot the field, so the fix is one source of truth rather than three corrections. Listings that only need identity (the auto-file sweep, the newer-backup check) keep their own narrower mask on purpose. `gdriveApi.ts`'s own comment claimed "the listing has always had it; this row simply dropped it" — false, corrected in place. Locked by `cloudPanelWiring.test.ts`, source-level on purpose (the guard needs two independent states set at once; the mask's effect is invisible to any hand-built fixture), comments blanked first, probed three ways. |
| (panel) | `DuplicatesPanel` (in `SettingsModal.tsx`) | The duplicate utility, in Settings → Données. Shown ONLY when `duplicateCount(dataRaw) > 0` — an always-present button for a condition most users never hit is noise, and the count IS the reason to look. Groups live rows sharing brand+name (via `utils/duplicates.ts`), shows per row the lots / weight / sessions / inventory numbers / last edit, highlights a shared identifying inventory number as « probablement la même boîte », and merges into the row the user designates: lots + maintenance move over, sessions are repointed, the dropped rows go to the TRASH (30-day undo). Follows the three rules this section learned the hard way — the panel carries its OWN close ×, its result renders under its own button, and the destructive action names exactly what will move before it runs. Locked by the « duplicates utility » block in `SettingsModal.test.tsx`. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorTrashModal` | Dedicated Trash modal (restore / × / empty). Toggled by `trashOpen`. Opened from the Home top-bar trash icon when `dataRaw` contains any soft-deleted entity or lot. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorLightboxOverlay` | Full-screen image viewer. Toggled by `lightbox` (image URL/key). |
| (overlay) | `CuratorWelcomeModal` | One-shot first-run welcome. Gated by `cave-curator-welcomed` localStorage flag. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorStartupNoticeModal` | On-demand broadcast pop-up. Driven by `public/notice.json` (fetched on every mount via `?_v=…` cache-bust). Surfaces a fresh (unseen) message; dismissed id stored in `cave-notice-seen`. Defers itself if the user hasn't dismissed the welcome modal yet. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorThemeModeNoticeModal` | One-off feature-announcement pop-up for the **display** settings — light/dark mode AND text size (both in Paramètres → Préférences). Shows at every app open until a fixed window closes (`EXPIRY_MS`) OR the user taps "Ne plus afficher" (`cave-thememode-notice-dismissed-v2`). Plain close = session-only. Defers to the welcome modal (`cave-curator-welcomed`). **RETIRED — `EXPIRY_MS` was brought forward to 2026-07-24 (from 2026-08-15) so the pop-up no longer fires.** The app is now shared publicly and a newcomer discovers those settings for the first time anyway, so announcing them as "new" right after the welcome modal was noise. The component is kept (not deleted) as the reusable announcement shell: to broadcast a future feature, set a fresh `EXPIRY_MS` + bump the `DISMISS_KEY` suffix (that resets everyone's opt-out, as `-v2` did). Its tests mock clocks that straddle `EXPIRY_MS`, so moving the date means moving `IN_WINDOW` in `ThemeModeNoticeModal.test.tsx` too. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorTastingBanner` | Floating banner shown above every non-tasting view while a tasting is running or in overtime. |
| (overlay) | `CuratorOverlays` | DelConfirm modal, save error/warn banners, update pill, auto-update countdown, just-updated toast. Plus `CuratorPhotoErrorBanner` (oxblood, surfaces `photoErr` set by `handlePhotoUpload` — previously the 20 MB cap fired silently) and `CuratorCloudNewerBanner` (global brass-tinted variant of the Home banner so the multi-device guard reaches users who never visit Home; gated on `view !== "home"` to avoid stacking with the Home in-flow version, and below saveError/saveWarn/photoErr). Both Restore buttons (Home + global) carry a `<Spinner>` + disabled state driven by `cloudRestoreBusy`. Plus `CuratorLangDetectedToast` (one-shot "Langue détectée automatiquement · Modifier" toast surfaced when `cave-lang-auto` is set — first-launch browser-language auto-detection; waits for `cave-curator-welcomed === "1"` so it doesn't stack on the welcome modal, and clears `cave-lang-auto` when shown). |
| (overlay) | `CuratorLotFormModal` | Lot CRUD modal invoked from the tobacco detail view. |
