**Un octet NUL était committé dans ce fichier de test**, dans `catColor("Anglais" + "\0nope")` — manifestement un espace mangé par une citation de shell lors d'une édition ancienne. L'assertion restait juste (NUL n'est pas davantage une catégorie qu'un espace), mais git et grep traitaient le fichier comme BINAIRE, ce qui m'a coûté deux lectures refusées dans cette seule session. Réparé en passant ; la règle de ce fichier — corriger le mort ou le cassé évident dans le même commit quand c'est sûr — couvre exactement ça.

**LE RÉSIDU, MESURÉ SUR UN EXPORT ESPAGNOL COMPLET plutôt qu'estimé.** Ce qui n'est plus dans la langue de l'utilisateur se compte désormais en TROIS classes, toutes délibérées : (1) les quatre marqueurs `=== PIPES ===` / `=== WISHLIST ===` / `=== ACCESSOIRES ===` / `=== SEANCES ===`, qui sont des SENTINELLES — le lecteur s'y arrête pour ne pas lire une ligne de pipe comme un tabac, donc les traduire ferait exactement la panne qu'ils préviennent ; (2) le JARGON du métier (`Ribbon`, `Flake`, `Billiard`, `Ebonite`), absent des maps `_XX` parce que toutes les langues disent le mot anglais — ce n'est pas du français non traduit, c'est le vocabulaire de la pipe ; (3) les DONNÉES de l'utilisateur (« Armoire A », « Lyon »), qui ne sont à personne d'autre. **Il ne reste aucune chaîne française ni aucun jeton interne dans un export non francophone.**

**(c) Importing the app's OWN CSV template turned the diagnostic red.** The template shipped a `Statut = Pot` example row and **no** "Date mise en pot" column, so the lot arrived opened-with-no-opening-date and tripped `jar-has-dateOpened` (and `finished-has-dateFinished`) at the next `save()` — on the exact path the help documents: download the template, fill it, import it. The EXPORT emits both columns and the parser has always read them, so an export round-trip was clean; only a template or hand-built file bit. **Fixed in the PARSER as well as the template, and the parser is the load-bearing half**: any hand-built CSV can say "Pot" without a date, and the app fabricates this date everywhere else on purpose (`applyLifecycleDates` stamps today when the user promotes a lot in the form). `parseTobaccoCsv` now back-fills the date a status IMPLIES, with the narrowest guess available — a tin cannot have been opened before it was bought, so jar → `datePurchased`, finished → `dateOpened || datePurchased` — falling back to the import date, which is the form's own guess. **`todayIso` is INJECTED** (like `applyCataloguePlan`'s `nowIso`) so the module stays pure and fuzzable; with no clock supplied it invents nothing. A supplied date is never overwritten, and a CELLAR lot is left alone (a sealed tin has no opening date). The template also gained the two columns its own comment already promised (*"the same header shape the export uses"*) in the export's own position, so it DEMONSTRATES the column instead of relying on the repair. Locked by the « lifecycle dates a status implies » blocks in `csvImport.test.ts` + `useExportImport.test.ts` — the latter drives the **real shipped template** through the real parser and the real invariants, because asserting the header list would have passed the day the parser regressed. Probed: reverting both halves reddens 2.

**The CSV recap actively MISREPORTED a duplicate it had just created.** `resolveMergeMatch`'s `brand|name` fallback fires only when EXACTLY ONE local and ONE staged row share the key (the ambiguity guard); when several do it refuses — rightly, it cannot tell which row this is — and the import lands as a NEW row beside them. **Nothing counted that**: `mergeRefusedByUid` requires the import to carry a `uid` AND the local candidate to be unique, and a CSV row has no uid at all. Meanwhile the CSV recap **recomputed** "already present" by `dupKey` against live rows, a computation with no knowledge of what the merge had done — so the message read « 1 tabac déjà présent : aucun nouveau lot ajouté » for an import that had just added a full duplicate. It COMPOUNDS: one duplicate pair (precisely the state the DuplicatesPanel exists to clean up) makes every later import of that blend add another copy, each reported as a no-op — the audit measured 54 of 800 randomised CSV round-trips non-idempotent, all attributable to this. Two halves: the new pure `mergeAmbiguousName(imp, keyIds, stagedKeyCount)` feeds `identityConflicts` at all four add sites (**disjoint from `mergeRefusedByUid` by construction** — that one requires `ids.length === 1`, this one `> 1` on one side or the other — so one counter cannot double-count, and a name matching NOTHING is an ordinary addition and stays unreported); and `MergeSummary.tobaccosMatched` lets the recap **read the merge instead of guessing at it** (`uptodate = matched − toppedUp − entitiesUpdated`, since an LWW refresh is a change, not a no-op). The CSV recap now also surfaces `merge_recap_identity` and `merge_recap_trashed`, which only the JSON picker had. `merge_recap_identity` was REWORDED in six languages — it said « mais une identité différente », true only of the uid refusal — to « sans pouvoir y être rattachées », which covers both. **A test pinned the old behaviour and its own comment explained the defect** (*"stageImport is mocked here, so onMerged never fires → the matched row counts as already up to date"*): that fallback WAS the bug, so the case now asserts the opposite and records the reversal, with two new cases driving `onMerged` to prove the line still appears when the merge genuinely reports a match. Probed: neutering `mergeAmbiguousName` reddens 4.

**The settings module's own comment claimed a reload that did not exist.** `appSettings.ts` said *"the caller reloads after a restore — which the restore path already does"*; `grep location.reload` over `src/` returns only `EB`, `useAppUpdate.doUpdate` and three catalogue/language error buttons. So for seven releases a replace-restore wrote every preference to storage and left the RUNNING app on the old ones: language, theme, light/dark mode, text size, currency, date format and the accounting toggle all looked unrestored until the next launch — and `saveWeightUnit` converts the two unit-scoped weights from REACT STATE, so the next unit toggle silently discarded the restored values and rewrote `cave-weight-unit` from the pre-restore state. `useImportConfirm` now reloads after a REPLACE, **gated on preferences having actually LANDED** (`applySettings` returns a count), so a settings-less backup or a block whose every key was dropped by the allowlist restores with no restart. **WHY A RELOAD RATHER THAN THE APP SETTERS**, since that looks like the tidier fix: `applySettings` writes the backup's RAW values, which are already consistent with each other (`cave-weight-unit: "oz"` beside a default weight expressed in oz), so feeding them through `saveWeightUnit` would CONVERT them a second time — routing ~20 setters while suppressing their conversions is a manual reload with more ways to be wrong, and four of these keys are read pre-mount in `main.jsx` and cannot be applied any other way at all. **The reload WAITS on the imported photo writes** (`photoWrites`, the promise introduced for it): those writes are asynchronous, and restarting over them would lose exactly the photos that promise exists to account for — probed, so the wait cannot be dropped. Locked by the « a REPLACE that adopts preferences reloads » block in `useImportConfirm.test.ts` (reload on a settings-bearing replace; NO reload for a settings-less backup, for an all-dropped block, or for a MERGE; the cellar saved before the restart; and the photo-write wait). The stale comment is corrected in place rather than deleted, because a comment asserting a mechanism that does not exist is worse than no comment.

**Two silent PHOTO losses, one on each side of the door.** Both from the audit; both are a loss reported as a success. **(a) IMPORT** (`useImportConfirm`): `imgCache.put` **RESOLVES `false`** on a transaction error or abort (quota exceeded, private mode) and only REJECTS when `open()` fails — the rule CLAUDE.md has stated — and this caller inspected the resolution not at all, only `.catch`ing. The shape is what made it costly: `save(next)` runs FIRST, so every row already points at its `local-photo-*` key, and the in-memory `imgLocal` map serves those photos for the rest of the session; the restore looked perfect until the next launch, when every photo became a placeholder — permanently. Failures are still swallowed PER PHOTO (one bad blob must not surface as an unhandled rejection mid-import, the original reason for the `.catch`); what changed is that the TOTAL is counted and raised on the app-wide photo channel (`setPhotoErr`, threaded in from App.tsx, typed `(m: string) => void` because that banner is cleared with `""` and not `null`). **(b) EXPORT** (`gatherLocalImages`): it swallowed every per-key failure and **always resolved**, with whatever subset it read — so `doExport`'s guard, whose own comment says *"gatherLocalImages can reject on a broken IndexedDB"*, **could not fire for the failure it names**, and a device with an unreadable store produced a backup with the photos missing, reported success and called `markExported()`. **TWO thresholds, and the second is the regression I nearly shipped**: a read that RESOLVES EMPTY means the photo is already GONE (an eviction, or the GC bug that really did wipe blobs) and must NOT reject — doing so would stop such a user exporting their CELLAR at all, far worse than a backup without photos they no longer have; only a read that THROWS counts, and only when EVERY read threw, which leaves a partial read alone (aborting a backup over one bad blob turns a recoverable partial export into no export). **The callers then split by STAKES, and the rule is worth keeping**: an interactive ONE-SHOT artifact (JSON export, ZIP) fails LOUDLY — the user keeps that file as their archive, so a silently photo-less one bites years later — while a CONTINUOUS UNATTENDED net degrades and records: the cloud auto-save swallows to an empty map and stamps `photos-unreadable` in its diagnostic, because stopping it would cost the user every backup. The manual cloud save degrades too but says so in the status line already sitting under its button. **THAT LAST CLAUSE WAS FALSE FOR SEVEN RELEASES, and the way it was false is the lesson.** The warning was set inside `gatherLocalImages`' `.catch` and then OVERWRITTEN one microtask later by `st_saving`, and again by `st_done` on success — with encryption off `maybeEncryptPayload` is `Promise.resolve(plaintext)`, so it never had a frame to be read in. The user saw « Sauvegarde… » then « ✓ OK », a backup with NO `_imageData` went up, and `markExported()` disarmed the "you have not backed up" reminder for 30 days; the loss surfaced only on a restore somewhere else. The comment sitting three lines above the `.catch` promised exactly what did not happen (*"says so in the status line the user is already watching (it sits right under this button)"*) — **a comment asserting a mechanism that does not work is worse than no comment**, and this file repeated the claim. The failure is REMEMBERED in a local flag now and reported at the END, where nothing paints over it, with a longer clear (6 s) like every other degraded message here; `markExported` still fires, because the cellar genuinely did reach the cloud and the reminder is legitimately disarmed — what was missing was only that the user be TOLD. **The AUTO path was correct throughout** (`recordAutosaveDiag("photos-unreadable")` persists), so it was the ATTENDED path that was silent, which is the inversion worth remembering: the one a human is watching had the weaker reporting. The new case asserts the SETTLED `gdriveStatus` rather than the setter calls — the defect was never "the warning is not set", it was "the warning does not survive to be read". Probed. Locked by the « a photo that fails to persist is reported » blocks in `useImportConfirm.test.ts` + `useGdriveSync.test.ts`, probed in BOTH directions — including a probe for the over-reach, so the empty-vs-unreadable distinction cannot be quietly flattened. **The guard then caught MY OWN fix and was right**: `new Error("photo store unreadable (…)")` is English prose, and `doExport`'s alert appends `e.message` verbatim — the exact class documented above, introduced by the person who had just read that section. The thrown message is now the CODE `photo-store-unreadable`, mapped to a translated sentence by `useExportImport.exportFailMsg`, and the guard was widened by exactly one tight rule: a **spaceless kebab code** is not prose, because prose always has spaces. Verified as a non-loosening against every historical defect the gate was written for (`"No token"`, `"invalid JSON"`, `"Fichier corrompu"`, `"Google non dispo"`, `"no refresh token"` all contain a space and all still fail), and those cases are pinned as fixtures — along with `"PHOTO-STORE-UNREADABLE"`, since shouting is not a code. Same build: the `photos-in-backups` prose contract fired on the edit and was RE-READ (the disclosure is unchanged — the rejection path alters no key that is walked), and the 16 `(5 languages)` label counts in `label-contracts.json` became `(all languages)`, a form that cannot rot at the seventh language.

**Merging your OWN backup duplicated every row sitting in your TRASH, and minted a second row with the SAME `uid`.** The second audit HIGH; measured at **419 failures out of 1500** randomised cellars containing trashed rows and **0 out of 1500** without, so trashed rows were the sole cause. `buildLocalMaps` skips `deletedAt` rows — deliberately, so an imported LIVE copy is not merged into and shadowed by an invisible trashed row — but excluding them from the identity decision ENTIRELY meant the backup's copy of a row you had just deleted found nothing to match and was added as new, carrying the same uid. `uidMap[uid] = row.id` is last-wins, so every later merge then matched one of the two arbitrarily and left the other permanently stale; restore the trashed row and you had two LIVE rows with one identity. **The fix keeps that intent for the case it was written for and refuses to guess in this one**: a uid matching a TRASHED local row is the SAME entity, so nothing is added, nothing is resurrected (the backup may be a month old and the deletion deliberate), and the imported row's sessions are MAPPED to the trashed row rather than cleared to a fantôme. It is COUNTED — `MergeSummary.trashedSkipped` + `merge_recap_trashed` in six languages — because silence here reads as « ma sauvegarde n'a pas restauré mon tabac » while the remedy is one tap away in a place the user may not think to look. Same treatment for a trashed SESSION (the trash used to accumulate one duplicate per merge and « Tout restaurer » then logged the bowl twice). **The obvious alternative — mint a FRESH uid on the added copy — was considered and is WRONG, which is worth recording because it looks right**: the imported uid would still match nothing live, so EVERY merge would add another copy; idempotency is asserted specifically against that. `trashedLocalIdFor` matches on **uid ONLY** — a `brand|name` match against a trashed row is exactly what that skip refused, and a uid-LESS trashed row (a cellar migrated before uids existed) keeps the earlier behaviour, disclosed rather than guessed at. **The same build adds `checkUidInvariants`** (`tobacco|pipe|accessory|wishlist|session-uid-unique`), wired into `checkAllInvariants`: `uid` has been the cross-device merge identity and **nothing checked it was unique** — the id invariants cover the per-device numeric id, which was distinct here, which is precisely why the class reached production. Scoped per KIND (a tobacco and a pipe sharing a uid is harmless — every lookup is already inside one collection) and skipping trashed rows, like every sibling check. Locked by the « merging your own backup does not duplicate a TRASHED row » blocks in `useImportConfirm.test.ts` + `lotInvariants.test.ts`; probed in both directions (neutering the guard reddens 4 cases, unwiring the invariant 1).

**The merge path that minted a fresh TOBACCO id and carried its LOTS verbatim.** Found by an import/export audit; reproduced, fixed and probed. The added-wholesale branch did `Object.assign({}, tb, { id: nid })`, so a row added because the merge REFUSED to match it (the ordinary pre-uid two-device state, an ambiguous `brand|name`, or a locally-trashed row that is live in the backup) shared lot ids with the very twin it had just declined to collapse. **Lot ids must be unique GLOBALLY** (`lot-id-unique-global`) because `useTrashOps` and the 30-day sweep both delete / orphan **by lot id across every tobacco**: purging the duplicate took the original's LIVE lot, and the sweep orphaned the survivor's session with no user action at all. **Two sibling paths already did this right** — the CSV importer re-stamps every lot (under a comment describing this exact damage) and the lot-level merge re-stamps each appended lot — so this was the one that was missed, and `migrateData`'s `dedupeIds` does not cover it (its lot pass is per-TOBACCO). Now every added lot gets a `monotonicId()` and the mapping goes into `incomingLotMap`, so the row's own sessions follow their lot to the new id; **trashed lots are re-stamped too** (a soft-deleted imported lot is exactly what a user purges from the trash), and `uid` is preserved so a re-import still dedups by uid. **A pipe's `maintenance` ids deliberately get NO equivalent treatment** — every maintenance mutation is scoped to one pipe and neither the trash ops nor the sweep touch maintenance, so a cross-pipe duplicate is inert; what made lot ids dangerous was never the duplication but the operations that filter by lot id across every tobacco. **The lesson worth keeping is on the invariant, not the fix**: `lot-id-unique-global`'s own comment asserted "the source is fixed (the importer re-stamps from monotonicId)", which was true of the CSV importer ALONE — so the whole class stayed reachable behind a note that read as a closed case. THE CHECK is the guarantee; a comment claiming a source is clean is not. **The same build fixed a second, independent instance of the class found while verifying the first: `mergeDuplicates` (utils/duplicates.ts) said "moves the lots" and COPIED them** — re-stamped onto the kept row, left untouched on the dropped one — and since the trash is documented as that merge's undo, restoring the dropped row (one tap, no warning) put the same physical tins in the cellar twice. Measured on the fixture: **150 g of live stock where there were 100**. The moved lots are now SOFT-deleted on the source (not removed, so the trashed row still records what it held, and they stay hidden while the parent is trashed per invariant #23); the patch is collected into a `srcPatch` object rather than assigned to `src`, which IS the caller's object — `rows` is a shallow array copy and this module promises to mutate nothing. Locked by the « added-wholesale lots get fresh ids » blocks in `useImportConfirm.test.ts` + `duplicates.test.ts`; probed in both directions (reverting either fix reddens exactly 3 cases each). **RESIDUAL, disclosed:** the refusal to auto-merge two same-name rows with different uids STANDS — but note the asymmetry it rests on was smaller than stated, because until this build its own stated remedy ("a duplicate is undone by deleting one") was itself destructive.

**An imported API key lands on REPLACE only.** The key is excluded from exports by default (`cave-exclude-apikey` is an opt-OUT), so a backup carrying one required a deliberate act from whoever exported it — but the write was MODE-BLIND, and **merge is precisely how you accept a file from SOMEONE ELSE**, combining their cellar with yours. Their key then became yours: your auto-fill calls billed to their account, visible in their provider console, and your own key overwritten with nothing on the device to recover it from. It was made to announce itself, which helps, but the notice arrives after the write and reads as information rather than as something to undo. **The split is NOT invented here — it is the rule already made for `_settings`**, one storey up and for the same question: a REPLACE adopts the backup's device configuration (you asked to make this device look like that backup, and the cellar is being wiped anyway) while a MERGE does not, because combining two cellars is no reason to inherit the other device's language, theme and units. An API key is the same class of thing, with money and a billing account attached. **The merge still STAGES the key** — the refusal is at the write, not the parse, so the picker can still offer a replace with it and the two modes cannot disagree about what the file contains. Three older cases asserted the write on a MERGE and were repointed to `replace`, each recording the reversal at its own assertion, because what they guard (validation, provider routing, deferral) is orthogonal to the mode. Reported by the pre-launch security agent; **decided by the user**. Probed: reverting to mode-blind reddens 3 cases.

**The merge DETACHED sessions from their lot and said nothing at all.** The rule doing the detaching is CORRECT and is not changing: on a DUP-matched tobacco, a session pointing at a lot whose LOCAL weight was never reduced for that bowl pushes Σsessions past `weightInitial − weightG`, overflows the balance and double-counts the stock, so clearing the ref to a safe orphan is the only balance-preserving answer. **What was wrong is the SILENCE, and its shape is the point**: the other device's bowls DO arrive — they appear in the journal, so the import looks complete — while none of their grams reach your stock. A cellar that quietly stops adding up, with no line anywhere saying why. The pre-launch drill measured **78 % of randomised two-device merges detaching at least one session**. `MergeSummary.sessionsDetached` + `merge_recap_detached` (six languages) now report it, and the sentence states the **consequence** (« leur grammage n'est pas déduit de votre stock ») rather than the mechanism, because the consequence is what the user can act on. **The counter is deliberately narrow and fires at the ADD site, not at the clearing site**: only a session that arrived WITH a lot reference, lost it, and was then ADDED counts — one that dedups into a local row leaves that row untouched, so nothing was detached and counting it would report a loss that did not happen (probed: moving the increment to the clearing site reddens that case). An added-WHOLESALE tobacco is also silent, since its lots and sessions arrive as a consistent unit (the safe case (a)). Locked by the « detached sessions are counted and reported » block in `useImportConfirm.test.ts`, which REPRODUCES the two-device state before asserting the count; probed in both directions.

**Lot uid-FIRST merge:** now that every lot carries a stable `uid`, the lot-level append dedup matches by `uid` FIRST — an imported lot whose uid is already local is the SAME physical tin (skip); an imported lot with a uid NOT present locally is a genuinely-NEW tin and is APPENDED **even if its `lotMergeKey` (acquisition identity) collides with a local lot**. `lotMergeKey` is now only the fallback for uid-LESS legacy imported lots. This closes the round-15 import stock-loss gap (two UNNUMBERED identical-acquisition tins — same empty box + price + dates — used to collide on `lotMergeKey` so a device holding an extra such tin had it silently DROPPED + its sessions orphaned). An appended lot KEEPS its imported uid (re-import dedups by uid → idempotent) or mints one if uid-less. The orphan rule is UNCHANGED (a deduped lot still isn't re-linked → its foreign session is orphaned, balance-safe). Locked by the "appends an EXTRA identical-acquisition tin by uid" + "re-import of uid'd lots is idempotent" tests. Maintenance entries also carry a uid now but are NOT yet cross-device-merged (preserved on a dup-matched pipe via `LWW_PROTECT_PIPE`) — the uid future-proofs that. |
| `useTobaccoStore` | `form`, `editId`, `detail`, `lotForm`, `lotDet`, `addLotMode`, `showFinished`, `tobGrouped`, `collapsedTobGroups` | `addTobacco()`, `updateTobacco()`, `updateTobaccoTastingNotes(id, notes)`, `deleteTobacco()`, `addLotToTobacco()`, `updateLotInTobacco()`, `removeLot()`, `changeLotStatus()`, `toggleTobGroup()` |
| `usePipeStore` | `pipeForm`, `editPipeId`, `pipeDet`, `showFinishedPipes`, `pipesGrouped`, `collapsedPipeGroups` | `addPipe()`, `updatePipe()`, `deletePipe()`, `changePipeStatus()`, `togglePipeGroup()`, `addMaintenance(pipeId, entry)` / `updateMaintenance(pipeId, entryId, entry)` / `removeMaintenance(pipeId, entryId)` (pipe maintenance log inside `pipe.maintenance`; refreshes the open `pipeDet` snapshot after each mutation) |
| `useWishStore` | `wishForm`, `editWishId`, `showWishForm`, `wishGrouped`, `collapsedWishGroups` | `addWish()`, `updateWish()`, `delWish()`, `wishToInv()`, `toggleWishGroup()` |
| `useAccessoryStore` | `accForm`, `editAccId`, `accDet`, `showRetiredAcc`, `accsGrouped`, `collapsedAccGroups` | `addAccessory()`, `updateAccessory()`, `deleteAccessory()`, `changeAccStatus()`, `toggleAccGroup()` |
| `useSessionStore` | `sessForm`, `editSessId`, `sessGrouped`, `collapsedSessGroups` | `addSession()`, `addSessionFromTasting(form)`, `updateSession()`, `deleteSession()`, `toggleSessGroup()` |
| `useTastingSession` | `tasting` (null \| setup \| running) | `tastingStart()`, `tastingResume()`, `tastingSetupUpdate()`, `tastingIgnite()`, `tastingPause()`, `tastingUnpause()`, `tastingUpdate()`, `tastingEnd()`, `tastingCancel()`, `tastingElapsedMs()`, `tastingOvertimePrompt()`, `tastingOvertimeRemainingMs()`, `tastingPostponeOvertime()` |
| `useDbSync` | `dbSync` (null \| { hit, diffs[] }), `applied` (ghost-click latch) | `applyDbSync()`, `dismissDbSync()` — shared "Sync with DB" for TobaccoFormView + WishFormView. Surfaces when the edited form's brand+name matches a catalog entry AND a field diverges; it is only ever computed against a FULLY loaded catalogue, so the diff can never be partial (the `descVer` prop is gone); `applyDbSync` patches the form to the catalog values (excludes `tastingNotes`). `applied` flips on ~420 ms → inert "✓ Synchronisé" Notice + full-screen tap-catcher overlay. See the `src/hooks/useDbSync.ts` structure-tree entry for the full ghost-click rationale. |
| `useStartupNotice` | `notice` (null \| `{id, tone, title, body}`) | `dismiss()`. Fetches `public/notice.json` at mount via `?_v=<timestamp>` (SW bypass) and surfaces a fresh (unseen) message as a pop-up via `CuratorStartupNoticeModal`. Tracks the dismissed id in `localStorage["cave-notice-seen"]` so a returning user doesn't see the same message twice. Empty file `{}` → no banner. Editing the deployed JSON pushes a message to every user with NO build / version bump. `expiresAt` field auto-hides past notices; fetch / parse failures silently fall back to "no banner". **The notice carries ONE OPTIONAL SLOT PER `LANGUAGES` CODE** (`RawNotice.fr?/en?/…`) and `pickContent` resolves requested → `en` → `fr` → any slot present, so a broadcast authored in only some languages still shows in a fallback language instead of always French. **That tail is DERIVED from `LANGUAGES`** — it was the literal `["…","en","fr","es","de","it"]`, so a notice authored ONLY in a newer language was invisible to every other reader while an fr-only one reached everyone, and `RawNotice` refused the newer slot outright. Gate 15 cannot catch this shape (the array mixes a variable with codes); **when you add a language, add its `RawNotice` slot — nothing else needs touching.** |

## Pure Utility Functions

`src/utils.ts` — pure helpers (date/age/aggregations):

| Function | Signature | Purpose |
|----------|-----------|---------|
| `parseAgingMax(v)` | `(v: any) => {min: number, max: number}` | Parses `agingMax` string. Empty → {0,0}. Single value "10" → {10,10}. Range "10-15" → {10,15} (swaps if reversed). |
| `lotAgingStatus(lot, agingMax)` | `(lot, agingMax) => "overaged" \| "approaching" \| null` | Returns the aging alert level. **Overaged**: age > max. **Approaching**: range → age in peak window; single value → age within last year before peak. **null**: finished lot, **jar (opened) lot**, no agingMax, or not close enough. **Aging is CELLAR-ONLY** — a lot "en pot" is OPENED (the user's model: opened even if physically still in its tin), so it's no longer cellaring and gets NO aging alert / maturity band; instead an opened jar shows a neutral **"ouvert depuis N"** signal (elapsed time since `dateOpened`, `lot_open_since` + `fmtLotAge`, InventoryDetailView LotRow). `lotMaturityBucket` (cellarInsights.ts) is cellar-only in lock-step (it delegates its upper bands to `lotAgingStatus`). **Always call it with `effectiveAgingMax(tob)`, never a raw `tob.agingMax`**, so the family fallback applies uniformly (a raw-agingMax call bypasses the fallback and reintroduces the "bar and card disagree" bug). **Rendered per-lot via `MaturityChip`**: tobacco card in `InventoryListView` (full per-band distribution with counts), lot row + lot detail modal in `InventoryDetailView` (`LotRow` `maturity` prop / modal), and the lot edit modal `LotFormModal` (`lg`, via `bucketFromAgingStatus`). |
| `FAMILY_AGING_MAX` / `effectiveAgingMax(tob)` | `Record<string,string>` / `(tob) => string` | Values revised after a sourced review (smokingpipes aging guide / pipestud "Latakia Aging" / pipe-club aging FAQ / pipesandcigars FAQ / tobaccoreviews). `FAMILY_AGING_MAX` = typical max cellaring age (year or `"N-M"` range) per tobacco family: Virginia `"15-25"`, VaPer `"15-20"`, Virginia/Burley `"10-15"`, Anglais `"6-10"`, Balkan `"8-12"`, Écossais `"8-12"`, Burley `"5-10"`, Latakia `"5-8"`, Dark Fired `"7-10"`, Perique `"10-15"`, Oriental `"6-10"`, Turkish `"6-10"`, **Virginia/Latakia `"8-12"`, Américain `"5-10"`, Cigare `"7-10"`, Lakeland `"5-10"`, Anglais aromatique `"3-6"` (REASONED, not calibrated: TWO volatile components leave first, the topping (top notes fade within a few years) and an often-light Latakia that can disappear altogether, so it cannot take pure Latakia's `"5-8"`; it stays above a Danish aromatic's `"3"` because there IS leaf structure — Virginia + Burley + Cavendish — that goes on marrying)**, Aromatique/Cavendish `"3"` (mirrors the AI-prompt defaults — Latakia fades so it's short, Virginia/Perique cellar longest). `effectiveAgingMax` returns the tobacco's own `agingMax` when it parses to a positive value, else the family default, else `""`. **THE single resolver** for every maturity/aging classification (utils.computeStats, cellarInsights.computeCellarMaturity, App.tsx filtered memo, watchlist, suggest, InventoryListView, InventoryDetailView, LotFormModal). Display of the raw entered value (form field, "Vieillissement: X ans" label) still shows `tob.agingMax` untouched. |
| `detectUiLang(preferred, supported)` | `(preferred: readonly string[], supported: readonly string[]) => string` | First-launch UI-language pick. Walks the browser's preferred languages, primary-subtag matching (`"de-CH"` → `"de"`) against the supported set, and returns the first hit; **falls back to English** when none match. Called by `src/main.jsx` before React mounts (see `cave-lang`). |
| `softBreakSlashes(s)` | `(s: string) => string` | Inserts a ZERO-WIDTH SPACE after each slash so a compound label wraps AT the slash. Reported with a screenshot: the Home "Familles" list rendered `Virginia/Burle` + `y`. An earlier fix had stopped that label being CLIPPED by letting it wrap (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), which guarantees it never overflows and, being *anywhere*, broke it mid-word. **MEASURED**: the label column is 81 px and the label needs **88 px at the default text size, 98 px at "L"** — it CANNOT fit on one line, so the only question is where it breaks; after the fix it is `Virginia/` + `Burley` at both sizes. **`break-word` is not the alternative**: `/` is UAX #14 class SY and is not a break opportunity of its own, so the label would overflow instead. `anywhere` STAYS as the last resort for a future category with neither space nor slash. **DISPLAY-ONLY — never store the result**, it would poison every comparison the app makes on a category value. Locked by `softBreakSlashes.test.ts`, which also checks the TRANSLATED labels (the rendered value comes from `xl()`). |
| `plural(n, one, other, lang?)` | `(n: number, one: string, other: string, lang?: string) => string` | Tiny pluralisation helper. Returns `one` or `other` per `n` and language plural rules; feeds the `unit_lot(s)` / `lbl_*_lower_p` / `lbl_finished_word_p` i18n keys so counts read naturally in every UI language. |
| `fmtNum(v, lang?)` | `(v: any, lang?: string) => string` | THE display formatter — comma decimal everywhere but English, typed precision preserved on a string, accumulation noise rounded off a computed number. **It must never render EXPONENTIAL notation, and for a long time it did.** `Number#toString` and `toFixed` both switch at 1e21, so a pasted price came out `1e+24` — in the app, and, measured, three times inside the collection report, i.e. in a document handed to a third party. The guard is one line and the interesting part is WHERE it sits: `collectionReport.money()` already carried its own 1e21 guard, correct, and **annulled it by handing its digits back to this function**, whose re-parse undid the work; fixing `money()` alone would have left every other caller leaking. When the source has no exponent and the result does, the SOURCE is returned (which also preserves typed trailing zeros); when the source is itself exponential — a computed `Number`, whose `String()` is already `1e+24`, or a hand-typed `1e24` — there is nothing to fall back to, so it is expanded via `toLocaleString`. No ordinary value reaches either branch: below 1e21 the two notations are identical by construction. |

`src/utils/lotUtils.ts` — pure functions with no React dependency:

| Function | Signature | Purpose |
|----------|-----------|---------|
| `pickJarLot(tob, weightUnit)` | `(tob: any, weightUnit: string) => {lot, idx} \| null` | Selects best jar lot (non-round weight first, then oldest `dateOpened`). `pickJarLot` and the internal `locateLotIdx` skip soft-deleted (`deletedAt`) lots so a trashed lot is never auto-selected for a session. |
| `applyLotWeightDelta(dat, tobId, lotId, delta, weightUnit)` | `(dat, tobId, lotId, delta, weightUnit) => AppData` | Adjusts lot weight, auto-finishes at 0g, auto-reactivates if delta > 0 on finished lot |

Both functions take `weightUnit` as an explicit last parameter (no closure over App state).

`src/utils/imgCache.ts` — IndexedDB image cache with no React dependency:

| Export | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `imgCache.open()` | Opens/creates the `cave-imgs` IndexedDB database |
| `imgCache.get(key)` | Retrieves a base64 image by key |
| `imgCache.put(key, base64)` | Stores a base64 image |
| `isSafeExternalUrl(url)` | Returns false for RFC-1918 IPs, localhost, bare hostnames, non-HTTP(S) protocols — used before any proxy fetch to prevent SSRF |

`src/utils/tobaccoDb.ts` — local catalog of ~1225 popular pipe-tobacco blends, used by the AI auto-fill:

| Export | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `loadTobaccoDb()` | Dynamic-imports `src/data/tobacco-db.json` once on the first lookup (it was a fetch on the same-origin `/tobacco-db.json` URL before, with a `?_v={APP_BUILD}` cache-bust). Vite emits the JSON as a content-hashed JS chunk under `dist/assets/tobacco-db-<hash>.js` so it's no longer exposed at the canonical `/tobacco-db.json` URL. Cache invalidation now rides on the chunk's content hash (changes whenever the JSON does, so any APP_BUILD bump shipping a new DB also ships a new chunk hash). Caches the parsed payload in module memory. Idempotent. Dedupes concurrent calls. Returns null on failure (bundler-level import rejection — practically unreachable) and sets a sticky `failed` flag so subsequent lookups short-circuit. |
| `tobaccoDbLookup(brand, name, lang)` | Async lookup. Awaits `loadTobaccoDb` then does the sync lookup. |
| `tobaccoDbLookupSync(brand, name, lang)` | Synchronous lookup against the cached DB. Exact match wins; on miss, falls back to approximate matching within the same brand (alias hit → substring → reverse substring), returning a result only when EXACTLY ONE candidate matches under the brand prefix. Brand is now also fuzzy-tolerant — punctuation-only differences ("G L Pease" ≡ "G. L. Pease"), "&"/"and" equivalence ("Cornell & Diehl" ≡ "Cornell and Diehl"), and small typos via Levenshtein ≤ 2 ("Sutlif" → "Sutliff", "Capstain" → "Capstan"). **Brand aliases** are sourced from the master CSV's column 13 — each brand row carries an optional `aliases: string[]` so a typed "C&D" / "Pease" / "MacBaren" resolves to the canonical brand without typos. **ASCII-fold of diacritics + ligatures** (NFD strip + æ/œ/ß/ø/đ/ł map) applies on every comparison: "vieux carre" ≡ "Vieux Carré", "jubilaeums" ≡ "Jubilæums". Returns null if cache isn't ready. |
| `tobaccoDbSearchMatch(blendKey, entry, query)` | Tokenized search matcher used by `CatalogView`. Splits the query on whitespace and AND-matches every token against the blob (brand display + key + name + composition + category + cut + aliases). Substring-first, then word-level Levenshtein fallback (cap 1 for 4-5 char tokens, cap 2 for ≥6). Solved the "Capstan blue" issue (DB stores "Capstan|Flake Blue" — "Capstan blue" wasn't a contiguous substring of "capstan flake blue"). Same ASCII-fold as the lookup path. |
| ~~`tobaccoDbRetry()`~~ | **GONE, and this row said otherwise until it was corrected.** It cleared a sticky `failed` flag so `CatalogView`'s Retry button could re-attempt a failed dynamic chunk import. There is no chunk: the catalogue is read from IndexedDB, `catalogueLoad` never rejects, and `null` means « you have not loaded one » — the ordinary state of a fresh install. Offering a retry would send the user to a remedy that cannot help, which is why the whole failure-kind apparatus (`isChunkFailure` / `tobaccoDbFailKind` / `tobaccoDbRetry`) went with it. Do not resurrect any of the three; `CatalogueMissing` is the answer. |
| `resolveCanonicalBrand` (interne) | **Un alias de FABRICANT pouvait masquer une marque réelle, et l'ordre des clés du fichier décidait du gagnant.** La résolution parcourait les marques UNE FOIS en rendant le premier trouvé, clé exacte **ou** alias : un alias porté par une marque figurant plus haut battait donc la vraie marque plus bas, et un simple réordonnancement du fichier changeait le résultat. Ce n'est pas cosmétique — cette fonction alimente l'auto-remplissage, l'offre catalogue des formulaires, `tobaccoDbCanonicalKey` (donc le badge « déjà en cave » et la détection de doublons) et la recherche. **Trois passes désormais — clé exacte, puis alias, puis substring/Levenshtein — pour qu'une marque qui EST le nom tapé batte une marque qui se contente de le lister, quel que soit l'ordre du fichier.** Sondé : revenir à une passe unique rougit 2 cas. |
| `tobaccoDbCanonicalKey(brand, name)` | Returns the canonical `"brand_key\|name"` string for a typed brand+name pair (or null if the blend can't be resolved). Uses the same brand-fuzzy + name-fuzzy ladder as `tobaccoDbLookupSync`. Powers the duplicate-detection warnings on the tobacco / wishlist forms so two differently-typed entries for the same blend collide (e.g. `"Solani 131"` ≡ `"Solani Red Label"` ≡ `"Solani Red"`). |
| `displayAliases(entry, brandDisplay?)` | The alternative names worth SHOWING on a fiche: the entry's `aliases` minus any already contained in the heading it would sit under (`tightNorm(brand + name)`), de-duplicated, original spelling preserved. The subtraction is the whole point — most aliases exist for the fuzzy matcher (`Peterson|Nightcap` carries `Nightcap`), so printing the raw list under the title reads as a bug. Measured over the shipped catalogue: 153 blends have something to say, 119 are fully suppressed. Rendered as the « Aussi appelé » line in `CatalogView`'s fiche, which is what makes a merged row's old name visible to the user rather than only to the matcher. |
| ~~`isChunkFailure(e)`~~ | **GONE with the bundled catalogue, and this row documented it as live until the audit that found it.** It matched a failed dynamic `import()` against the real wording of all three engines. There is no chunk to fail: the catalogue is read from IndexedDB and `catalogueLoad` never rejects. |
| ~~`tobaccoDbFailKind()`~~ | **GONE with the bundled catalogue.** It reported WHY the last load failed (`"chunk"` / `"shape"` / `"other"`) so a chunk failure could be given the RELOAD wording rather than a retry. The row also claimed it was *read by `CatalogView`, both entry forms and `useAiAutoFill`* — false on all four; nothing in `src/` references it. `CatalogueMissing` is what those surfaces render now. |
| `tobaccoDbSize()` | Number of blends in memory, 0 before the load. Diagnostic accessor; used by `tobaccoDb.test.ts` to assert the shipped catalogue is non-empty so the suite cannot pass vacuously. |
| `isTobaccoDbReady()` | Boolean — true once the cache is populated. |
| `_resetTobaccoDbForTests()` | Test-only — wipes the cache + failed flag. |

The DB is reference data, **NEVER persisted to localStorage / IndexedDB and NEVER included in user backups** (Drive, Dropbox, JSON / CSV / ZIP exports). The user's tobacco entry receives the DB **values** when auto-fill fires — those values become the user's data (which IS backed up). The DB itself stays read-only in-memory + SW-cached.

## Core Functions Reference

These functions live in `App.tsx` or are exposed via `appCtx` from hooks. UI rendering helpers and cross-cutting concerns stay in `App.tsx`; CRUD and business logic live in `src/hooks/` (see Custom Hooks section above) and are surfaced in `appCtx` for views to consume.

| Function | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `load()` | Deserialize state from `localStorage` |
| `save(data)` | Serialize and persist state to `localStorage` |
| `migrateData(d)` | Migrate older data schemas on load. **Every ELEMENT of the five collections must be an OBJECT, not just the array.** A primitive inside `data.pipes` made this function THROW, and the cost depends on the caller: on the LOAD path the `.catch` fires, the app boots on `INIT`, and the first save overwrites the intact blob (the shape through another door); on the CLOUD-RESTORE path `stageImport(…, {autoApply:"replace"})` has no guard at all, so the tap on « Restaurer » in the cloud-newer banner simply died with nothing on screen. **REPRODUCED before fixing across all five collections × three primitive kinds, and ONLY `pipes` threw** — `Cannot create property 'maintenance' on number '5'`, and for a string `Cannot assign to read only property 'length'` (`length` is in `_PIPE_STR_FIELDS`, and assigning any property of a primitive throws in strict mode, which ES modules are). **The other four SURVIVED, and that is luck rather than design**: they happen to write nothing unconditionally today, so the next field added to any of them re-opens the hole — hence ONE rule at the top for all five rather than a guard bolted onto the pipe loop, and hence `migrateDataPrimitiveRows.test.ts` covers the four that never threw, which is where the regression would appear. **DROP rather than coerce to `{}`**: a `{}` would be minted an id by `dedupeIds` and shown as a blank entry the user never created, in a cellar they are already trying to repair; a primitive carries nothing to recover. Pruned IN PLACE, not with `.filter()`: `migrateData` mutates `d` everywhere else, and two pre-existing cases assert the collections keep their ARRAY IDENTITY (*"does not overwrite existing accessories array"*) — `.filter()` returns a new array and broke both, and **the assertions were right, so the implementation was the thing to adjust**. Probed in both directions (dropping the pipes prune reddens 9 cases, keeping everything reddens all 29). Also clamps `nxT/nxW/nxP/nxA/nxJ` to integers ≥ 1 to prevent string concatenation if a tampered import file contains non-numeric counter values. **From audit #4:** (1) non-object payload guard — a bare JSON number/string/array in localStorage degrades to an empty cellar instead of throwing on `d.accessories=[]`; (2) after the clamp, `bumpCounterPastMaxId(counter, arr)` reconciles each counter PAST the max existing id of its collection so the next add() can never reuse a live id (was silent for tobaccos/wishlist/sessions — an old/forged/drifted counter minted a duplicate id); runs on load AND on every import path (replace `migrateData(INIT+staged)`, merge `migrateData(next)`); (3) the lot-id back-fill counter is seeded above the max existing lot id (not just `Date.now()`) so a forged near-future lot id can't collide with a back-filled one. |
| `addTobacco()` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Create a new tobacco entry. Mints ONE empty starter lot (`nextBoxNumber` + `monotonicId`/`newUid`) so a fresh tabac lands ACTIVE. **That starter lot no longer INVENTS 50 g.** It was built with `Object.assign({}, BL, …)` and `BL.weightG` is `"50"`, so every tabac created through the form arrived holding 50 g the user never entered: the fiche read « 50 g », the Home total counted it, the collection report valued it, and the first session debited a stock that does not exist. Found by the pre-launch first-run agent, and the user's own reading of it is what fixed the direction: *« si on crée avec 0 il va automatiquement être considéré comme un lot terminé »* — **so the answer is EMPTY, not zero, and the two are different states the app already models.** `isUntrackedWeight("")` is true, which short-circuits BOTH `stepApplyDelta` (nothing to debit) and `stepAutoFinish` (no zero to reach), while `""` is exactly what `checkLotInvariants` has skipped the balance for — *"an ABSENCE of data, not corruption"*. `"0"` would mean an empty tin and close the lot on the first bowl, which is the defect the user predicted. The tabac still lands ACTIVE because `countActive` keys on `status !== "finished"` and never reads the weight, and `isUsableLot` still offers the lot in a session. **`BL` itself is deliberately UNTOUCHED**: its `50` is the prefill of the *add a lot* form, a field the user is looking at and can edit — the defect was inheriting it where there is no field and no user. The CSV importer overrides `weightG` explicitly, so it never inherited it either. Probed: restoring the `50` reddens 3 of the 5 cases in `useTobaccoStore.test.ts` (the other two are the non-regression `countActive` guard and the `BL`-untouched assertion, green by construction) — **A lot-LESS tabac is now INACTIVE** (the inventory "active" filter is `countActive(t) > 0`, the `|| lots.length === 0` clause was dropped; kept in lock-step at App.tsx `filtered`, `InventoryListView` chip count, and `stats.ts` ratings histogram). Delete every lot and the tabac drops out of "Actifs" (still reachable via "Tous"). **The old `"inactive"` umbrella (`countActive===0`) was SPLIT into two statusFilters — `"used_up"` ("Épuisé": `countActive===0 && lots.length>0`, a tabac smoked/used up, a rebuy candidate) and `"nolot"` ("Sans lot": `lots.length===0`, no lot at all, incomplete data). Both placed before the "Lot fini" chip (the `finished` filter — `f_finished` relabelled from "Terminés"→"Lot fini" to clarify it means "has ≥1 finished lot", distinct from "Épuisé"=all lots done), both auto-hide at count 0. App.tsx `filtered` + the `InventoryListView` counts/chips/`STATUS_PILL_IDS` are in lock-step; i18n keys `f_used_up` / `f_nolot`. (The filter chips were briefly split into "Statut"/"Maturité" labelled rows; **that was REVERTED to ONE horizontally-scrollable chip row** — the split ate too much vertical space before the first card. Status chips then maturity chips in a single strip, no group labels, auto-hide unchanged; `flt_status`/`flt_maturity` keys removed. **The secondary filters — the Type/Coupe/Note dropdowns + the tag chips — are collapsed behind a disclosure (`filters_more`, `advFiltersOpen` local state), hidden by default. That disclosure moved from a labelled button on its own row to the `filter` funnel icon in the existing controls row (present in BOTH the tobacco and wishlist rows), lit while a hidden filter is active.**) (The Home "Tabacs" tile counts active tabacs = `activeRefs` = `hasActive`; a "Tabacs en stock" relabel was reverted per user request — the tile reads "Tabacs".) |
| `updateTobacco()` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Edit an existing tobacco entry |
| `deleteTobacco(id)` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Remove a tobacco entry |
| `addLotToTobacco()` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Append a lot/batch to a tobacco. Takes an optional 3rd `count` arg (clamped 1..50) — creates that many identical lots in ONE save (bulk purchase), each with its own `monotonicId()`; a NUMERIC boxNumber increments across the clones (5, 6, 7…), a non-numeric/empty one is copied verbatim. Omitted count → a single lot (backward compatible). The `count` flows from `LotFormModal`'s create-mode "Nombre de lots" field via `onSave(lot, count)`. |
| `updateLotInTobacco()` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Edit an existing lot (auto-fills dateOpened/dateFinished) |
| `removeLot(tobId, lotIdx)` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Soft-delete a lot (stamps `deletedAt`). Sessions referring to it keep their `lotId` intact so they re-attach cleanly if the lot is restored from Trash within 30 days. Only the 30-day cleanup or `permanentlyDelete("lot", id)` actually clears `lotId` on referencing sessions. `weightG` is preserved for stats. |
| `changeLotStatus()` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Quick status advance: `cellar → jar → finished` (auto-fills dateOpened / dateFinished). Always clears `dateFinished` when transitioning to any non-finished status. |
| `addPipe()` | **In `usePipeStore`** — Create a new pipe entry |
| `updatePipe()` | **In `usePipeStore`** — Edit an existing pipe |
| `deletePipe(id)` | **In `usePipeStore`** — Remove a pipe entry |
| `addWish()` / `delWish()` | **In `useWishStore`** — Add/remove wishlist items |
| `updateWish()` | **In `useWishStore`** — Edit an existing wishlist item; restores scroll position via `scrollSaveRef.current["wish"]` after save |
| `wishToInv(w)` | **In `useWishStore`** — Pre-fills the tobacco add form from a wishlist item and sets `fromWishRef.current = w.id`; the wishlist entry is deleted only when `addTobacco()` actually saves (deferred, atomic) |
| `addAccessory()` | **In `useAccessoryStore`** — Create a new accessory entry |
| `updateAccessory()` | **In `useAccessoryStore`** — Edit an existing accessory |
| `deleteAccessory(id)` | **In `useAccessoryStore`** — Remove an accessory entry |
| `addSession()` | **In `useSessionStore`** — Create a new session log entry; resolves `lotId` from `pickJarLot` if `sessForm.lotId` is empty, ensuring sessions always store a valid lot reference; if `weightG > 0`, deducts from the selected lot via `applyLotWeightDelta` |
| `updateSession()` | **In `useSessionStore`** — Edit a session: restores old lot weight first (uses finished-lot fallback in `applyLotWeightDelta` if `lotId` is empty or stale), then deducts new weight. |
| `deleteSession(id)` | **In `useSessionStore`** — Soft-delete a session (stamps `deletedAt`) and restore its `weightG` back to the lot. Skipped if `sess.lotId` is empty (lot was permanently purged via `permanentlyDelete("lot")` or 30-day cleanup). **Round-15: idempotency guard `if (!sess \|\| sess.deletedAt) return;` at the top — a second call on an already-soft-deleted session would double-credit the lot (weightG can exceed weightInitial, real corruption invisible to the underflow-blind balance check). This is the mutation-site prevention the balance invariant deliberately can't provide.** |
| `pickJarLot(tob, weightUnit)` | **In `src/utils/lotUtils.ts`** — Selects the "best" jar lot. App.tsx exposes a wrapper `pickJarLot(tob)` in `appCtx` that curries `weightUnit`. |
| `applyLotWeightDelta(dat, tobId, lotId, delta, weightUnit)` | **In `src/utils/lotUtils.ts`** — Adjusts lot weight by `delta`. App.tsx calls with explicit `weightUnit`. |
| `toggleTobGroup(key)` | **In `useTobaccoStore`** — Toggle collapsed state of a tobacco brand group |
| `toggleWishGroup(key)` | **In `useWishStore`** — Toggle collapsed state of a wishlist brand group |
| `togglePipeGroup(key)` | **In `usePipeStore`** — Toggle collapsed state of a pipe brand group |
| `toggleAccGroup(key)` | **In `useAccessoryStore`** — Toggle collapsed state of an accessory type group |
| `toggleSessGroup(key)` | **In `useSessionStore`** — Toggle collapsed state of a session year/month group |
| `aiAutoFill()` | **In `src/hooks/useAiAutoFill.ts`** — AI-powered form auto-complete (multi-provider) |
| `gdriveSave()` / `gdriveRestore()` | **In `useGdriveSync`** — Cloud backup/restore via Google Drive. `gdriveSave` always POSTs a new file then rotates old ones (3-backup window via `GDRIVE_MAX_MANUAL`). `gdriveRestore` fetches all available backups in parallel and shows a picker. Auto-retry on 401/403. |
| `gdriveSaveQuiet()` | **In `useGdriveSync`** — Silent background Drive save (auto-save) |
| `gatherLocalImages(dat)` | **In `useGdriveSync`** — Collect all `local-photo-*` images from IndexedDB and return `{key: base64}`. Used by all export/backup functions to resolve photo keys before writing output. |
| `doExport()` / `doImportFile()` | **In `useExportImport`** — JSON export/import. On import, `_imageData` keys are filtered: only entries whose key starts with `local-photo-` and whose value matches `/^data:image\/(jpeg|jpg|png|webp|gif);/` are written to IndexedDB. |
| `doExportCSV()` | **In `useExportImport`** — Export inventory as CSV |
| `doBackupZip()` | **In `useExportImport`** — Create ZIP archive including images |
| `handlePhotoUpload()` | **In `App.tsx`** — Upload and resize images (max 800px, JPEG 80%, max 20 MB). Used in tobacco, pipe, wishlist, and accessory forms. Exposed on ctx. |
| `resetAll()` | **In `useExportImport`** — Confirm dialog → save(INIT) → clear the photo store → `wipeAppStorage()` → reload. **It erases the CREDENTIALS too, and says so first.** It used to wipe the cellar and the photos and leave behind `dropbox-rt` (a refresh token that renews indefinitely), `gdrive-tk`, the three AI API keys and `gdrive-account-hint` — an e-mail address. Tolerable with one user; a mislabel for a public one, where this is the button someone taps before handing the phone on or before posting a support screenshot. See `appStorage.wipeAppStorage` for why a reset SWEEPS where an export ALLOWLISTS. **It RELOADS, and that is not tidiness**: language, theme, mode and font scale are read once pre-mount in `main.jsx` and the terms gate keys on a flag read at mount, so after the wipe the running app holds preferences that no longer exist on disk — the post-restore reasoning. The IndexedDB clear is AWAITED first, or the reload can outrun the transaction and leave the photos; `nav("home")` survives only as the fallback for an environment that refuses to reload, and the test that asserted it records the reversal. `confirm_reset` was rewritten in all six languages to name what goes (cellar, pipes, journal, photos, cloud connection, API keys, every preference), that it is irreversible, and — the reassuring half, and true — that backups already in the cloud are NOT deleted. |
| `restoreFromTrash(kind, id)` | **In `src/hooks/useTrashOps.ts`** (exposed on ctx by App.tsx) — Clear `deletedAt` on a single trashed row. `kind` ∈ `tobacco / pipe / wish / accessory / session / lot`. The `"lot"` branch takes an optional third arg, the lot's own tobacco id — a lot id is unique PER TOBACCO, not globally, so restoring by id alone resurrected another tobacco's identically-numbered lot. See conv. n°23 and the `useTrashOps` entry. |
| `restoreSelectionFromTrash(selection)` | **In `src/hooks/useTrashOps.ts`** — Clear `deletedAt` on every row whose `"kind:id"` string is in the Set, in ONE atomic save. The per-row loop dropped all but the last save (React batching + stale closure) — never split this into per-row calls. |
| `restoreAllFromTrash()` | **In `src/hooks/useTrashOps.ts`** — Clear `deletedAt` on every entity AND every lot in one save. No confirm dialog (restore is non-destructive). |
| `permanentlyDelete(kind, id, scopeTobId?)` | **In `src/hooks/useTrashOps.ts`** — Hard-remove a single trashed row. For `"lot"` also clears `lotId` on referencing sessions, **both scoped to `scopeTobId` when given** — a lot id is unique per tobacco only, so without it this purged another tobacco's LIVE lot of the same number and orphaned its sessions (see the `useTrashOps` entry). For `"tobacco"` / `"pipe"`, refreshes the snapshot on referencing sessions FIRST (via `refreshSnapshotsForRemoval`) so the journal continues to display the entity's last-known photo + label. **From audit #6-1:** the `"tobacco"` branch ALSO clears `lotId` on sessions whose lot lived inside the purged tobacco (its lots vanish with it), and `emptyTrash` does the same for the lots of every trashed top-level tobacco — matching the `"lot"` branch + the 30-day sweep, so a session never keeps a dangling `lotId`. |
| `emptyTrash()` | **In `src/hooks/useTrashOps.ts`** — Wipe every soft-deleted row in one go. Keeps a `window.confirm` because it sidesteps the 30-day safety net (the only confirm dialog left in the trash flow). |
| `checkUpdate()` | **In `src/hooks/useAppUpdate.ts`** — Check for a newer version of the app. **Even when no newer build is found**, the function fires `doUpdate()` after a brief 1.2 s "À jour — rafraîchissement…" toast — unconditional cache purge + reload. Refreshes `notice.json` and any other data file the SW had cached without waiting for an APP_BUILD bump. IndexedDB is preserved. |
| `doUpdate()` | **In `src/hooks/useAppUpdate.ts`** — Trigger SW update. Sends `SKIP_WAITING` (best-effort, 1s timeout), then UNREGISTERS every SW + DELETES every `caches.*` entry, then `location.reload()`. This aggressive purge is unconditional: the historical SKIP_WAITING+controllerchange dance was unreliable on iOS Safari standalone (the event sometimes never lands, and the next navigation could still be served by the old cache mid-transition). IndexedDB is preserved — same contract as the EB chunk-load recovery (`purgeCachesAndReload` in App.tsx). |
| `cancelAutoUpdate()` | **In `src/hooks/useAppUpdate.ts`** — Stops the auto-update countdown; the update pill in Settings remains available for manual trigger. |
| `Stars` (component) | Curator star rating row — `src/components/curator/primitives.tsx`. Read-only by default; pass `onChange` to make it interactive. |
| `hBars(data, opts)` | Horizontal bar chart (SVG). In `src/components/Charts.jsx`. |
| `vBars(data, opts)` | Vertical bar chart (SVG). In `src/components/Charts.jsx`. |
| `donutChart(data, opts)` | Donut/ring chart (SVG). In `src/components/Charts.jsx`. |
| `calendarHeatmap(byDay, opts)` | Smoking-calendar heatmap (SVG). In `src/components/Charts.jsx`. Uses sage/amber/oxbloodHi thresholds from `theme-curator.ts`. |
| `EB` | Error Boundary (class component). Wraps the app root to catch render errors. |

Status / rebuy badges are now inline in the relevant Curator views (no shared helper). Lot rows and tobacco cards are inlined in `CuratorInventoryDetailView` / `CuratorInventoryListView` respectively.

## Grouping UI Pattern

All list views (inventory, pipes, wishlist, accessories, journal) support a **collapsed-by-default grouping** mode:

| View | Grouped by | State vars |
|------|-----------|-----------|
| `inv` | Tobacco brand (alphabetical) | `tobGrouped`, `collapsedTobGroups` |
| `pipes` | Pipe brand (alphabetical) | `pipesGrouped`, `collapsedPipeGroups` |
| `wish` | Wishlist brand (alphabetical, sorted brand→name) | `wishGrouped`, `collapsedWishGroups` |
| `acc` | Accessory type (`ACC_TYPES` order) | `accsGrouped`, `collapsedAccGroups` |
| `journal` | Year then month | `sessGrouped`, `collapsedSessGroups` |

**Journal latest-month default (was current-month in 178):** the journal is **decoupled from the global `cave-default-grouped` setting** — `useSessionStore` inits `sessGrouped` to `true` (always grouped by default; its own ToggleBtn still flattens it) and `saveDefaultListGrouped` no longer calls `setSessGrouped` (otherwise a flat global setting made the journal flat, so the collapse default did nothing). A one-shot store effect seeds `collapsedSessGroups` from `latestSessionMonthSeed(sessions)` (utils.ts) once sessions load — expands ONLY the month of the MOST RECENT session (`m:YYYY-MM` + its `y:YYYY`), i.e. the current month when it has sessions, else the last month present (an earlier version keyed on the calendar current month, which expanded nothing when that month was empty). `Object.assign({}, seed, prev)` lets a user toggle win; the normal toggle takes over after.

**Inverted collapse logic** — `undefined` = collapsed (default), `false` = explicitly expanded:
```js
// Is group collapsed?
var isCollapsed = collapsedXxxGroups[key] !== false;  // true when key is absent (default)

// Toggle function pattern:
function toggleXxxGroup(key) {
  setCollapsedXxxGroups(function(prev) {
    var n = Object.assign({}, prev);
    if (n[key] === false) delete n[key];  // remove → back to collapsed default
    else n[key] = false;                  // set false → expanded
    return n;
  });
}
```
